### 7C implementation contract — ruled 2026-09-08 before source edits

- **[S14-D4] Runtime identity is authored and world-scoped.** A streaming-capable volume carries a positive `int32` volume id; `INDEX_NONE` preserves the existing non-streamed whole-volume behavior and zero is invalid rather than becoming an accidental shared id. A tile's durable identity is `{volume id, fixed-lattice tile coordinate}`. `CookKey`, source package, actor name, ECS handle, pointer, and load order are not part of that value identity. Within one `UWorld`, sources with the same volume id may contribute disjoint coordinates to one logical volume; a second live source claiming an owned coordinate is rejected as one atomic admission. The same source may replace the coordinate it already owns. A repeated level instance that reproduces an already-owned authored identity is rejected without mutation in 7C; 7F must explicitly rebase instance ids and coordinates before such instances are admitted.
- **[S14-D5] 7C composes one declared lattice.** The first admitted source for a world/volume id establishes the canonical field params, lattice, and exact profile-tag set; every later source must match all three. Each source transaction carries the default and every required profile, with the same coordinate set in every profile. 7C never expands a lattice, transforms positions, remaps tags, or joins distinct volume ids. Those are 7F operations. Partitioning is decided by the pure composition/manifest layer before publication; no processor retrospectively divides a published field.
- **[S14-D6] The world streaming sidecar owns source state; fields remain values.** The per-world registry owns ephemeral source handles and, for each accepted tile identity, the exact last accepted serialized default/profile tile blobs plus `Enabled` or `Disabled`. Disable removes the tile from the published candidate but retains those blobs. Re-enable restores only from the retained blobs and performs zero geometry queries. Unload removes the source's retained blobs and ownership, so it cannot be re-enabled without another load. A successful normal cook load, runtime build, repair, cost derive, or link derive refreshes the source's retained enabled/disabled blobs before the aggregate publish; this prevents a later re-enable from resurrecting stale markup, links, or repaired geometry. Engine objects and handles never enter a field, blob, or chunk id.
- **[S14-D7] Composition and publication are all-profile transactions.** A transition validates source identity, duplicate ownership, lattice, coordinate set, every blob, and every required profile into temporary state before changing the registry. The pure field layer copies the current candidate, applies explicit tile replace/remove operations, re-derives only adjacencies touching changed tiles, then re-derives dependent links and reachability over the complete candidate. `Compose_LoadedField` remains the whole-field correctness oracle. Stable sorted adjacency records flatten to the existing portal view, so A-then-B and B-then-A are byte-equivalent to the same accepted tile set after epoch-normalized comparison. Only after every profile succeeds does one write-lock swap publish the immutable default and complete variant map. Any refusal leaves retained state, published pointers, epochs, and notifications unchanged.
- **[S14-D8] Bootstrap and F1.34 behavior remain explicit.** Existing `INDEX_NONE` volumes keep today's cooked/runtime whole-volume setup. A positive-id source also enters with every built tile loaded and enabled; streaming does not silently make authored volumes empty. Every successful load, unload, disable, or enable is a geometry publication whose epoch is newer than all fields in the logical volume and whose changed bounds are the union of the old and new tile bounds across profiles. An installed corridor crossing removed or disabled tiles is tagged for repair through the existing F1.34 rebuild queue. A search pinned to the pre-transition field may not install a route containing a removed or disabled tile: at terminal publication it is checked against the current profile field and publishes one `Unbuilt` failure when the route now crosses unknown ground; unrelated in-flight searches keep the existing repath-on-publish behavior. World teardown cancels pending requests once, drops the world sidecar, and retains no field entry. The acceptance tests must prove both installed and in-flight paths, including profile variants, without a crash, ensure, stale success, duplicate callback, or silent stall.
- **[S14-D9] One logical owner resolves source composition.** Exactly one live ECS GroundNav volume owns each positive `FCk_GroundNav_VolumeId` in a world. Streamed chunk sources are data-only manifests routed to that owner; they are not additional volume entities and carry no independent markup, link, repair, or teardown ownership. Duplicate live owners for one positive id are rejected atomically. Markup and link records remain owned by the logical volume and are resolved against its currently composed field. Unloading the owner removes the whole logical volume and every retained source; unloading a chunk removes only that source's tiles. This narrows the same-id sources in [S14-D4] to data contributions under one owner and resolves the multi-entity ownership ambiguity in [S14-D6].
- **[S14-D10] 7C source and producer boundary:** a successful chunk load allocates an opaque, world-local source handle; that handle is required for replacement, disable, re-enable, and unload and becomes invalid after unload or owner/world teardown. It is lifecycle authority, not durable field identity, and never enters serialized data. Additional 7C chunks are admitted only as complete serialized all-profile manifests. A positive-id owner bootstraps one reserved source from the built tiles in its existing cooked or runtime-built whole field; later aggregate repair/derive results are partitioned back into retained blobs by current coordinate ownership. 7C does not invent runtime geometry partitioning or subscribe to level events without a chunk asset. Phase 7E authors the manifest asset and binds level/World Partition load and unload events to the 7C transaction API. Until then, 7C production acceptance drives that public API with serialized manifests directly.

### 7D implementation contract — ruled 2026-09-08 before 7D source edits

- **[S14-D11] Invoker scope is an explicit positive-id volume mode.** Existing `INDEX_NONE` legacy volumes and existing positive-id full-volume bootstrap behavior remain unchanged. A volume opts into invoker-driven scope explicitly; that mode requires a positive streaming volume id, registers a canonical all-profile fixed lattice with no built tiles, and never performs an eager whole-volume bake merely to purge most of it on the first tick.
- **[S14-D12] Selection is a pure deterministic hysteresis calculation.** A point invoker uses its finite world-space transform location plus finite radii satisfying `0 <= inner <= outer`. Its generation set contains fixed-lattice tiles whose closed XY AABB lies within the inner-radius distance; its retention set uses the outer radius. An axis-aligned box invoker is the second source: its valid inner world-space box generates intersecting tiles and a finite nonnegative outer padding retains tiles intersecting the expanded box. Across all invokers in a world, `desired = generation union (currently built intersect retention)`. Build and purge deltas are unique and sorted by canonical row-major tile index, so entity/tick iteration order cannot change the result. Invalid invokers contribute nothing and cannot mutate state.
- **[S14-D13] Targeted building extends F1.10 without weakening whole builds.** The field scheduler accepts a validated unique sorted tile-index list and advances only that list under the existing budget. The existing whole-lattice API remains a wrapper supplying every index. A targeted multi-profile result keeps the full declared lattice, contains built data only for requested coordinates, and remains private until every requested profile completes; stale target generations are discarded before publication.
- **[S14-D14] Invoker publication reuses 7C ownership and all-profile atomicity.** The logical owner retains one complete producer bundle. Successful targeted builds atomically add or replace their coordinates in the owner's reserved source; purges disable selected owned coordinates while retaining their blobs for zero-probe restoration when they re-enter the generation set. Re-enable, new build, and purge affect the default and every exact profile variant together and publish one geometry epoch/notification. Coordinates missing retained blobs build before admission. No per-entity processor publishes while invokers are still being aggregated.
- **[S14-D15] Runtime ownership and cancellation are per world.** An ECS-native point-invoker fragment may live on any transformed entity; an axis-aligned box-invoker fragment supplies the volume source. A world aggregation processor computes one target generation for every invoker-driven owner, cancels or supersedes older pending target work deterministically, and applies completed results only if their generation remains current. Removing the last invoker purges tiles outside all retention sets; owner or world teardown cancels pending work and removes registry state. Installed and in-flight path behavior continues through 7C/F1.34.

### 7E implementation contract — ruled 2026-09-08 before 7E source edits

- **[S14-D16] The selector is a canonical inclusive set of stable data-layer names.** An empty selector preserves the existing collect-all behavior. A non-empty selector includes geometry only when its source actor belongs to at least one exact selected data-layer asset/instance name; actors with no matching layer, including unlayered actors, are excluded. Empty names are invalid. Order and duplicates are normalized before comparison, hashing, collection, or persistence.
- **[S14-D17] Filtering happens at geometry collection, not after baking.** The canonical selector travels with the field/build params into every region and whole-body backend query. The production Jolt collection seam must reject excluded bodies before their triangles enter a batch, and closure/body queries use the same decision. Stub/layer-aware tests must prove the exact subset. A selector carried only as metadata is insufficient.
- **[S14-D18] Selector identity is part of the content contract.** The canonical selected-name set participates in the input/content fingerprint and cooked lookup identity. Changing only the selector makes the previous cook/build stale and forces a rebuild; permutation and duplicate spelling of the same canonical set do not.
- **[S14-D19] Existing cooked tiles become World Partition chunk records without changing field value semantics.** Every cooked tile asset carries its positive `VolumeId`, integer tile/chunk coordinate, exact world bounds, profile tag, selector identity, and content hash/fingerprint beside the serialized value blob. The index manifest carries the same volume/selector identity and exact profile set. Runtime load rejects any mismatch atomically before publication; no engine-object reference enters a field or serialized blob.
- **[S14-D20] Editor traversal is descriptor-complete and acceptance remains split.** The editor cooker may use the established `FWorldPartitionHelpers::ForEachActorWithLoading` pattern to discover unloaded authored GroundNav volumes and layer membership, deduplicating actors already loaded in the world. It emits distinct assets for distinct canonical selectors and profiles. Layer-subset, fingerprint, and asset-contract rows are local Layer-1 gates; an actual data-layer-varying World Partition level bake remains `[EDITOR-VERIFY]`, and packaged Development/Test plus Shipping compile remain build-machine evidence.

### 7F implementation contract — ruled 2026-09-08 before 7F source edits

- **[S14-D21] A transformed instance is one explicit pure value transaction.** The input is an immutable host field, an immutable source field decoded from serialized data, and a value-only instance descriptor. Success returns a new field plus its positive destination `FCk_GroundNav_VolumeId`; failure leaves the caller's output byte/value unchanged. No registry, `UObject`, source handle, world lookup, runtime allocator, or load-order-derived identity enters this layer.
- **[S14-D22] Placement uses a destination tile anchor and exact yaw degrees.** The pivot is the source lattice origin. The destination anchor names the lower-left tile of the rotated source rectangle in the host's already-declared lattice. Yaw must be a finite integer multiple of 90 degrees and is normalized to 0/90/180/270; any other value is diagnosed with `CK_ENSURE_IF_NOT` and rejected by a separate ordinary branch. The API has no scale, pitch, roll, fractional tile offset, or Z offset. Quarter turns map source tile/cell coordinates by the canonical rectangular rotations; 90/270 swap the source division extents.
- **[S14-D23] Admission preserves the host lattice and rejects overlap.** Source and host must have compatible cell/tile sizes, profile, bake config, merge tunables, vertical slab, and clearance cap. Every mapped source coordinate must lie inside the host lattice and land only on an unbuilt host tile. The transform rotates each built tile's layer-major surface/clearance arrays, plate rectangles and cell map, portal coordinates/directions, seam stubs, and boundary world segments; it never expands the host or copies field-global derived indices.
- **[S14-D24] Authored identity and tags are explicit and total.** Source markup and link IDs are rebased by separate authored `int32` offsets using checked wide arithmetic; overflow, invalid results, or collision with any host or same-source mapped ID rejects atomically. Every valid tag appearing in source markup area tags, link area/user tags, or tile plate policy containers must have an explicit valid destination entry, including identity mappings. Empty tags remain empty. Markup world transforms and link endpoints receive the same rigid XY placement before they join the host-owned params.
- **[S14-D25] Derived topology is rebuilt from the complete candidate.** The merge discards source field-global seam portals/adjacencies, tile-edge boundary, plate offsets, resolved links, and reachability labels. After all mapped tiles and authored records are accepted, it re-derives seam portals and edge boundary for the complete candidate, resolves links, and labels reachability. Layer-1 acceptance compares an aligned translated/quarter-turned source merged into an empty host lattice against a direct bake of the transformed geometry, excluding only epoch/cook-time noise; invalid rotation, missing tag map, ID collision/overflow, lattice mismatch, out-of-bounds placement, and tile overlap each leave a sentinel output unchanged.
- **[S14-D26] A runtime source partition is authored by World Partition cell, logical volume, and canonical selector.** Its durable identity is `{positive VolumeId, positive PartitionId}`; tile identity remains `{VolumeId, TileCoord}`. The manifest owns a sorted, disjoint coordinate set and the complete default-plus-exact-variants payload for every coordinate. Partition identity never enters a field or tile blob and load order never creates identity.
- **[S14-D27] Generated source manifests belong to the cooked volume catalog.** A manifest is a generated data asset referenced from the volume's cooked index/catalog, with lattice, fingerprint, selector, profile-set, coordinate, bounds, hash, and soft tile identity sufficient for complete validation before any source handle is allocated or publication changes. Legacy and whole-volume cooked indexes remain compatible.
- **[S14-D28] Runtime availability distinguishes disable from unload.** Effective data-layer deactivation while a cell remains resident disables its source and retains validated blobs for zero-probe re-enable. Runtime-cell unload removes the source and releases its retained blobs and handle. Late or stale lifecycle events are generation-checked and cannot resurrect a replaced or unloaded partition.
- **[S14-D29] Repeated instances require explicit identity rebasing.** A second active `{VolumeId, PartitionId}` in one world is rejected atomically. Intentional repeated instances must author distinct positive identities or use the 7F rebasing contract; neither runtime order nor package duplication may infer a replacement identity. A manifest-backed positive-id owner registers the declared empty lattice without creating an all-coordinate bootstrap source, so cell sources never overlap an owner reservation.
- **[S14-D30] The production stream bridge is explicit and owner-fenced.** A cell-resident `ACk_GroundNav_StreamManifestBinding_UE` authors positive `{VolumeId, PartitionId}`, the exact canonical selector, and a strictly row-major coordinate set. Runtime derives the generated manifest path from the registered owner's `{source-level package, CookKey, selector}` plus `PartitionId`; a non-null authored soft reference must equal that path. The binding coordinates must equal the manifest coordinates before any blob is decoded or source is published.
- **[S14-D31] A multi-layer streamed source is enabled only when all selected layers are effectively Activated.** The empty selector is active. A resident source transitioning inactive retains its validated blobs; returning active calls `Reactivate` without asset reload or geometry probing. Level removal calls `Unload`; failed unloads retain a retry tombstone. Every event carries the captured owner lifetime and a monotonic per-partition generation.

## Decisions

- **[NN-D53]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **Reachability verdicts and their neutral
  mapping.** GroundNav answers `PossiblyReachable` (same label — connectivity for a body of no
  size; clearance is not in the label, F1.9's limitation restated in the contract), `Unreachable`
  (different labels, both components closed) or `Unknown_OpenComponent` (different labels, either
  component borders unbaked ground). The facade maps PossiblyReachable → Reachable, Unreachable →
  Unreachable, NoSurface at either end → Unreachable (Recast parity: a point that does not project
  is unreachable there too), and Unbuilt / Blocked / Unknown_OpenComponent →
  Unknown_ProviderNotReady. `_ExpansionCount` is carried on the result and asserted zero.
- **[NN-D52]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **How an off-thread facade call reaches a
  GroundNav snapshot.** A per-world published-field registry in CkGroundNav (`Facade/
  CkGroundNav_WorldFieldRegistry`): the volume's build processor publishes `(world, volume, FieldPtr)`
  on the game thread; a reader copies the shared pointer out under an `FRWLock` READ lock and
  releases it before querying. The lock guards the pointer handoff only — never a query — which
  keeps [NN-D49] intact and the "no locks in the query path" fence honest. Entries drop on
  `OnWorldCleanup`. Per-world-keyed, as A2 allows.
- **[NN-D51]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **The facade dispatches through a provider
  table, and `ECk_NavSurface_Provider` gains `GroundNav` NOW.** PHASE_0 said "entries are added
  when the provider exists, never in advance" and deferred the entry to PHASE_3; PHASE_2 §7 step 4
  requires every CkGroundNav query registered behind the facade with equivalence tests, which
  needs an identity. The provider exists and answers queries, so the entry is added here, not
  speculatively; PHASE_3 adds the path branch. Mechanism: `NavSurface/CkNavSurface_ProviderTable`
  — a code registry of per-capability `TFunction`s keyed by provider, filled at module startup
  (CkNavigation registers Recast's own table; CkGroundNav registers its adapter from
  `StartupModule`), so the facade has ONE dispatch path; per-world selection stays on the
  transient entity's `FFragment_NavSurface_Provider`, seeded from a new project setting
  `_DefaultNavSurfaceProvider` (Recast) and overridable by `Request_SetProvider`. Markup requests
  stay on Recast until the markup phase. Default behaviour is unchanged: Recast answers unless a
  world opts in.
- **[NN-D50]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7 — Gate 2B-1 STOP, ruled) **A point exactly on a
  cell line belongs to the cells on both sides of it; every position lookup resolves over CLOSED
  squares, with no epsilon.** Gate 2B-1 (`Saved/Logs/P2-2B-Gate1.log`, 119 run, 117 passed) reported
  896 "escapes" at radius 0 and 514 at radius 30 out of 10,000 moves, plus 9 verdict disagreements and
  11 off-segment hits in the raycast symmetry test. Verbatim first escapes: radius 0 — start
  (800.0000, 1371.4840, 300.0000), target (1306.7761, 951.8463, 300.0000), result NoSurface (never
  walked); radius 30 — start (675.0000, 805.7533, 0.0000), target (904.1884, 1235.3495, 0.0000),
  ended (700.0000, 825.0000, 0.0000) Success in tile 2 layer 0 cell (27, 0) plate 0. In both the
  start's X is an exact cell line (800 = 32 × 25; 675 = 27 × 25): projection clamps its answer into
  the nearest CLOSED cell square, which can be the square's edge, and `Get_IsNavigable` floored that
  point into the NEIGHBOURING cell — the wall top at z 300 beside the floor it was projected onto
  (hence NoSurface within the start tolerance), and the 25 uu-clearance cell beside the one that
  admitted the 30 uu body (hence a walk that began, and stayed, in a cell the caller never chose).
  The walk itself left no cell it was given: the DDA's containment held. Fix: `Get_CellAddressesAt`
  returns every cell whose closed square holds the point (floor cell first; the cell before it on
  each axis where the coordinate equals the cell line, by exact equality because both come from the
  same arithmetic), and is-navigable — hence attributes-at and every walk/raycast start — picks the
  admitted candidate with the smallest vertical distance. No clamp, no epsilon, no re-projection: the
  closed-square rule that projection already uses is now used by the lookup too.
  **Gate 2B-2 (`Saved/Logs/P2-2B-Gate2.log`): radius 0 → 0 escapes (from 896); off-segment hits 11 → 0;
  raycast verdict disagreements 9 → 2; radius 30 still 506 with the SAME first escape** — the walk
  resolved its start with radius 0 and the tie between the two on-line cells went to the floor cell
  (the 25 uu one), so the body started, and stayed, in a cell the projection had refused it. Amendment:
  a TRAVERSAL start (walk, raycast) resolves over the closed-square candidates preferring a cell the
  body is ADMITTED on (an unadmitted cell remains acceptable only when nothing else qualifies — the
  tight-spot rule), then the smallest vertical distance, then the cell on the side the motion ENTERS
  (a start on a line never begins by stepping back across that line — which is also the raycast
  symmetry: the two remaining disagreements are A→B "reached" at the line without a step versus B→A
  starting on the far side and being refused at t = 0). **Gate 2B-3 (`Saved/Logs/P2-2B-Gate3.log`,
  119 run, 118 passed): radius 0 → 0 escapes, radius 30 → 0 escapes over 10,000 moves each — GATE
  2B-1's HARD ZERO IS MET** (radius 30: 8,511 moves run, 2,783 reached their target, 7,020 slides, 0
  plate early-outs; radius 0: 8,671 run, 2,960 reached, 6,934 slides, 1,801 early-outs). The raycast
  symmetry test's 2 disagreements survived the start refinement, so they are a third mechanism: the
  traversal declared the endpoint reached from the derived parametric distance of the next boundary
  (`t >= 1`), and for an endpoint exactly on a cell line the two directions round that distance to
  opposite sides of 1.0 — one steps across the line at its very end (and is refused), the other does
  not. Amendment two: the endpoint is reached when the TARGET POINT lies inside the current cell's
  closed square, decided on its own coordinates with exact comparisons; the parametric distance
  only orders boundaries. **Gate 2B-4 (`Saved/Logs/P2-2B-Gate4.log`): Total 119 · Passed 119 ·
  Failed 0, zero ensures; 0 escapes at radius 0 and 30 over 10,000 moves each; 200 raycast pairs with
  0 verdict disagreements and 0 off-segment hits — 2B EXIT on this artifact.** Owed beyond 2B: the
  Recast-agreement pass (PHASE_2 §5 step 3) once the shared fixtures exist ([P2-B1]).

**A4 BUDGETS MEASURED on the Recast path (2026-09-02, `Saved/Logs/P2-RecastBudgets.log`,
`CkAutoTest_NavSurface_RecastBudgets_OriginFloor` green, [NN-D48]).** Fixture: the level floor
`StaticMeshActor_1` (±1500 uu, top at z 0) clipped by `NavMeshBoundsVolume_1` (±1000 uu) — the rim is
the volume edge. Recast (agent radius 35, its default cell size):

| Measurement | Recast vs analytic | Budget = deviation + one 25 uu cell |
|---|---|---|
| projection height error, 25 points inside the rim | 10.0 uu max, XY drift 0.0 | vertical 35 uu, horizontal 25 uu |
| raycast-to-rim stop distance, 8 directions | 12.0 uu short (16.97 along diagonals = the same 12 measured obliquely) | hit point within 37 uu |
| walk-to-rim end distance, 8 directions | 12.0 uu short, every end re-projects onto the surface | endpoint within 37 uu |

**2C ENTERED (2026-09-02, session 7).** Design of record in the session scratchpad `2C/DESIGN_2C.md`
(summarised here so it survives): boundary runs precomputed AT BAKE per tile from the published plates
and portals — every plate edge cell no portal covers, merged into runs, winding fixed (interior on the
LEFT walking Start→End), an 8×8-cell bucket index per tile (`Bake/CkGroundNav_Boundary.{h,cpp}`,
`FCk_GroundNav_Tile::_Boundary`); runs on a tile's RIM are kept as candidates and resolved at
composition against the seam portals into `FCk_GroundNav_Field::_TileEdgeBoundary` (an unbuilt
neighbour makes the whole rim a boundary — fail closed). Written by the orchestrator (bake change,
like [NN-D46]/[NN-D47]); Gate 2C-1 launched to prove the bake still bakes and to see the probe pin
move by the edge-cell visits. Query F1.17/F1.18 headers drafted; units H (query .cpp), I (tests incl.
the Gate 2C-3 epoch-consistency stress test), J (draw mode 6 + `ck.GroundNav.EdgesAt`) follow.
**Gate 2C-1 (`Saved/Logs/P2-2C-Gate1.log`): 119 run, 118 passed; the one red is the reference pin
moving exactly by the boundary derivation — probes 1078460 → 1080284 (+1824 edge-cell visits),
walkable 9792 / plates 22 / portals 6 / seams 17 / components 5 unchanged, field 191645 → 217669 bytes
(the per-tile segment arrays, rim candidates and bucket index), zero ensures; the 100-bake determinism
and the byte-identity pins stayed green. Re-pinned with the delta explained.** One compile error en
route, mine: `auto RunStart = INDEX_NONE` (the recorded enum-deduction trap), fixed to `int32`. The
F1.17/F1.18 query contract is in the tree (`Query/CkGroundNav_Query_Boundary.h`, boundary types in
`QueryTypes.h`); units H, I, J dispatched.
**Units H, I, J returned and reviewed (no changes required).** H: `Query/CkGroundNav_Query_Boundary.cpp`
(484 lines) — AABB clamped into the field's cell range before the tile division so an off-field circle
is empty rather than truncating onto tile zero; per-tile `TBitArray` visited masks; rim runs read once
per tile; ring walk over global bucket coordinates terminating on `best <= ring × bucketSpan`; a
corrupt lattice (zero cell size) ensures and answers NoSurface rather than spinning. I: 11 tests
(5 bake, 5 query, 1 stress). Fixture facts the unit found and I confirmed: the dividing wall ends ON
the east seam (x = 800), so tile (0,0)'s east rim is a 300 uu drop with no seam portal at all — the
seam-bearing rim is the NORTH one, and both the seam-coverage and unbuilt-neighbour tests use it;
`Request_Publish` stamps the field's own epoch rather than incrementing, so the stress test gives the
two worlds epochs 1 and 2 and asserts the publisher reports the published field's epoch on every one
of 1000 publishes; every random point is projected onto a surface before the closest-boundary
comparison so the brute-force reference resolves the same plate. J: draw mode 6 (per-layer runs, rim
runs orange, 20 uu inward ticks), `ck.GroundNav.EdgesAt X Y Z R`, snapshot `_Boundary` + summary
line. **Gate 2C-2** (`Saved/Logs/P2-2C-Gate2.log`): build failed on one line — `auto DifferingBake =
INDEX_NONE` in the bake test (the recorded enum-deduction trap, third occurrence this phase; fixed to
`int32`). **Gate 2C-3** (`Saved/Logs/P2-2C-Gate3.log`): 130 run / 129 passed / 1 failed, zero
ensures. Green on first run: winding over all 50 runs clean; 20-bake determinism; rim coverage (east
rim 32 cells all by runs / 0 by seams — the wall ends on that seam; north rim 32 all by seams / 0 by
runs); unbuilt neighbour walls the rim and restore reproduces the runs exactly; room walls 2 runs both
wall-facing, in order; storeys separated (ground 1 run, deck 3, none from the other); closest edge =
brute force at 8671/8671 projected points (worst delta 1.5e-5, closest point on its run every time);
**stress: 1000 publishes × 8 workers, 24000 queries, 0 mismatches, epoch agreement 1000/1000, 999
changes** — [NN-D49] pinned; reference probes 1080284 as re-pinned. The one red,
`Boundary_NoRunLiesOnAPortal` (96 crossing cell pairs, 192 covered), was a real defect in MY bake
patch, not the test: the crop pass called `DoDerive_Boundary` with `OutTile._Portals` BEFORE the
portals were copied into the tile (`OutTile._Portals = Portals` came 20 lines later), so the
derivation saw an empty portal table and walled every doorway. The determinism, winding and rim
tests could not see it — only the portal-coverage test could, which is why it exists. Fixed by moving
the derivation after the portal copy + halo rebase. **Gate 2C-4 (`Saved/Logs/P2-2C-Gate4.log`):
130 run / 130 passed / 0 failed, zero ensures.** Winding-checked runs 50 → 42 (the eight doorway
runs are gone), field 217669 → 217429 bytes, probes 1080284 unchanged (the per-side-cell visits do
not depend on coverage), every other report identical to 2C-3. Crowd pattern (the parallel
avoidance consumer, untouched by 2C) run test-only on the same binaries as `Saved/Logs/P2-2C-Crowd.log`.
**Crowd gate (`Saved/Logs/P2-2C-Crowd.log`): 107 run / 104 passed / 3 failed, zero ensures.** Against
the phase-entry baseline by NAME: `Crowd_Grounding_OffMeshWalkerHolds` and
`Queue_CrowdAdapterMovesAndResumes` are baseline reds; `Queue_CrowdAdapterFacesOrigin` is red here

StationaryAgentReGrounds flips run to run. Not attributable to this batch: 2C touches only
`Source/CkGroundNav`, which is not registered behind the facade until 2D step 4, so the Recast path

### 2D — reachability, flood fill with the shared funnel, point generation, facade registration (2026-09-02, session 7)

**Batch 4 (dispatched):** F1.19 + facade dispatch + debug draw. **Batch 5 (next):** F1.21 point
generators + full-suite delta-zero. Design: session scratchpad `2D/DESIGN_2D.md`; contracts in the
tree: `Query/CkGroundNav_Funnel.h` (THE one funnel — `Get_StringPull`, `Get_StringPull_ToSegment`
with radius inset and a wedge-visible target point), `Query/CkGroundNav_Query_Reachability.h`
(flat plate addressing over `_TilePlateOffsets`, `Get_CrossingsFrom` oriented away from the plate
over tile portals + seam portals, `Get_IsReachable`, `Get_FloodFill` with a `TFunctionRef` stop
predicate, `Get_FloodDistanceTo/DistancesTo`), and the 2D types at the end of `QueryTypes.h`
(`ECk_GroundNav_Reachability {PossiblyReachable, Unreachable, Unknown_OpenComponent}`,
`FCk_GroundNav_Crossing`, `FCk_GroundNav_Flood{Query,Crossing,Result}`). Flood fill = Dijkstra over
crossings where a candidate's cost is the funnel from the source through its predecessor chain to
the nearest wedge-visible point of its interval (consistent, so exact for the portal sequence);
plate distance to a point = min over the plate's settled entry crossings of the funnel to the
point; source plate = straight line (a plate is convex). Units: K funnel `.cpp`; L reachability
`.cpp`; M tests (funnel geometry, two-island expansion-zero, flood vs a visibility-graph reference
on the ground storey over 100 targets within one cell, early-exit bounds, one-to-many, radius
refusing a 60 uu doorway — the F1.9 limitation pinned); N facade dispatch + adapter + equivalence
test; O `ck.GroundNav.ReachAt` / `FloodAt`.
**Orientation correction caught in review (before any gate):** my brief told units L and M that a
body travelling +X has the +Y end on its LEFT; unit K implemented the funnel in Unreal's frame, where
a body facing +X has +Y on its RIGHT, which is the header's plain meaning. Had both stood, every
"tighten" in the funnel would have widened. Corrected the crossing builder (`Make_Crossing`: left =
the end with a NEGATIVE cross product against the outward direction) and messaged the test unit
mid-flight with the corrected rule; K and O reviewed clean, L reviewed clean apart from this.
**Unit N reviewed — one defect fixed by me:** its `Get_ProviderForWorld` resolved a world's provider
by reading the transient entity's fragment out of the ECS registry, on whatever thread called the
facade — and `Get_BoundarySegments` is off-thread by contract, so the parallel avoidance consumer
would have read a registry pool while the game thread wrote it. Replaced with a per-world mirror
(`TMap<TWeakObjectPtr<UWorld>, ECk_NavSurface_Provider>` under an `FRWLock`, written on the game
thread by the two sites that write the fragment — the RevisionWatch seed and `Request_SetProvider` —
read under a read lock for the enum handoff only, dropped on `OnWorldCleanup`). The fragment stays
the game-thread record. [NN-D51] amended accordingly; the same handoff-only pattern as [NN-D52].
Everything else in N stands: `GroundNav` enum entry, provider table with Recast registered at
CkNavigation startup, `_DefaultNavSurfaceProvider` setting, `Request_SetProvider`/`Get_Provider`,
adapter mappings, world field registry published from the volume's build processor, equivalence
test on a `CreateWorld` world (which N verified DOES carry a transient entity). Unit M reviewed:
its rejection of my (420,120) inset-corner expectation is correct (the inset moves along the
interval, so the corner cannot be a diagonal offset); the doorway test's 60 uu gap is flagged as
possibly resolving to 3 free cells — watch that test. **Gate 2D-1 (`Saved/Logs/P2-2D-Gate1.log`):
built first time across all five units; 146 run / 145 passed / 1 failed, zero ensures.** The
numbers that matter: **flood-fill distances equal the visibility-graph reference on the ground
storey at 100/100 targets, worst delta 0.000000** (13 expansions, 9 settled crossings; the reference
graph had 15 nodes over 14 runs); **facade equivalence 0 mismatches over 1000 points** for projection,
raycast, boundary and reachability; two-island reachability Unreachable with expansions 0; the open
component Unknown with expansions 0; the 60 uu doorway admits radius 0 and refuses radius 40 (the
F1.9 limitation pinned, and the doorway did resolve to two free cells); all seven funnel tests
green including the L corridor at exactly three waypoints; the earlier suites unchanged (10k walks
0 escapes, 24000 stress queries 0 mismatches, closest edge 8671/8671). The one red is a contract
inconsistency of mine, not a unit defect: `_MaxExpansions = 3` reported `_ExpansionCount = 4`
because the cap was tested after the pop was counted — my header said the count "includes the ones
the early exit refused", and my test brief said the cap bounds it. The cap must bound it; fixed by
testing the cap before counting and amending the header sentence. **Gate 2D-2
(`Saved/Logs/P2-2D-Gate2.log`): 146 run / 146 passed / 0 failed, zero ensures** — capped expansions
3 / settled 3, everything else identical to 2D-1. Module docs updated in the tree: the CkGroundNav
`CLAUDE.md` debug table now lists every query command and draw modes 5 and 6; the CkNavigation
`Claude.md` gains a "NavSurface provider dispatch" section. Consumer gates on the same binaries,
test-only: `Saved/Logs/P2-2D-Nav.log` (pattern `Nav`: the NavSurface autotests, the deferred-queue
tests, the thread contract, GroundNav again) then `Saved/Logs/P2-2D-Crowd.log`.

- **[NN-D57]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 8) **3A graph shape, ruled from the header-verified design
  (`scratchpad/design_3A_PlatePortalGraph.md`).** (1) The A* node is a CROSSING ARRIVAL (a canonical
  crossing id into a per-search pool, source node 0), not a flat plate id: two doors between the same
  rooms are two portals with two clearances (`Portals.h:20-22`), a plate node would make `Cost` re-derive
  which door and the funnel could pick another — a second definition of the route; the flood fill
  already uses this node. Tradeoff accepted: expansions ≈ admitted crossings, not plates. (2) The
  shared per-query seed holds the field as `FCk_GroundNav_FieldPtr` (a sliced search outlives a
  rebuild; the epoch is stamped) — unlike the single-tick raw-pointer precedents. (3) Cost = Euclidean
  between transition points × area multiplier × (1 + k_slope·rise/run) × (1 + k_wall/(clearance_cells+1)),
  wired in 3A with k_slope = k_wall = 0 so the optimal-length test compares against a pure Euclidean
  optimum; 3B sets the measured constants. The area multiplier is a private helper returning 1.0 until
  markup exists (PHASE_4) — no `TFunction` hook, no `CompiledFilter` type invented. (4) The transition
  point is the midpoint of the radius-inset interval (collapse to the raw midpoint below 2R) — the same
  rule `Get_StringPull` documents, so pricing and funnelling agree; it never runs a funnel. (5) CkAStar
  has NO node cap and NO best-node tracking: the driver owns the expansion cap and corridor-length cap
  (checked after `ContinueSearch` returns, so a slice boundary cannot change a verdict) and the graph
  memoises (bestH, bestNode) inside `Heuristic` for 3B's partials. (6) `CostThreshold` stays 0. (7) No
  seam-portal index in 3A; `Get_CrossingsFrom` scans `_SeamPortals` linearly per expansion — recorded as
  a perf candidate to decide on the measured expansion counts. (8) Durations are `FCk_Time`, converted
  at the CkAStar boundary. Statuses are module-local `{InProgress, Ready, Partial, Unbuilt,
  NoStartSurface, NoGoalSurface, Unreachable, BudgetExceeded, Blocked}`; 3C maps onto
  `ECk_Nav_PathStatus` / `ECk_Nav_PathFailReason`.
- **[NN-D58]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 8) **3B shape, ruled from the header-verified design
  (`scratchpad/design_3B_FunnelPartialsCostPostProcess.md`).** (1) Cost constants and the per-plate
  multiplier table live ON THE QUERY (`FCk_GroundNav_PathCostParams{_SlopePenaltyK 0, _ClearanceBiasK 0,
  _CornerOffsetK 1.0, _PlateCostMultipliers}`), copied into the shared seed — no settings object (none
  exists in the module; a global would make the hermetic cost tests mutate shared state) and no
  `TFunction` filter (the query's table IS the per-query rewrite; PHASE_4's painted regions merge
  max-wins into a field-side table through the same pure `Get_MaxMerged`). (2) `Cost()` stays
  goal-blind (the goal node's heuristic is exact, optimality already holds); the per-leg formula is
  lifted into `Get_LegCost` and `_SearchCost` gains the final leg to the goal point; the same-plate
  early-out prices its straight leg instead of reporting 0. (3) The best-node memo moves from
  `Heuristic` (pushed) into `IsGoal` (expanded) so the partial endpoint is on the closed set; best ==
  source ⇒ Unreachable with no waypoints, never Partial; corridor-length cap ⇒ BudgetExceeded even with
  partials on. (4) Post-processing is a separate `FCk_GroundNav_PathPlan` computed once after a terminal
  status (never fused into the per-slice result): funnel → corner offset along the interior bisector,
  accepted only if navigable AND ≥ R from the boundary, halving up to four times, else the un-offset
  point → the skip-first pass mirrored verbatim from `CkNav_Algorithm.cpp:193-207` → per-waypoint fill
  (XY integrated distance = the funnel's own return; cost from `Get_LegCost`, straddling segments at the
  max of both plates' multipliers). (5) `k_corner` ships at the spec'd 1.0 and the test pins the analytic
  `R + k·R/√2`; parity (Recast raw 1.55 R, shipped offset 0) is a later reference-number decision.
  (6) `k_wall` keys off the crossing's clearance so it chooses a DOOR, not a line inside a plate — the
  "toward the centre line" claim is tested as same plate corridor / different crossing / strictly larger
  minimum boundary distance. (7) Exclusion (`_ExcludedAreaTags`) is a PHASE_4 admission predicate in
  `Neighbors`, never an infinite cost. (8) `Bake_RampScene` has no level alternative; 3B adds
  `Bake_LCorridorScene`, `Bake_TwoRouteScene`, `Bake_RampVsLevelScene` fixtures.
- **[NN-D59]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 8) **3C shape, ruled from the header-verified design
  (`scratchpad/design_3C_InstallProviderBranch.md`).** (1) The GroundNav dispatch mirrors CkVoxelNav's:
  a CkGroundNav-owned `Path/` quartet (request/result fragments on the AGENT, `FProcessor_GroundNavPath_HandleRequests`
  slicing `FCk_GroundNav_PathSearch` under CkGroundNav's OWN per-frame budget — `_MaxSearchesPerFrame` +
  an `FCk_Time` slice — then `Get_PathPlan`), and a crowd-side `FProcessor_CrowdAgent_OnGroundNavPathResolved`
  that installs through `InstallExternalPath` / `FailPath` only. CkCrowd gains a `CkGroundNav` dependency
  (T4→T4, the CkVoxelNav/CkPathNetwork precedent). (2) The branch is selected by
  `Get_ProviderForWorld(World) == GroundNav` ALONE and sits immediately before the Recast fallthrough;
  there is NO silent fallthrough to Recast when no field is near the agent — the search answers
  `Unbuilt`, which DEFERS (re-park, restamp `_PendingSinceSeconds`, `ck.GroundNav.MaxDeferralSeconds`
  default 5.0) and force-fails `NoNavData` (a hidden fallback would mask a missing bake; the default
  provider is unchanged). (3) The TOTAL status → verdict table lives in CkCrowd
  (`CkCrowdAgent_GroundNavInstall_Algorithm.h`), a constexpr table with a count `static_assert` and a
  consteval identity check so an appended OR inserted status fails to compile: Ready/Partial → Install,
  Unbuilt/InProgress → Defer, NoStartSurface → StartProjectFailed, NoGoalSurface → EndProjectFailed,
  Unreachable → FindPathNoPath (the load-bearing one for the strict→permissive→OnGoalFailed sequence),
  BudgetExceeded → BudgetExceeded, Blocked → FindPathInvalid (an `AgentTooLarge` reason is a cheap
  follow-up, not 3C). (4) `InstallExternalPath` gains a defaulted trailing `InInstallAs` so a GroundNav
  `Partial` installs as `Partial` and gets the crowd's existing partial behaviour — still one install
  path (CkNavigation touched ⇒ `Nav` consumer gate). (5) Plan repair: node ids are per-search pool ids,
  so a stored path is re-canonicalised from `FCk_GroundNav_CrossingKey`s on the current field through a
  new non-minting `TryGet_NodeForKey`, validated with `astar::ValidateExistingPath`, and warm-started
  through a new `Request_BeginRepair`; same epoch ⇒ valid with zero expansions; k == 0 or a moved
  source plate ⇒ cold replan. (6) FINDING filed, not fixed: `Get_MaxPathQueriesPerFrame()` bounds
  nothing in CkNavigation (`_BudgetRemainingThisTick` is assigned at `CkNav_Processor.cpp:175` and read
  nowhere) — explains the A5 throughput result. (7) The `NoRouteFailsClean` /
  `Stall_UnreachableGoalFailsBounded` GroundNav twins block with Jolt geometry + a rebake, not
  `Request_ImpassableBox` (markup is PHASE_4); the originals are never edited (delta-zero). (8) The
  Layer-2 walk autotest uses a fresh band (Y 122000), the facade test's slab + volume + `Request_Build`
  idiom, capture/switch/restore of the provider in `DoEndPlay`, and asserts on `OnPathReady` /
  `OnGoalReached` signals.
- **[NN-D60]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 8) **3D shape, ruled from the header-verified design
  (`scratchpad/design_3D_ShadowMode.md`).** (1) The shadow setting is `ECk_NavSurface_ShadowMode {Off,
  GroundNavShadowsRecast}` beside the provider: a field on the world's provider fragment, a per-world
  FRWLock mirror copied from the provider's (and the world-cleanup lambda extended to purge BOTH maps),
  project default Off, `Request_SetShadowMode` / `Get_ShadowMode` on `UCk_Utils_NavSurface_UE`. (2)
  Shadow dispatch is appended AFTER the Recast `Request_FindPath` inside `Request_NavigationPath`, so
  every internal re-dispatch (the permissive leg included) shadows for free; `_ActiveProvider` stays
  `Navigation`; the GroundNav request and result carry `_IsShadow`; the result gains `_SearchDurationMs`
  from the slice loop's existing measurement (no new wall-clock reads). (3) Gate 3D-1 is one early
  return: `OnGroundNavPathResolved` ignores a shadow-flagged result BEFORE its movement-tag gate;
  `DoReleaseProviderQuery`'s `Navigation` case abandons a shadow episode. (4) The compare processor
  lives in CkCrowd (`Shadow/CkCrowd_ShadowCompare_Processor`), because every `TProcessor` header names a
  handle type and the values-only grep over `CkGroundNav/.../Shadow/` must return zero hits; the
  diagnostics fragment (on the WORLD transient entity, the `FFragment_Nav_DeferredRequests` precedent),
  accumulator, pure report builder, `UCk_Utils_GroundNav_Shadow_UE` and `ck.GroundNav.ShadowReport` live
  in CkGroundNav `Shadow/`. (5) Fixture key = explicit `Request_BeginShadowFixture(FName)` /
  `Request_EndShadowFixture` (the PIE world is shared, so map name is not diff-stable; agent names carry
  ids); fallback = map name. (6) p95 = fixed-bucket histogram with interpolation, column `p95~`. (7)
  `_ContainmentEscapes` exists now with its producer owed to the containment check owner. (8) Values-only
  proof = `static_assert(std::is_trivially_copyable_v<>)` on the leaf structs + the grep gate + a
  Layer-1 test that copies the fragment, destroys the original and reports from the copy. (9) No
  `ck.GroundNav.ShadowAt` (the facade has no path capability; drawing a Recast route from CkGroundNav
  would reintroduce the F1.27 dependency); the overlay is a separate crowd `DrawShadowRoutes` processor
  + one label line in `DrawNavStatus`, `[EDITOR-VERIFY]`. (10) FINDING to fix in the tests unit:
  `CkGroundNavPath_Processor.cpp` has four `FPlatformTime::Seconds()` reads and is absent from the
  snapshot wall-time allow-list meta-test — a latent full-suite red.
- **[NN-D49]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **The thread contract, made concrete.** F1.19
  is met by construction, not by a lock: the field is an immutable value behind
  `TSharedPtr<const FCk_GroundNav_Field>`, a publish swaps the pointer and bumps the epoch, and every
  query (projection, is-navigable, attributes, status, walk, raycast, boundary, closest boundary)
  reads only the snapshot the caller holds and writes only the caller's own storage — no statics, no
  shared scratch, no lazily built index. Consequences: (a) capturing a snapshot (`Get_Published()`)
  is a game-thread act; the game thread hands the pointer to workers and the workers never touch the
  publisher; (b) a worker's answers are self-consistent by construction because its snapshot cannot
  change beneath it — the stress test pins this by alternating two fields that differ by one wall
  over 1000 publishes with 8 workers checking every answer against the variant their snapshot names,
  and zero mismatches means zero torn reads; (c) the debug draw's retained `LastDebugField` is a
  game-thread copy of the same pointer and obeys the same rule. The boundary index is baked with the
  tile (buckets per tile) and the rim runs derived with the seam portals, so a boundary query never
  builds anything at query time — which is what rule (a)'s "no lazily built index" protects.
- **[NN-D48]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **What an A4 "agreement budget" is, and how it
  is measured.** VALIDATION says every budget is "measured on the Recast path on the same fixture".
  Read as: measure how far Recast's own answers sit from the fixture's ANALYTIC geometry — the
  erosion a raycast stops short of a rim by, the distance a walk toward a rim ends from it, the
  height a projection sits from the floor top — because |GroundNav − Recast| cannot be held tighter
  than |Recast − truth| + GroundNav's own cell quantization. Budget = the measured Recast deviation
  plus one cell size (25 uu at the fixtures' resolution), per capability, recorded with the log it
  came from. First fixture: the level's own floor (`StaticMeshActor_1`), read-only, no spawn, via
  `CkAutoTest_NavSurface_RecastBudgets_OriginFloor.as` (drafted in the session scratchpad; enters
  `CkTests/Script/CkNavigation/` when the editor is closed). Interior-wall budgets follow on the
  second NavMeshBoundsVolume's band. The GroundNav half of each agreement TEST needs GroundNav's
  queries reachable from AngelScript, which is 2D step 4 — the budgets do not.
- **[NN-D47]** (maintainer "yes to fixing the clearance transform now", 2026-09-02, session 7;
  design by the orchestrator) **Clearance relaxes only across LINKED neighbours.** Resolves [P2-R1].
  `DoCompute_Clearance` gains the connection field; per layer it maps each column to the span it
  contributes, and a neighbour contributes its own distance only where that span names the
  neighbour's span in the connection field (diagonals through either two-step path around the
  corner); an unlinked walkable neighbour contributes as a blocked cell — the boundary between them is
  the obstacle, one step away. Exactness argument recorded in the header: every cell's nearest
  obstacle (hole or unlinked edge) is reached along a monotone path of linked steps, because a path
  crossing an unlinked edge has that edge nearer than anything beyond it. Billing: one probe per
  connection consulted, so the reference probe pin moves and is re-pinned with the delta explained
  at the first gate; walkable cells, plates, portals, seams, components and the byte footprint are
  unchanged by construction (the transform writes the same array). Known limit kept and documented:
  a cross-layer crossing reads a false pinch on the layer it leaves. Three new fixtures pin it
  (raised solid beside a floor; curb under the step height; step over it). The reference scene's
  "so clearance varies away from every wall" becomes true.
- **[NN-D46]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **A plate carries its minimum clearance, derived
  in the tile bake's unbilled crop loop.** PHASE_2 §5 step 1 admits a move "staying inside one plate's
  rectangle and above its clearance" without stepping cells, which assumes a per-plate clearance the
  shipped tile does not store (clearance is per cell; the plate has residual and height range only).
  Clearance is not concave along a segment — it dips beside a notch — so endpoint checks cannot
  substitute, and scanning the plate at query time costs more than the walk it would save.
  `FCk_GroundNav_Plate::_MinClearanceUu` is therefore filled in `DoBake_Tile`'s crop pass (the loop
  that already publishes every cell's clamped clearance and bills no probes, because it is a copy,
  not a stage), as the min over the plate's published cells. Consequence for the pins: probes,
  walkable cells, plates, portals, seams, components all unchanged; the field's byte footprint grows
  by one float per plate and is asserted identical across runs, not absolute. This is the same class
  of addition PHASE_2 §9 already allows for 2C's boundary array — a query-side precompute over
  finished products — and is recorded as an amendment to that exception; maintainer veto open. Until
  a plate's minimum is at or above the body's radius the walk steps cells; at or above it, a move
  whose start and target cells lie in one plate is admitted with one cell read for the target height.
- **[NN-D45]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **Build status: a published field never says
  Building; the volume does.** `ECk_GroundNav_RegionStatus {Built, PartiallyBuilt, Building, Unbuilt,
  OutsideField}`. Over a field the answer is Built / Unbuilt / OutsideField (PartiallyBuilt for a box
  touching both kinds of tile); OutsideField is kept distinct from Unbuilt because ground no volume
  covers will never be built and a consumer must not wait on it. The volume layer promotes Unbuilt to
  Building while its build is in flight. Consequence for PHASE_2 §4 step 4's "Unbuilt → Building →
  Built exactly once per tile per build": the publish is atomic and the partial field is unreachable
  by construction (`_Partial` private, [P1-R3]), so per-tile Building is not observable and the
  transition is field-wide; the Layer-1 test asserts it over publisher + build state, the Layer-2 PIE
  test over the volume. Health composes as Building (in flight) > Ready (published, idle) > Error
  (nothing published, last build failed) > NoData.
- **[NN-D44]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **F1.20 area tags and cost multiplier ship as
  SHAPE, not content, this phase.** Nothing in the bake carries a traversal policy — [NN-D30] ruling 4
  deliberately emitted no policy key because markup is PHASE_4 — so `FCk_GroundNav_SurfaceAttributes`
  carries `_AreaTags` (empty) and `_CostMultiplier` (1.0) beside the real plate/layer/normal/clearance,
  and PHASE_2 §4 step 3's "attributes inside a painted region match the paint" verify is DEFERRED to
  PHASE_4 with the markup that gives it meaning. The doc's "use a bake-time authored area tag" has no
  input to author against; inventing one would be exactly the one-valued placeholder [NN-D30] refused.
  Doc-vs-code contradiction resolved by the orchestrator; maintainer veto open.
- **[NN-D43]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **Projection ranking and statuses, made exact.**
  PHASE_2 / F1.13 say "tie-break horizontal-first — a point 10 uu sideways beats one 50 uu below";
  a literal lexicographic horizontal-then-vertical order contradicts its own example (the cell
  directly below has horizontal distance 0 and would win). Rule adopted: per admitted candidate cell,
  horizontal distance = distance from the query XY to the cell square (0 inside), vertical distance =
  |query Z − surface Z|; rank by floor(vertical / profile StepHeight) FIRST, then horizontal, then
  vertical. This satisfies the example, gives "horizontal-first" a meaning (within one step band the
  horizontal distance decides) and encodes the behaviour containment depends on: keep the agent on the
  floor it stands on unless a surface within a step is directly under it. Down / Up restrict to
  surfaces at-or-below / at-or-above the query height. Statuses: radius above the field's clearance
  cap → `Blocked` at every entry (no new enumerator this phase; fail-closed is the [NN-D32] intent);
  a hit → Success even if an unbuilt tile was also reached; no hit + an unbuilt tile reached →
  `Unbuilt`; no hit + all reached tiles built, or no tile reached → `NoSurface`. Ring expansion
  terminates when the best candidate is in band 0 and the next ring is horizontally further.
- **[NN-D42]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **The surface normal is DERIVED at query time.**
  REPRESENTATION.md lists a quantized normal per cell, but P1 shipped the tile with exactly two
  per-cell arrays (height, clearance) and plate-level everything else, and PHASE_2 §9 forbids any bake
  change beyond the boundary array. The normal is recovered from central differences of `_SurfaceZ`
  over same-plate neighbours (one-sided at a plate edge, up on a one-cell plate); a plate is planar
  within the 10 uu merge tolerance, so this is the plate's plane to within that tolerance and a flat
  plate answers exactly up. Recorded as a knowing reconciliation of REPRESENTATION with the shipped
  tile, not a deviation from it; maintainer veto open.
- **[NN-D41]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **CkGroundNav depends on CkNavigation, not the
  reverse.** Verified: nothing depends on CkGroundNav, CkNavigation depends on no provider, and
  CkVoxelNav already depends on CkNavigation (the precedent for a provider living behind the seam).
  The query math therefore speaks the neutral `ECk_NavSurface_QueryStatus` / `ProviderHealth` /
  `ProjectionMode` enums directly — PHASE_2's illustrative `Get_BoundarySegments` signature already
  assumes this — and the GroundNav→facade adapter (2D step 4) lives in CkGroundNav and registers into
  CkNavigation, mirroring the Recast adapter's registrar rather than making the facade include a
  provider. `GameplayTags` added explicitly for `FGameplayTagContainer` in the attributes result.
- **[NN-D40]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-02, session 7) **F1.2's Provenance cell stands as written.** It
  names the open-source `recastnavigation` project (zlib) as the published reference for span-based
  heightfield rasterization. PROMPT.md §0 permits exactly that — "open-source code under permissive
  licenses" and public talks are named permitted sources; the forbidden class is proprietary sources
  and structural mirroring of them. No code was copied (P1 close-out: attribution none owed), and the
  F1.15/F1.16/F1.17 cells already use the identical form. The P1 close-out's flag on this row is
  resolved as CONFIRMED, not changed. VALIDATION §0's "no forbidden-source language" gate is therefore
  unaffected by any of the four cells.
- **[NN-D39]** (maintainer, 2026-09-01, session 6) **PHASE_1 closes WITHOUT the full-suite delta-zero
  run.** The maintainer's words: "Close P1 without running the full-suite." VALIDATION.md §0's
  phase-exit delta-zero gate and PHASE_1 §8's "full suite delta-zero" box are therefore NOT met and are
  recorded as WAIVED, not as passed: every number this phase carries is `--test-pattern GroundNav`. The
  claim P1 can make is "the GroundNav family is green on the final artifact and nothing outside the
  module was edited except the CkJolt query session, whose only other consumer is CkVoxelNav". The
  first full-suite run of the campaign re-establishes the baseline for PHASE_2 entry, which is where a
  regression this waiver let through would surface.
- **[NN-D38]** (maintainer, 2026-09-01, session 6) **THE CLOSED-COLLISION CONTRACT. Every Solid static
  body the bake reads MUST be a closed mesh; the bake does not rasterize vertical faces.** Resolves
  [P1-R11] by ruling the premise out rather than building for it. The maintainer's question was "why
  are we supporting [fence planes and bottomless walls]? Why not assume that all meshes that will cut
  the navmesh need to have thickness?" — and with closed collision the flush underside of a body
  resting on a floor is not a coincidence but an invariant: every solid presents a down-facing face in
  each column it covers, so [NN-D37]'s non-walkable-wins tie is the design, not a patch over a gap.
  Consequences taken in the same batch: (1) the contract is ENFORCED, not assumed — a closure check
  (`Bake/CkGroundNav_MeshClosure.h`) runs once per body per build over the body's WHOLE mesh (an
  unclipped per-body fetch, `ICk_GroundNav_GeometryBackend::Get_BodyTriangles`, because a region-clipped
  mesh has cut edges that look like holes), welds vertices within 0.1 uu, and counts edges used by only
  one triangle; (2) heightfields are `ECk_GroundNav_BodyKind::Surface` and exempt — a surface has no
  interior to describe and its steep parts rasterize unwalkable on slope alone; (3) a violation does
  NOT fail the bake: the field publishes with the offenders recorded on `FCk_GroundNav_Field::_OpenBodies`
  (one bad asset must not take a level's navigation down) and ONE warning per build names them, which
  the AutoTest harness escalates to a failure so CI catches it; (4) the debugger draws every open body
  in RED in every mode, prints them at the top of the summary, and puts a line on screen — the
  maintainer's instruction was "make sure the debugger is VERY clear about this and shows it to the
  dev". What the contract costs: Recast walked open geometry, so any fence plane or complex-collision
  shell without an underside that navigated under Recast bakes open here and is flagged; whether such
  collision exists in BusterBlock or Rewind99 is an audit still to run. What it saves: no vertical-face
  rasterization, no boundary-side assignment rule, no tie-order change, no re-pin of every fixture on
  a rasterizer change, and [NN-D25]'s ledge semantics untouched.
- **[NN-D37]** (maintainer, 2026-09-01, session 5 — the "nothing deferred" batch) **Exact-height
  span ties break on CONTENT, never on arrival order.** `DoInsert_Span`'s `_MaxZ` compare was strict,
  so on an exact tie the last-submitted triangle's walkability won ([P1-R6]). Rule: on equal `_MaxZ`
  the NON-walkable surface wins, then the quantized normal orders lexicographically (`_Z`, `_Y`,
  `_X`). No epsilon; the order is total over the span's content. Pinned by a span-level
  order-reversal test.
  **Amended the same session, from gate evidence.** The first cut let the WALKABLE face win. Gate 1
  (`Saved/Logs/P1-Batch2-Gate1.log`) then baked the reference scene with plates 22 -> 15, intra-tile
  portals 29 -> 0, seams 14, components 5: every wall and the pillar stopped blocking. A body resting
  on a floor has its bottom face exactly flush with the floor's top — an exact tie in every scene —
  and the rasterizer only ever sees faces, never a body's interior, so that flush non-walkable face is
  the ONLY evidence a column holds that the floor there is covered. The old strict compare produced
  solid walls purely because walls were submitted after the ground. **Non-walkable wins is the
  conservative reading and the one the module's fail-closed stance requires.** It reproduces the
  pinned plate count by content rather than by arrival order.
  **[P1-R11] raised, open:** walls are solid in the span field only through this flush-face
  coincidence. A body hovering above the floor by any amount, or a vertical face with no bottom face
  in a column (`Get_TwiceProjectedArea` drops zero-footprint faces as slivers), leaves the column
  open. The honest fix is Recast's: rasterize a vertical face as a non-walkable span over its Z range
  in every cell its edge crosses. That changes walkable-cell, component and plate counts on every
  fixture and is a maintainer ruling, not an executor call.
- **[NN-D36]** (maintainer, 2026-09-01, session 5) **A sliced build FAILS CLOSED when the geometry
  backend's world changes under it.** The backend exposes `Get_WorldRevision()` (Jolt: the world's
  static-scene revision counter, forwarded JPH-free through the query session; Stub: a test-bumpable
  member). **Amended 2026-09-01, session 6:** the first cut SUMMED the static-scene and body-removed
  counters. The body-removed counter is bumped for EVERY motion type (`CkJoltBody_Processor.cpp`, the
  EndPlay funnel: "every motion type, not just Static"), so a despawning projectile or NPC anywhere in
  the level would have aborted every multi-frame bake — in a live scene the sliced build could never
  finish. The bake reads static bodies only (`Get_StaticTrianglesInAABox` enforces `IsStatic()`), and
  every static add / remove / move / motion-type change and the cooked static world already bump the
  static-scene counter, so that counter alone is the honest token. The orphaned
  `UCk_Jolt_Subsystem::Get_BodyRemovedRevision` was removed with it. The build state captures it on its first slice and re-checks it before
  every tile's fetch; a mismatch sets the build `Failed`, returns the new
  `ECk_GroundNav_BakeStatus::StaleGeometry`, and publishes nothing. The counter is world-wide, so a
  change far from the volume also aborts — accepted: a rebuild is cheaper than a silently vanished
  seam portal, the failure class [NN-D33] exists to prevent. Seam derivation additionally counts
  unmatched stubs and warns once per build, so any residual disagreement fails an autotest instead
  of reading as BLOCKED. Resolves [P1-R5]; first in-house precedent (CkVoxelNav has the same
  exposure and no mitigation).
- **[NN-D35]** (maintainer, 2026-09-01, session 5) **ONE PROBE IS ONE INNERMOST CELL OR SPAN READ.**
  Every bake stage bills its dominant loop(s) in that unit and nothing else, so the per-tile sum is
  a homogeneous cost a budget can be denominated in, and every billed loop iterates in index order
  over value arrays so the count stays exactly reproducible. Resolves [P1-R7], which found the six
  stages billing triangles / spans / assigned spans / cells×layers / plate COUNT / span visits and
  ~40% of the pinned 117962 coming from the cheapest stage. The name "probe" is kept (F1.10 and
  PHASE_1 use it); the definition lives on `FCk_GroundNav_BakeStageResult`. [NN-D34]'s tile-granular
  slice budget stands unchanged — its determinism argument was never in question, only its unit. The
  reference pin is re-measured once with the per-stage delta explained in the commit, and the
  volume's default `_ProbeBudgetPerTick` is re-derived from one measured reference tile.

### [P1-B1] Geometry-backend input fork — RESOLVED by the maintainer as [NN-D24]

**The contradiction.** PHASE_1 §4 step 3 specifies a seam whose middle operation is
`Get_ShapeGeometry(BodyRef, GeometryBatch&)` — triangle-soup extraction — and REPRESENTATION.md's
provenance line names *span-based heightfield rasterization*, a triangle algorithm. But CkJolt's
JPH-free surface (`ck::jolt::FCk_Jolt_QuerySession`) exposes only `Get_IsBoxOccupied`,
`Get_IsSegmentBlocked` and `Get_BodiesInAABox` — **no geometry extraction of any kind**. The only
triangle extractor in CkJolt, `StaticWorld/CkJoltBakeExtraction.h`, is JPH-typed
(`JPH::VertexList`, `JPH::IndexedTriangleList`), which PHASE_1's own fence forbids CkGroundNav from
touching ("no `<Jolt/...>` include may appear in CkGroundNav", plus the zero-hit exit criterion).
Executor could not resolve this locally: PROMPT §8 STOP (contradiction between a campaign document
and the code).

**[NN-D24]** (maintainer, 2026-08-31) **Extend CkJolt's JPH-free surface with geometry extraction**,
then rasterize triangles as PHASE_1 and REPRESENTATION.md literally specify. The executor's
recommendation (ray-sampled spans via the existing `Get_RayCastMulti`, which returns position and
normal and would have made F1.10's probe-count budget literal, at the cost of rewriting
REPRESENTATION.md's provenance) was **not** taken. Consequence accepted: exact, conservative bake
input that captures thin geometry and overhangs sampling would miss; campaign provenance unchanged.

**Scope consequence:** CkGroundNav is no longer the only module this phase touches — CkJolt gains
public API. That is outside the campaign's chartered file inventory, so it lands as its OWN commit on
`feature/ck-navigation` and is called out separately in any review request.

### 1A progress
