### 1B step 1 — span rasterization: DONE and gated (15/15)

`Saved/Logs/P1-1B-Gate.log`: Total 15 / Passed 15 / Failed 0. Total rose 9 -> 15, so the new tests
ran rather than riding a cached discovery list.

- `DoRasterizeSpans` clips each triangle to every column it covers (Sutherland-Hodgman against the
  column's four axis-aligned planes) and contributes that column's Z interval. Point-sampling the
  cell centre would drop a triangle narrower than a cell, and a dropped floor is invisible until an
  agent falls through it. Clipping is done once per ROW then per column within it, so a wide
  triangle does not re-clip from scratch per column.
- Spans within the profile's step height merge, keeping the HIGHEST contributing surface's normal.
  That is what collapses a floor box's six faces into one span instead of three stacked ones.
- Normals are quantized to three signed bytes: full precision buys nothing against a merge cone
  measured in whole degrees, and quantizing makes the bake bit-reproducible across triangle
  orderings. A degenerate normal stores as sideways, never as straight up, so it cannot read as a
  perfect floor.
- Fixtures green first run of the algorithm: flat floor = one span per column at the authored height
  +/- half a cell height; two floors 1000uu apart = exactly two spans per column, ordered; a 20uu gap
  merges at a 40uu climb threshold and does NOT at 10uu; a 45-degree ramp is monotone along its run
  with every sampled normal within tolerance of the analytic normal; degenerate (collinear) and
  non-finite triangles are dropped AND counted; an over-budget lattice fails `LimitExceeded` and
  publishes zero columns.

**Profile contract tightened (executor, mechanical):** a profile with NO authored `_StandingExtents`
is now rejected at admission (`MissingStandingExtents`), and `Get_StandingHeightUu()` derives full
height per shape type. Headroom is undecidable without an agent height, and waiving the headroom test
would report every crawlspace as walkable — the permissive direction Gate 1A-4 names as unacceptable.
The default-constructed profile is therefore no longer admissible; the affected fixtures now author a
human-sized capsule (56 half-height + 34 radius = 180uu).

### [P1-B2] `_LedgeSensitivity` semantics — RESOLVED by the maintainer as [NN-D25]

**Ruling (maintainer, 2026-08-31, session 3):** "If you think it does not make sense, then omit it
for now. But if you think there is a missing tuner and this fits the bill, then go with that
explanation." Executor judged that it does fit, and implemented it. Fable 5 was NOT consulted — the
fit was unambiguous enough not to spend that budget.

**[NN-D25] `_LedgeSensitivity` is the reciprocal of how many sides must drop away.**

FEATURE_MATRIX F1.3 defines the ledge filter as demoting a span whose neighbours differ in height by
more than the climb threshold **"on enough sides"**. That sentence leaves exactly one quantity
unspecified — how many sides is "enough" — and the profile carries exactly one unexplained tunable.
That is the fit. The height threshold in the same sentence is explicitly pinned to the climb
threshold, so it is NOT the free quantity, which eliminates candidate readings (a) and (b).

    RequiredDroppingSides = LedgeSensitivity <= 0 ? (never met) : clamp(ceil(1 / Sensitivity), 1, 4)

- Default 1.0 → one dropping side demotes: the most conservative setting, which is the right default
  because the constrain processor's correctness rests on this field being conservative.
- Below 1.0 → more sides must drop. This is what narrow authored geometry needs: a one-cell catwalk
  drops away on BOTH long sides and is erased entirely at the default. 0.34 demands three.
- 0 → filter off.
- The name stays monotone (sensitivity up = fires more readily), saturating at 1 side. Candidate (a)
  inverted the name; (c) was a radius, which the spec's wording does not support.

Documented on the member in `CkGroundNav_AgentProfile.h`, pinned by
`Walkability_LedgeSensitivityRelaxesForNarrowGeometry` at 1.0 / 0.34 / 0.0.

### [NN-D26] The ledge filter tests FALLING, not height difference

Surfaced while authoring the cliff fixture, and not a design fork — a literal reading of F1.3
("neighbours differ in height by more than the climb threshold") demotes the ground at the FOOT of a
cliff just as readily as the lip, because the height difference is identical in both directions. The
filter is named for falling and its stated rationale is "the lip of a drop", so difference alone is
the wrong predicate.

**Rule as shipped.** A side is supported when the neighbouring column holds a span with
`_MaxZ >= TopZ - StepHeight` AND `_MinZ <= TopZ + StandingHeight` — something reaching at least one
step below our feet whose underside starts no higher than our head.

- The LOWER bound is the fall test: an agent cannot fall upward, so a wall or a rising ramp beside us
  is support, not a drop.
- The UPPER bound stops a floor far overhead from counting, so a walkway with a bridge above it and a
  void beside it still reads as the ledge it is.

**One intermediate version was wrong and was replaced before gating.** The first attempt asked for a
surface within one step OR a solid *straddling* our height. That is correct for solid spans, but the
rasterizer builds spans from FACES, so a ramp rising beside a deck contributes a thin surface span
that straddles nothing — every ramp edge and every step-up in the world read as a ledge. The
head-height bound subsumes the straddle case and does not over-fire. Both halves are pinned by
`Walkability_LedgeFilterReadsAWallAsSupportNotADrop`, whose control is a floor 200 uu overhead that
must still be demoted.

### Rasterizer defect found and fixed while authoring the cliff fixture

A vertical face lying exactly on a cell boundary survived Sutherland-Hodgman clipping into BOTH
adjacent columns as a three-or-more-vertex sliver of zero XY footprint, planting a phantom surface in
the column BEYOND the wall. Every cliff edge would have had a phantom floor beside it, and the ledge
filter would never have fired anywhere. Fixed by rejecting a clipped polygon whose projected area is
below `CellSize^2 * 1e-4` — scaled to the cell so one threshold holds at every cell size, and orders
of magnitude below any genuinely thin triangle. The six 1B-step-1 fixtures stay green across the fix.


### 1B steps 2-3 — walkability filtering + layer extraction: DONE and gated (24/24)

Gate: `--test-pattern GroundNav --discover-fresh` → **Total 24, Passed 24, Failed 0**
(`Saved/Logs/P1-1B3-Gate.log`). Total rose 15 → 24: six walkability tests, three layer tests.

**F1.3 as three ordered passes** (`CkGroundNav_Walkability.{h,cpp}`), each callable and tested on its
own, plus one driver that fixes the order:
- `DoFilter_LowClearance` — headroom to the next span up, against the profile's standing height.
- `DoFilter_Ledges` — per [NN-D25] and [NN-D26]. Demotions are decided against the state on ENTRY and
  applied together, so no span's verdict depends on where in the scan its neighbour was visited;
  within-pass cascading would have been exactly the pass-order dependence Gate 1B-2 forbids.
- `DoBuild_Connections` — 4-neighbour mask storing the neighbour's SPAN INDEX, not a flag: a column
  may hold several floors and "connected north" does not say which one. Rough-perch tolerance waives
  the slope-change test for surfaces closer together than the tolerance; zero waives nothing.

Connections are **symmetric by construction** — an edge survives only if the far span records the
mirror edge. Nearest-span tie-breaking is per-span, so in a column with several reachable floors the
two ends can disagree, and a one-way edge would make a flood fill depend on where it started.

Four neighbours, not eight: a diagonal step crosses a corner an agent's volume cannot pass, so
admitting one would let paths cut through the one place the clearance field says they must not.

**F1.4 layer extraction** (`CkGroundNav_Layers.{h,cpp}`): flood fill over the mask in column-major
seed order, then a component is placed WHOLE into the lowest layer whose footprint it does not
intersect — which is how "mutually reachable spans land in the same layer" is honoured. A component
that overlaps ITSELF cannot satisfy that for any layer, so it alone is split span by span. That is the
spiral-ramp case, and it stays one component across the split. Nothing is ever dropped: a span that
fits nowhere opens a new layer.

**Gate 1B-2 conditions, checked explicitly:** all three named fixtures behave as specified; no filter
result depends on pass order beyond the declared one; no second definition of "walkable" exists — the
mask is the only adjacency, and `DoBuild_Connections` refuses to record an edge touching a demoted
span (pinned by `Walkability_ConnectionsAreSymmetricAndSkipDemotedSpans`).

**One fixture was weakened deliberately, and it is worth flagging.** F1.3's cliff fixture was written
to also assert the foot of the cliff survives. It cannot, as authored: the ground box runs under the
plateau, so the neighbouring column has ground at the same height and the assertion would pass without
testing anything. The rule is pinned instead by a hand-built three-column span field
(`Walkability_LedgeFilterReadsAWallAsSupportNotADrop`) whose control is a floor 200 uu overhead that
must still be demoted.

**Known modelling limitation, recorded rather than worked around.** Spans are built from FACES, not
solids, so a box contributes a thin span at its top and another at its bottom with nothing between.
A column under a plateau therefore carries the ground beneath it as a separate walkable span. This is
correct for the acceptance criteria as written (F1.2's "two floors 1000 uu apart are exactly two spans
per column" depends on it) but it means "the column's spans" is not "the column's solids". If a later
phase needs solid intervals, that is a rasterizer change, not a filter change.

### 1C step 1 — per-cell clearance (F1.5): DONE and gated (27/27)

Gate: `--test-pattern GroundNav --discover-fresh` → **Total 27, Passed 27, Failed 0**
(`Saved/Logs/P1-1C1-Gate.log`). Total rose 24 → 27.

Two-pass chamfer sweep (3 orthogonal / 4 diagonal, Borgefors 1986) per layer, integer throughout and
scaled to world units only at the end, so two bakes of the same input agree bit for bit.

**The brute-force agreement is EXACT, not approximate**, and that is a property worth recording: the
two-pass sweep computes the chamfer metric exactly, so the reference test asserts equality rather than
a tolerance. A tolerance would let a propagation bug hide. The reference is an exhaustive
min-over-blocked-cells scan using the closed-form chamfer distance — an independent implementation,
not a re-derivation of the sweep. It agrees on all 3072 walkable cells of the L fixture.

Acceptance criteria met: centre of a 1000 uu square = 500 uu (within one cell diagonal); a 90 uu
corridor spine = 45 uu (within one cell); an isolated single cell = exactly one cell size; concave
corner covered by the L fixture; `clearance >= R` admits the same set as the reference, and that set
is asserted non-trivial.

**[NN-D27] The field border counts as blocked, and tiling owes a halo because of it.** The open-square
acceptance criterion REQUIRES it — without a blocked border a fully walkable field has unbounded
interior clearance and the number means nothing. The consequence is that clearance reads short near
the border. VALIDATION A3 names the cross-tile case as "the one that silently breaks", and this is
exactly the mechanism: a tiled bake that computes clearance per tile in isolation gets a false pinch
at every seam. **1D must rasterize a halo beyond each tile boundary** rather than treating the tile
edge as the field edge. Recorded in the header contract as well as here.

**Cross-tile clearance is NOT yet verified** — there are no tiles until 1D, so VALIDATION A3's
tile-boundary evidence is owed by 1D, not satisfied here. Not claiming otherwise.

### 1C step 2 — merged-plate decomposition (F1.6): IMPLEMENTED and gated (31/31), FROZEN VALUES OWED

Gate: `--test-pattern GroundNav --discover-fresh` → **Total 31, Passed 31, Failed 0**
(`Saved/Logs/P1-1C2-Final.log`). Total rose 27 → 31.

Greedy rectangles per layer, row-major seeds, grown along X then Y. Exactly the three specified merge
criteria — no fourth added, none removed. `FCk_GroundNav_MergeTunables` exposes the two tolerances;
policy equality is not a tolerance and is not exposed as one.

Each plate carries BOTH `_MaxPlaneResidualUu` and `_HeightRangeUu`. The second exists because the
first cannot detect an over-merge: a plane fits two treads perfectly well by tilting through them, so
residual stays small while the plate flattens a step. Height range does not.

Acceptance criteria met at the default tunables: flat 100x100 plane = 1 plate; plane with one hole =
4 plates with the hole still absent; 12-tread staircase = 12 plates (inside the 12-24 band) with zero
height range; identical across 100 runs; identical under reversed geometry submission order.

**Gate 1C-2 sweep is MEASURED and recorded in § Blockers as [P1-C1]. The executor has not picked the
frozen values and will not proceed past 1C without them.** Two findings there need a ruling rather
than a number: the normal cone is inert on both named fixtures (so this sweep cannot inform it at
all), and shatter/over-merge have no overlapping window under the gate's own definitions — which
Gate 1C-2 itself names as the condition that makes the merge criteria an orchestrator decision.

### 1C steps 3-4 — portals (F1.7) + the pinch-point pin: DONE and gated (41/41)

Gate: `--test-pattern GroundNav --parallel 1 --no-nullrhi --discover-fresh` → **Total 41, Passed 41,
Failed 0** (`Saved/Logs/P1-1C3-Portals-Gate3.log`). Total rose 36 → 41, so the five new tests ran
rather than riding a cached discovery list, and the delta is exactly the tests added.

`CkGroundNav_Portals.{h,cpp}` — `FCk_GroundNav_Portal` (plate pair, crossing direction, the A-side
cell interval, the surface height at either end, `_TraversalClearanceUu`) and
`FCk_GroundNav_PortalField` (portals + the plate → portals adjacency table). Extraction reads the
connection field, collects one record per crossing cell pair, sorts on a total key
(direction, boundary line, plate pair, position along the interval) and runs neighbouring records
together. Sorting rather than grouping in the enumeration loop is deliberate: the loop visits every
layer of a column before moving on, so crossings of one boundary arrive interleaved with crossings of
the boundary a floor above.

**Enumerating only the two POSITIVE directions is complete, not a shortcut.** `DoBuild_Connections`
drops any edge the far span does not name back (`CkGroundNav_Walkability.cpp:316-350`), so every
surviving connection is mutual and each boundary is visited exactly once from one side. Verified by
reading that pass, not assumed from the header's "symmetric by construction".

Tests (`Test_GroundNav_Portals.cpp`), against F1.7's acceptance criteria:
- **one doorway = one portal**, spanning the whole three-cell mouth, traversal clearance 50.0 uu
  against a 75 uu corridor — half the width within one cell, as the criterion requires;
- **an unreachable island yields zero portals** and an empty adjacency row (the criterion's
  fully-enclosed plate);
- **the pinch-point pin (step 4)**: two 500 uu rooms joined by that corridor. Both portals reject a
  100 uu radius; both rooms' own best-cell clearance (250 uu) would have admitted it. This is the
  assertion the whole representation was chosen for, and it is now green;
- **byte-identical across 8 repeat bakes and under reversed geometry submission order**;
- **a cross-layer crossing is extracted** on the stacked-decks fixture — see [NN-D31].

Also landed, and the reason it is worth having now: the debug tool draws portals
(`ck.GroundNav.Debug.Mode 4`), coloured blue-to-red by traversal clearance against the widest
crossing in the bake, with a mast on any crossing that changes floor, and the summary block gained
`portals : N crossings (M change floor, tightest lets X uu through)`. The gym panel carries mode 4
with its legend, and the station text points at the two pillars — 160 uu apart, so the crossing
between them offers about 80 uu whatever the open floor either side reads. The snapshot's portal copy
is asserted too: two disconnected storeys must produce zero portals, because a route the world does
not have is the one error a path consumer cannot detect for itself.

### 1C step 3 — portal contract rulings (2026-09-01, session 4)

**[NN-D30]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-01) Portal contract rulings. Four forks were open between
PHASE_1 §6 step 3, FEATURE_MATRIX F1.7, and what the bake actually holds today; all four ruled here
before a line was written, none delegated.

1. **The portal's clearance number is the tightest point on the WIDEST crossing — a max over the
   interval of a min across it — not a min over the whole interval.** F1.7 words it "minimum
   clearance across the crossing", which reads as a plain min, but its own acceptance criterion
   demands `door width / 2`, and that is the value at the door's CENTRE. A plain min over the
   interval would return the jamb cell's half-cell clearance for every portal in the field and
   nothing would ever pass. Per crossing cell-pair the value is `min(clearance(A), clearance(B))` —
   an agent must fit on both sides of the step — and the portal takes the `max` of that over its
   interval, because the agent chooses where along the portal to cross. Field named
   `_TraversalClearanceUu` so the aggregate is not mistaken for a plain minimum.
2. **Adjacency is read from the connection field only, never recomputed from plate rectangles.**
   `CkGroundNav_Walkability.h` states the connection field is the ONLY adjacency downstream may
   consult, and a second definition of "these two cells are neighbours" would drift from the first.
   The consequence is a simplification worth stating: cross-LAYER portals need no special case at
   all. A connection already names the neighbour SPAN, so a ramp that changes layer resolves to a
   different plate through exactly the same lookup as a flat crossing.
3. **One portal per maximal contiguous run of crossings between the same plate pair, not one per
   plate pair.** Two separate doors between the same two rooms are two crossings with two different
   widths; folding them into one portal would take one number for both and understate the wide one.
   Runs are keyed on (axis, boundary line, plate pair) and broken wherever the interval breaks.
4. **The traversal policy key is NOT emitted this phase.** F1.7 lists one, but nothing in the bake
   carries a policy today — area markup is F1.31–F1.35 in PHASE_4, and PHASE_1 §9 fences markup out
   explicitly. The plate merge's third criterion ("policy equality") is currently satisfied by
   walkability alone. Emitting a field with exactly one possible value would be a placeholder, so
   P4 adds it with the markup that gives it meaning.

**[NN-D31] A cross-layer crossing requires a SELF-OVERLAPPING component, and a ramp climbing over
open floor is not one.** Found by a red test, not by reading. The first cross-layer fixture — a ramp
rising over the floor it starts on — produced two layers and ZERO cross-layer portals. The mechanism
is `DoBuild_Connections`' second pass (`CkGroundNav_Walkability.cpp:316-350`): an edge survives only
if the far span names it back, and nearest-span tie-breaking makes the two ends disagree exactly
where a ramp lifts off a floor. The floor beneath is severed from the ramp, so the two land on
different layers with nothing joining them — correct behaviour, and a fixture that proves nothing.

The layered representation only produces a cross-layer connection where ONE component covers a
column twice, which is what layer extraction splits span-by-span. The fixture was therefore replaced
with the shape `Layers_RampOverItselfSplitsWithoutSevering` already pins — two decks over one
footprint joined by a ramp in the strip behind them — where the ramp arrives at the upper deck
already on another layer. Reusing a proven fixture rather than inventing a second one is deliberate:
the first attempt cost a gate cycle precisely because it was invented.

**Deferred to 1D, not skipped:** F1.7's cross-tile clauses (a portal emitted only once both tiles are
built; a wall across a tile boundary yielding zero portals; remove-and-re-add reproducing
byte-identical portals) need tiles, and tiles are 1D. This is the same deferral [NN-D27] records for
cross-tile clearance, and PHASE_1 §10 requires both before P1 can close. Within-field byte-identity
across repeated bakes IS asserted here.

sub-phase: **1D-a** halo-aware single-tile bake + field value types, **1D-b** field assembly +
cross-tile portals + immutable publish/epochs + reachability, **1D-c** slicing and the probe budget,
**1D-d** the ECS shell.

being exactly the four new tile tests. Published: CkFoundation `204d60738` · CkTests `82caa5fb` ·
root `579b481`.

`Field/CkGroundNav_FieldTypes.{h,cpp}` (epoch, tile coord, `ECk_GroundNav_BuildStatus`, the published
`FCk_GroundNav_Tile`) and `Field/CkGroundNav_TileBake.{h,cpp}` (`Get_HaloCellCount`, `Get_TileBounds`,
`Get_TileHaloBounds`, `DoBake_Tile`). The pipeline runs over the halo-expanded lattice through
clearance, the halo is masked out before decomposition, and the crop rebases plates and portals into
tile-local coordinates.

**The seam evidence VALIDATION A3 asks for is now green**, and in its strong form: every cell a tile
publishes carries the EXACT number a bake of far wider ground gives that cell, clamped to the cap, on a
fixture with a hole inside the tile so the comparison is not a field of identical caps agreeing with
itself. That is the cross-tile clearance case [NN-D27] owed, satisfied at the tile level; the remaining
cross-tile items (seam portals, remove-and-re-add byte-identity) belong to 1D-b.

**One defect found and fixed by self-review before the commit**: the crop clamped published cells to
the cap but left portal traversal clearance on the halo's unclamped transform, so a crossing could
advertise more room than either side of it admits — exactly the agent the cap exists to turn away. Both
the clamp and an assertion that no published number exceeds the cap are in.

Two small enablers rode along: `_TileSizeUu` became settable (`CK_PROPERTY`) because nothing could
configure a tile otherwise, and `FCk_GroundNav_Portal::Get_Endpoints` gained an origin-and-cell-size
form so a published tile can answer it without keeping the span field it was derived from.

**Module doc written but NOT tracked**: `Source/CkGroundNav/Claude.md`. CkFoundation's `.gitignore:49`
ignores `*.md` wholesale; `CkVoxelNav/Claude.md` sits untracked the same way. Force-adding it would be
a unilateral change to the repo's own convention, so it is left untracked like its sibling and noted
here instead.

**[NN-D34] The resumable unit of a sliced build is the TILE, and the probe budget gates whether a
tile STARTS rather than interrupting one.** Ruled before writing 1D-e, because it is a knowing
deviation from PHASE_1 step 4 rather than an oversight.

PHASE_1 says a slice consumes "at most N probes". With tile granularity a slice can overshoot by one
tile's cost: the builder checks its remaining budget before beginning a tile and then runs that tile to
completion. The alternative — pausing inside the pipeline — means checkpointing the rasterizer, the
walkability pass, or the distance transform, and a resume point inside a stage is exactly where Gate
1D-4's named failure comes from: "probe count varies with slice size means the slice boundary is
re-probing".

A tile that is never split cannot re-probe, so its probe count is identical whatever the slice size,
which is the property Gate 1D-4 actually measures. The overshoot is bounded and reportable (a tile's
cost is deterministic for a given fixture and config), where a mid-stage checkpoint would trade a
bounded overshoot for an unbounded correctness risk. Recorded as a deviation with its reason; the
maintainer may reverse it, and reversing it is a rewrite of the slice mechanism rather than a tweak.

**[NN-D33] Cross-tile connectivity will be carried by SEAM STUBS the tile bake emits, not
re-derived at composition.** Recorded before 1D-b is written, because the obvious implementation is
the wrong one: a published tile keeps no span field and no connection field, so composing two tiles
tempts a fresh "these two cells are adjacent if their heights differ by less than a step" test — a
second definition of adjacency, which [NN-D30] ruling 2 exists to forbid. Instead each tile emits,
per edge cell and layer, what its OWN halo connection field already knows about the crossing, and
composition pairs stubs that both sides agree on. That mirrors the mutual-agreement rule the
connection pass already enforces, and it keeps one definition of adjacency in the codebase.

**1D-b DONE and gated (49/49)** — `Saved/Logs/P1-1Db-FieldPublish-Gate.log`, up from 45, delta
exactly the four new publish tests. Published: CkFoundation `77dadab79` · CkTests `50996a77` · root
`afd6c74`.

`Field/CkGroundNav_Field.{h,cpp}`: `FCk_GroundNav_FieldParams` (settings held once for the whole field,
not per tile), `FCk_GroundNav_Field`, `FCk_GroundNav_FieldPtr = TSharedPtr<const ...>`,
`FCk_GroundNav_FieldPublisher`, and `DoBake_Field` which bakes every tile from geometry fetched for its
HALO bounds.

**1D's stated exit criterion is met for the publish half**: a reader holding the field across a full
rebuild observes the old contents exactly, at the epoch it took, while the publisher has moved on — and
the test asserts the rebuild genuinely changed the world first, so it could not pass against a
publisher that did nothing. Also pinned: a recorded failure leaves the published pointer and epoch
untouched; the aggregated tile-epoch SUM rises whichever tile rebuilds and lands on the same number for
two different orders; a point outside the field resolves to nothing rather than to the nearest tile.

Still owed by 1D: seam portals (1D-c, designed below), reachability (1D-d), the probe budget and
`SlicedBakeMatchesOneShotBake` (1D-e), and the ECS shell (1D-f).

**1D-c DONE and gated (54/54)** — `Saved/Logs/P1-1Dc-Seams-Gate.log`, up from 49, delta exactly the
five new seam tests. Published: CkFoundation `0a578d776` · CkTests `432bffb1` · root `2d4f793`.
Implemented as designed below; no fork appeared while writing it.

**F1.7 is now COMPLETE, cross-tile clauses included.** Its acceptance criteria, each with a green
test: a floor across a boundary produces a crossing whose interval matches the floor width (a 100 uu
bridge yields exactly four cells, starting where the bridge starts); a wall — here two missing cell
columns — produces none while both tiles still carry plenty of ground; and removing and re-adding a
tile reproduces byte-identical portals, with the rebuilt tile carrying a newer epoch so the test cannot
pass against a rebuild that never ran.

**An unbuilt neighbour is distinguishable from a wall, and the test proves the difference is
recoverable**: marking a tile Unbuilt drops the crossing, the built tile keeps every stub it observed,
and restoring the neighbour brings the crossing back WITHOUT re-baking the tile that never went away.
That is the property streaming needs and the one a "boundary is blocked until proven otherwise" shortcut
would quietly destroy.

Design note kept for later phases: seam portals carry TILE-LOCAL plate indices on both ends, so a plate
is only addressable through the tile beside it. A field-wide plate id would have to be re-derived every
time any tile rebuilt, which is exactly the kind of identity churn [NN-D7] fences against.

**1D-d DONE and gated (58/58)** — `Saved/Logs/P1-1Dd-Reach-Gate2.log`, up from 54, delta exactly the
four new reachability tests. Published: CkFoundation `0b8ffff58` · CkTests `ff1acecc` · root `f223deb`.

F1.9 as PHASE_1 step 3 specifies it: union-find over every crossing (within-tile portals and seam
portals both), one label per plate, flat storage with a per-tile offset table. **The honest limitation
is enforced by the API rather than documented beside it** — the accessor is
`Get_AreProvablyDisconnected` and there is deliberately no `Get_AreReachable`, because a different
label proves unreachability while the same label proves only that something of zero width could pass.
An unlabelled plate answers "not proven disconnected", since this result is used to refuse work and
refusing on a hole in the data is worse than not refusing.

Labels are numbered in scan order AFTER merging, and the union always points at the lower index, so the
numbering depends only on connectivity and never on the order tiles were built or merges arrived in.
Verified green: separate ground is separate components; a door merges them on rebuild; ground across a
seam is ONE component (a labelling that stopped at tile boundaries would refuse every route that left a
tile); re-labelling an unchanged field reproduces the same numbers; and the wrong-half case — two plates
sharing a label with a doorway no fat agent can use.

**One red gate en route, fixed in one attempt**: `auto X = INDEX_NONE` deduces UE5's unnamed enum
rather than `int32`, so the variable would not bind to an `int32&` out-parameter (C2664) nor mix with
`FMath::Max` (C2672). Braced as `int32{INDEX_NONE}`. Saved to memory, since the declaration compiles
fine on its own and the error surfaces at the call site where it reads as the callee's fault.

**1D-e DONE and gated (61/61)** — `Saved/Logs/P1-1De-Budget-Gate2.log`, up from 58, delta exactly the
three new budget tests. Published: CkFoundation `a927d24d0` · CkTests `3a8d57c0` · root `ff9353b`.

`Field/CkGroundNav_FieldBuild.{h,cpp}`: `FCk_GroundNav_FieldBuildState`, `Request_BeginBuild`,
`Request_AdvanceBuild`, `Get_CompletedField`. Implemented per [NN-D34]; no new fork appeared.

**Gate 1D-4 is satisfied on its own terms.** `SlicedBakeMatchesOneShotBake` compares BYTE FOR BYTE —
every surface height, clearance value, plate rectangle, within-tile crossing, seam stub, seam portal and
component label — across a 3x3 field built in nine slices at a one-probe budget. The probe total is
asserted equal across four slice sizes (1, quarter, exact, tenfold) and against the one-shot bake; a
count that moved with the slice size is precisely the re-probing defect the gate names, and it does not
occur because a tile is never split.

**The partial field is unreachable, and that is enforced rather than documented.** `_Partial` is the
one private member in a module whose value types are otherwise plain aggregates, reachable only through
`Get_CompletedField`, which returns nullptr until the build is whole. The test checks this on EVERY
slice rather than once at the start.

**Two mechanical compile failures en route, both mine, both from the same over-caution**: I moved the
friend declarations off the export macro to dodge a suspected "dllexport on a first declaration"
problem, which produced C4273 inconsistent-dll-linkage instead — they are REdeclarations of already
exported functions and must carry the macro. Restored, with the reason in a comment so it is not
"cleaned up" again.

**1D-f LANDED and gated (63/63), with one verification explicitly OWED** —
`Saved/Logs/P1-1Df-Volume-Gate4.log`, up from 61, delta exactly the two new composition tests.
Published: CkFoundation `aded2cf2a` · CkTests `1edac5ee` · root `9ca0ae6`.

`Volume/CkGroundNavVolume_{Fragment_Data.h, Fragment.h, Processor.h/.cpp, Utils.h/.cpp}` — the feature
quartet, mirrored from CkVoxelNav's volume as PHASE_1 §5 directs. Four processors, all registered
(`CK_REGISTER_PROCESSOR` x4 verified by grep on the final artifact): Setup, HandleRequests, StartBuild,
Build. The two that read world geometry sit in `FGroup_Transform` after `FProcessor_JoltWorld_WaitForAsync`
and before `FProcessor_JoltWorld_Step`, the placement PHASE_1 names; Build declares no `MarkedDirtyBy`,
so the scheduler runs it once per main tick and never pumps it, which is what makes a per-tick budget
mean anything.

**What is NOT verified, and this is the honest gap**: PHASE_1 §5's verify line asks for "a Layer-1 or
headless test drives a build to completion through the request". The two tests written cover COMPOSITION
only — what `Add` puts on the entity, what the accessors report before anything bakes, that a request
lands on the queue rather than baking inline. Driving the build end to end needs a physics world for the
geometry backend AND the real processor graph to tick it, and the headless registry harness
(`ck::FEcsWorld`) has neither. That verification is owed as an AngelScript AutoTest or an
`[EDITOR-VERIFY]`, and until it exists **the volume path has never actually run**. The scope note is
written into the test file itself rather than left to this document.

A third test was written and then DELETED rather than shipped: it asserted that `Request_Build` on an
invalid handle fires `Failed_NotEnqueued` instead of stranding the caller. It would have tripped its own
`CK_ENSURE`, and `FCk_Delegate_Request_OnCompleted` is a dynamic delegate with no `BindLambda`. The
contract is implemented; asserting it needs an ensure-suppression idiom I could not verify blind.

Two design points worth carrying forward: the build epoch is read from the PUBLISHED field rather than
from the build state (beginning a build resets that state, so counting from it would restart at one on
every rebuild and every reader comparing epochs would conclude it was current), and publishing MOVES the
field out of the build state through a new `Request_ReleaseCompletedField` rather than copying it.

**Three red gates on this chunk**, all mine, all missing-declaration rather than design: the Jolt backend
needed `groundnav::` qualification from inside `namespace ck`; `CK_REGISTER_PROCESSOR` needed
`CkEcs/Scheduler/CkProcessorRegistration.h`; and the module doc's own quoting of the no-Jolt-header rule
tripped PHASE_1's fence grep, so it was rephrased.

### TRACKED NUMBERS — measured and pinned (2026-09-01, session 4)

Gate 65/65 (`Saved/Logs/P1-Reference-Gate.log`). Published: CkTests `5480455d` · root `3149ee1`.

PHASE_1's exit criteria require the measured collapse ratio and the reference probe count recorded as
TRACKED VALUES. They are pinned in the assertion (`Test_GroundNav_ReferenceNumbers.cpp`) rather than
written only here, because a number that lives solely in a document drifts from the code in silence.
A bake stage that changes what it produces for the same input now fails there first, with the whole set
named in the message.

**The reference scene** is defined in the test file, not taken from a gym or a level, so the numbers
mean the same thing on every machine and in every checkout: a 2400 uu floor, two dividing walls with a
gap between them, a raised platform that overhangs the floor, and a pillar in the open. 3x3 tiles at
800 uu, 25 uu cells, 200 uu clearance ceiling.

| Number | Value |
|---|---|
| Probes spent | **117,962** |
| Walkable cells | **9,792** |
| Plates | **22** |
| **Collapse ratio** | **445.09 cells/plate** |
| Within-tile portals | 6 |
| Seam portals | 17 |
| Reachability components | 5 |

**Identical across two runs in the same process**, which is the property that makes a probe count
assertable at all.

**The collapse ratio comfortably beats the in-house volumetric precedent** PHASE_1 names as the
comparison point (91,752 -> 359 = 255.6). Caveat stated rather than buried: this is a synthetic
fixture chosen to exercise every stage, not a real level, so it is evidence that the decomposition
works and NOT a prediction of what a shipped map will collapse to. PHASE_1 is explicit that a poor
ratio would be a PHASE_3 budget finding rather than a correctness failure; a good one is not a
performance claim either.

**Still owed from this criterion**: bake wall time and peak memory on a REFERENCE SCENE. Those need a
real level and a profiling pass, not a synthetic fixture, so they stay owed and are not faked from
this one.

### FIELD VIEW — the tiled bake made visible (2026-09-01, session 4)

Gated 63/63 (`Saved/Logs/P1-FieldView-Gate3.log`, AngelScript compiled clean in the same boot).
Published: CkFoundation `db15bd2c3` · CkTests `705436fc` · root `351a471`. Test count unchanged — this
is view code over already-tested products, and no new assertion would have been honest.

Motivation: nothing 1D produced was PIE-visible. The halo, the seam crossings and per-tile build status
are the three things a tiled bake gets wrong INVISIBLY, and none of them had a view.

- `ck.GroundNav.BakeFieldAt <X> <Y> <Z>` bakes the same scene as several tiles through the identical
  pipeline, so a field bake and a region bake of one scene are directly comparable.
- **Draw mode 5** shows the lattice and the seams. **Unbuilt tiles are drawn**, in their own colour and
  heavier, rather than omitted — showing only what built would make a place nothing is known about look
  exactly like a place with no floor.
- Summary gains `tiles : N of M built, S seams between them`.
- `ck.GroundNav.Debug.TileSizeUu` (default 800) so the tiling can be varied from the console.
- Gym: **Y** bakes the tuning range as a field. The row is APPENDED, not inserted — the panel indices
  are hand-maintained constants and inserting renumbers every row after it.

**The snapshot gained a second producer, not a second shape.** `Make_DebugSnapshotFromField` flattens a
field into the same cells/plates/crossings a region bake makes, so every existing draw mode works over a
tiled field unchanged, and tiles/seams are additions rather than a parallel type.

Ruled while writing it: the clearance ceiling for a DEBUG field bake is a quarter of the tile size
rather than a free knob, because a ceiling wider than a tile makes every halo swallow its neighbours
whole — precisely the configuration in which a seam cannot be wrong, and therefore the one this view
must not default to.

**[EDITOR-VERIFY] — owed by a human, and now the cheapest way to exercise everything 1D built:**
1. PIE the tuning range (Tab → "GroundNav Tuning Range"), press **R**, note the summary.
2. Press **Y**, then **T** to mode 5. Expect a lattice of blue tile boxes with thick seam lines on the
   shared edges, and the summary reporting all tiles built with a non-zero seam count.
3. Compare the two summaries. Plate and cell counts should be close; a wildly different walkable-cell
   count between R and Y would mean the tiled path disagrees with the region path.
4. Any RED tile box is a tile that failed to bake — report its position and the summary block.

Two self-inflicted errors worth recording, both from scripted edits rather than from the design: one
script re-read the file mid-way and silently discarded its own first edit, which shipped a declared-but-
undefined accessor and only surfaced at link time; another wedged a new declaration between an existing
doc comment and the function it documented. **A scripted edit needs its result read back, not just its
exit status.**

**1D-c design, settled ahead of writing it** (the shape [NN-D33] implies, recorded so a compaction
does not lose it): the tile bake collects a SEAM STUB per edge cell, layer and outward direction —
`{direction, along-index, plate, near surface Z, far surface Z, clearance}` — taken from the halo
connection field BEFORE the halo is masked away, with the plate index patched in after decomposition.
Composition pairs tile A's stub with tile B's stub at the same world position when each side's far
surface matches the other's near surface, which mirrors the mutual-agreement rule the connection pass
already enforces. Matched stubs with consecutive along-indices and the same plate pair run together
into a seam portal, exactly as within-tile crossings do. Seam portals live on the FIELD rather than on
either tile, because they depend on two tiles and must be re-derived whenever either rebuilds. Only the
two positive directions are composed, so a seam is never emitted twice. A neighbour that is Unbuilt
yields no seam portal at all — the boundary is a hard edge and a path across it fails as unbuilt, never
as blocked.

**[NN-D32] The halo is sized from a clearance CAP, and clearance saturates at it.** This is the
ruling [NN-D27] left owed, and it is the only shape found that makes a tiled bake agree EXACTLY with
a one-shot bake rather than within a tolerance.

The problem: the distance transform propagates without bound, so no finite halo makes an open field's
interior clearance correct at a seam. Two ways out. Truncating at the halo makes seam cells read
short — conservative for admission but silently different depending on how the world was tiled, which
is exactly the class of bug VALIDATION A3 calls "the one that silently breaks". Capping instead makes
every cell beyond the cap read the SAME value everywhere, so a tile baked alone and the same ground
baked as part of a bigger region produce identical numbers.

- The cap is a configured maximum clearance of interest. Below it the field is exact; at or above it
  every cell reads the cap.
- Halo width is `ceil(cap / cellSize)` cells. A tile cell whose true clearance is under the cap has
  its nearest blocked cell within that many cells, so the halo already contains it; anything further
  is clamped anyway. The chamfer metric only ever over-estimates Euclidean distance, so the bound
  holds in the direction that matters.
- Consequence a query must respect: a radius above the cap cannot be admitted on clearance alone.
  The field carries its cap so such a query refuses rather than silently mis-admitting.
- `DoCompute_Clearance` is NOT changed. The cap is applied by the tile bake after the transform,
  which is exactly equivalent given the halo, and leaves 1C's exact-agreement test untouched.

Rasterization, walkability, layers and clearance all run on the halo-expanded lattice; the crop to
the tile's own cells happens before plate decomposition, so no plate straddles a tile boundary.

`RejectsOffNavmeshCompiledDetour` passes with 2 direct verdicts, so its rejection is decided by the ribbon-containment check
(`:1824` / `:1954`), not by `Try_ResolveNavmeshSegment`. A verdict-agreement pass over this corpus therefore proves only the
direct branch; the detour/reject branches need a fixture authored to reach them before 5C can claim agreement there.

- **[NN-D100]** (maintainer + orchestrator, 2026-09-05, session 10) **The tuning-range gym splits into four gyms, one world
  each, with a live VERDICT row per station.** Maintainer's PIE walk of section 2.1 item 1 returned a FINDING, not a pass: the
  45-row panel (no scroll, already at the viewport edge at 1200 px) shows raw state and never says whether a station passed;
  the world is three disjoint sections (tuning scene at the origin, links deck north-west, the range slab 200 uu of void
  south with its five boards 400 uu east over void) spanning 7300 uu north-south, every surface the same grey, both slabs
  flush at Z 0, and the only way back is the V row buried at row 22. Read-only Opus research (session 10) found NO code
  trap (fly pawn, no bounds, no teleport-on-station, rows 1-7 steal no movement key; the maintainer confirmed the pawn
  stayed responsive) and that this gym is the only one in the corpus packing 7 stations, 2 volumes and 45 rows into one
  world - the house pattern is one registry entry per station on the base grid (CkCrowd 10, CkJolt 11). Ruling: split
  into **GroundNav Tuning Range** (scene, R/Y/T/B, tunables, P/O/V), **GroundNav Links** (deck, volume, U, resolution
  rows), **GroundNav Repair** (slab, obstacle nudge + repair, markup paint, walkers, provider row), **GroundNav Routing**
  (slab, ramp, crossing route + path draw, no-route pocket + probe); shared code (Jolt-baked box spawner, the
  volume-mint-and-settle poll the links and range stations duplicate, a fly-back aimed at the station's footprint anchor)
  moves to `Script/CkGroundNav/CkGroundNavGym_Common.as`; every gym sits on the base grid at the origin; each gym's panel
  opens with one VERDICT status row computed from live readbacks only (no new mirrored state - a criterion with no
  readback is a STOP, not a fake). No autotest references the gym (only `CkTests_GymRegistry.as:99`), so the split is
  one registry edit and no test change; gates = editor boot (AS compile) + `CkTestsGym` + a scoped `GroundNav` family; no
  full suite ([NN-D54]). VALIDATION 4.1 stays OPEN until the maintainer re-walks the four gyms.

- **[NN-D99]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **The escape pin stages its own Recast ground.** `P8-B1-Final3.log`: the
  polled wait never became true in 900 polls in the `GroundNav` family, while the same pin passed in the `Nav` family
  with the SAME predecessor — ambient level state, not a race: the Nav family's earlier fixtures leave Recast-walkable
  floor geometry near the outside point; the GroundNav family's floors are Jolt-only bodies Recast never sees. Ruling:
  the pin spawns a Recast-walkable static-mesh slab under the outside point the way the Recast fixtures do (thick enough
  to bake tiles), keeps the polled wait for the rebuild, and destroys the slab on every exit path; the slab stays clear
  of the field's bounds. A pin that depends on what the previous test left behind is not a pin.
  **Corrected by the unit's STOP:** the premise was wrong — the GroundNav fixture's floor IS the level's own `StaticMeshActor_1`
  (Recast bakes over it); the pin simply never asked for the navmesh build the two passing shadow siblings ask for
  (`Request_SurfaceRebuild_ForTesting` + `Request_NavigationRebuild_ForTesting`), and its outside point (+900) sat inside
  Recast's rim erosion of the ±1000 bounds volume. Ruling: the kick step is added before the polled wait and the point moves
  to +750 (250 uu clear of both the field and the rim). No slab.

- **[NN-D98]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **The escape pin's staging is polled, not asserted.** Finals rerun
  `P8-B1-Final2*.log`: `GroundNav` 407/406 with `Shadow_ContainmentEscapeIsCounted` red at staging ("X=900 is not on the
  level's Recast navmesh") while the SAME pin passed in the Nav leg with the counter moving then going quiet — an order
  race: the level's dynamic navmesh is still rebuilding after the previous fixture's teardown when the pin first
  projects. Ruling: a `WaitUntil` step (`Check_OutsidePointOnRecast`, the settle budget) polls the Recast projection at
  the outside point before the agent is parked; the park step keeps its projection. `Crowd` 136/133 (baseline three),
  `Nav` 570/568 (baseline pair), `CrowdDebugger` 26/26 stand on this artifact; `GroundNav` + `Nav` rerun test-only as
  `P8-B1-Final3*.log`.

- **[NN-D97]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **P8-1 Final 1's two new reds ruled.** `P8-B1-Final1.log` `GroundNav`
  407/406 and `-Nav.log` 570/566: (1) `NavSurfaceAreaPolicy.ImpassableIsTheOnlyWalkabilityArea` (a standing PHASE_4 rule
  pin) went red because [NN-D96] B3 registered the crowd's `HardExclude` as a Walkability policy — the review's premise
  was wrong: on Recast `UCk_NavArea_CrowdAvoidanceVolume_HardExclude` is `DefaultCost = 16384` (prohibitive, NOT excluded),
  so parity is a COST policy on both providers; B3 reverted (`DoRegister_CostPolicy` off the class), its pin deleted, the
  rule pin stands; site 4's route refusal comes from the impassable boxes its fixture stages, not from HardExclude. (2)
  `GroundNav_Shadow_ContainmentEscapeIsCounted` failed staging: the origin field's default half-extent (1000) IS the
  level's navmesh extent, so X=1400 was off Recast ground too → the pin stages a 500 half-extent field and parks the agent
  at +900, on Recast ground outside the field. Both fixed by the orchestrator; finals rerun `P8-B1-Final2{,-Crowd,-Nav,-CrowdDebugger}.log`.

- **[NN-D96]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **PHASE_8 batch 1 review ruled** (read-only Opus; full brief
  `scratchpad/brief_P8_Batch1_Fix.md`). U4 HIGH ×2: the confirmation probes still asked Recast so `_ConfirmedOnMesh` could
  never flip on GroundNav (R2a mandatory, both sites → `Get_IsMarkupLive`); a paint whose bounds meet no GroundNav volume
  raised `CK_ENSURE_IF_NOT(AnyVolumeTookIt)` — an ensure storm under every stationary agent outside coverage → the adapter
  refuses QUIETLY (no-surface result, `Get_IsMarkupLive` false, no ensure). MED: the avoidance volume's `HardExclude` tag
  registered a COST policy so a hard exclusion was merely expensive on GroundNav → the walkability-rejection kind; the
  orphaned `Get_NavAreaClass` deleted. U1: link-traversing agents excluded from the escape check; the shadow-mode read
  hoisted per tick (the shadow-off path paid a lock per agent per frame); a one-time line when the pairing cannot be
  counted (post-promotion the counter reads 0 by construction — documented); the counter is report-only (the
  `[SHADOW-CMP]` line does not carry it — documented); the status sweep gets a cardinality `static_assert`; the
  NoProvider-outside-coverage split is the counter's honest reading until the whole-band field lands. U-Strip: the
  `DrawMarkup` cvar + `Get_IsMarkupDrawEnabled` join the guard; the four label helpers fold in. U-Tools: the offline table
  is NOT the engine's schema=1 (no `partial_disagree`/`containment_escapes`/`len_delta_rel`, nearest-rank p95) → relabelled
  `offline-aggregate=1`; a committed expected file makes `--check` exercisable; the dual-defined `Nav.Filter.Customer`
  (native + the uncommitted ini line) has NO in-repo precedent → the gate log's `LogGameplayTags` is checked before the
  gate is trusted. Both `Source/CkCrowd/Claude.md` paragraphs present.

- **[NN-D94]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **U1's pin STOPs ruled (R1a).** The producer landed (the constrain pass
  reconstructs the resolved position from the queued offset, gates on the world's shadow mode before any projection,
  projects on both providers with the constraint's own search box, banks a split verdict through the shadow-compare

- **[NN-D77]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **F2.8 6b review ruled** (Gate 1 `P5-5F-6b-Gate1.log`: build clean, `GroundNav
  --discover-fresh` 353/353 = 348 + 4 C++ pins + the AS pin, the Iris ensure; Opus read-only review, 12 findings; full text
  `scratchpad/rulings_6b_review.md`). R1 (HIGH, blocked the ship): the invalidator resolved a corridor's field with the
  two-argument `TryGet_Field` — the default — so a variant-only publish never invalidated a variant-planned path (the
  crawler kept walking ground its own field no longer allowed until the DEFAULT moved). Ruling: the profile tag rides
  `FFragment_GroundNavPath_Current` and every lookup in `Path/` uses the three-argument form; the exactness predicate then
  falls to bounds for a variant-only change as [NN-D76](2) intended. Rejected: bumping the default's epoch when a variant
  moves. R2: `Publish` takes the variant map under ONE lock; `Publish_Variants` deleted (no shims). R3: the merged publish
  retires dropped/replaced variant sums so the revision never falls. R4: `StartBuild`'s two failure blocks fail the riding
  requests (the takeover never strands a caller). R5: fields-vs-variants count is ensured, a mismatch fails the build
  whole. R6: variant profiles pass the default's validity check at admission. R7: the build state snapshots the variant
  tag list at start. R8: `Get_BuildEpoch` keeps the newest-epoch meaning, doc says so. R9: four tests owed (repair→build
  takeover incl. R4, variant-only derive publishes newest epoch + union bounds, variant-planned path invalidated by a
  variant-only change, revision never falls). R10: five audit edits applied verbatim, the registry-header edit superseded
  by R2. Executor deviations kept: `Get_VariantRevision` (the reviewer confirmed `Get_Fields` is default-only, so
  without it a variant-only publish would not move the world revision), per-field epoch compare (testing against the
  fragment's max would silently suppress a default-only change after any variant moved), the bare-world
  `ForEachEntity` idiom (established in four sibling tests). Filed, not now: debug draw resolves default fields only
  (a variant's walkable set cannot be visualised; `ck.GroundNav.DrawProfile <tag>` is the follow-up).

- **[NN-D76]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **F2.8 6b's two STOPs ruled.** (1) The local repair is single-field
  (`Field/CkGroundNav_FieldRepair.h:163-165`, four singular entry points) and making it N-profile is 6a repeated; shipping
  variants without it would leave the default describing the world as it is and every variant as it was — the split-brain
  the build state's own admission rule forbids. Ruling: **a repair raised on a volume that holds variants converts to a
  full rebuild** (one flag at `FProcessor_GroundNavVolume_StartRepair`: the pending box and parked requests are taken over
  by a `Request_Build`, exactly the takeover a build start already performs), honest and bounded; a multi-profile repair
  (`Request_BeginRepair_MultiProfile`, mirroring 6a) is filed as the follow-up that must land before any project authors a
  second profile — which is itself a CTO decision ([NN-D7]). (2) The derives' publish gate: **any field moving publishes.**
  Each field (default and every variant) keeps its own epoch; the fragment's `_Epoch` is the NEWEST across them; the
  notify carries the UNION of `Get_ChangedTileBounds` over every field that moved; `InBuiltField._Epoch == _Field->_Epoch`
  relaxes to `>=`, so the invalidator's exactness predicate (`Note._Epoch == Field->_Epoch`) falls to the bounds floor for
  a variant-only change — conservative and correct — and the `RevisionWatch` sees the newest epoch. A variant-only change is
  never left unpublished.
