- **[NN-D73]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **5B forks ruled on `scratchpad/design_5B_LinkRuntimeState.md`** (header-verified, 8 forks, 18 findings; mirrored in `scratchpad/rulings_5B.md`):

Paths from the CkGroundNav module root (`Plugins/CkFoundation/Source/CkGroundNav/Public/CkGroundNav/`) unless written in full.
[NN-D71] stands in full; 5B builds on the landed 5A shapes.

- **(F1) The 5A derive IS the runtime-state mechanism (A).** No per-world overlay: two sources of truth for "is this link
  on" is the split-brain the module already refuses for crossing keys and plate pricing, and the labels would go stale.
  F2.2's "no epoch bump" is superseded (Fi-a): the endpoint-tile epoch bump is the channel F1.34 reads and the reason a
  toggle invalidates anything. 5B adds the API gaps only: `Request_ReleaseLink_ById(volume, id)` and
  `Request_ReleaseAllLinks(volume)` (bulk by volume), `Request_LinkBatch(volume, TArray<FCk_Request_GroundNavVolume_Link>)`
  completing ONCE when every entry has been admitted (one delegate, `Failed` if any entry was refused — atomic admission,
  nothing applied), and a per-link end-status read `Get_LinkResolution(volume, id) -> FCk_GroundNav_LinkResolution`
  (reflected value: both end statuses, both flat plates, resolved yes/no, live yes/no). A toggle stays a `Request_Link`
  update with `_Enable` flipped. The batch is one derive by construction (§1c).
- **(F2) Metadata: the STABLE `_Id`, GroundNav-only (A), with `_LinkIndex` NOT carried.** A reflected parallel array
  `TArray<FCk_GroundNavPath_LinkWaypoint> _LinkWaypoints` on `FCk_GroundNavPath_Result` — `{int32 _WaypointIndex; int32
  _LinkId; ECk_GroundNavPath_LinkWaypointRole _Role (Entry|Exit); ECk_GroundNav_LinkDirection _EntryDirection; float
  _DistanceFromStartUu}` — produced by two fields on the internal `FCk_GroundNav_PathWaypoint` stamped where the pinned
  point is recognised (the exact-equality rule F14a already uses; the ONE funnel untouched), surviving skip-first and
  corner offset because they ride per waypoint, collapsed into the array at the flatten in `DoPublish_Success`.
  `_Waypoints` is byte-identical by construction (the pin asserts `TArray<FVector>` equality against a reference path).
  The crowd's install stamps the array onto the agent (Unit D-crowd). The neutral `FCk_Nav_PathResult` is NOT touched in
  5B — extending it is filed on the P8 promotion list beside [P5-B1] (a consumer that only speaks the neutral result has
  no link metadata until then; the crowd today reads the GroundNav result at its install seam).
- **(F3) Link queries are free functions over the metadata array (C)** in `Path/CkGroundNavPath_Utils.{h,cpp}`:
  `Get_LinksOnPath(result) -> TArray<FCk_GroundNavPath_LinkSpan>` (entry/exit pairs by id in walk order, with both
  distances) and `TryGet_NextLinkBeyond(result, distanceUu) -> FCk_GroundNavPath_LinkSpan` (invalid id = none), with
  `UFUNCTION(BlueprintPure)` wrappers taking the path handle. The distance is the carried `_DistanceFromStartUu` — never
  a second polyline integration.
- **(F4, F5, F6) The handshake is consumer state on the TRAVERSING entity, with NO provider-table capability (A/A/A).**
  Completing a traversal changes nothing geometric, so the provider is not consulted and the table stays at 14. In
  CkNavigation (neutral, so any consumer and any future provider share it): `FCk_Request_NavSurface_BeginLinkTraversal
  {int32 _LinkId; int32 _CorrelatorId; ECk_GroundNav_LinkDirection... -> a neutral ECk_NavSurface_LinkEntryDirection
  {Forward, Backward}}` and `FCk_Request_NavSurface_CompleteLinkTraversal {int32 _CorrelatorId}` on the HANDLE
  (PHASE_5's own signature: `Request_BeginLinkTraversal(UPARAM(ref) FCk_Handle& InHandle, const FCk_Request_..., const
  FCk_Delegate_Request_OnCompleted& InDelegate)` — the delegate LAST, no default), a `FFragment_NavSurface_LinkTraversal_Current
  {_ActiveLinkId, _ActiveCorrelatorId, _EntryDirection, _State}` composed on first use, a `_Requests` fragment drained on
  the game thread by a CkNavigation processor with `MakeCompletionGuard`, signals `OnLinkTraversalBegun` /
  `OnLinkTraversalCompleted` (payload: handle, link id, correlator) for game code (ladder animation, gating), an EndPlay
  canceller (`Failed_Cancelled` for anything pending), and `Request_CancelLinkTraversal(handle, correlator)` completing the
  active traversal `Failed_Cancelled`. Exactly-once from the guard; a begin naming the ACTIVE correlator completes
  `Succeeded` and does nothing; a complete for a correlator that is not active completes `Failed`; a begin while another
  correlator is active completes `Failed` (one link at a time). On the crowd agent: a NON-exclusive
  `FTag_CrowdAgent_TraversingLink` beside `Flying`/`Permeable` (the Idle/PathPending/Walking triple and the F1.34 pin's
  `nonWalkingFrames=0` are untouched) plus `_ActiveLinkId`/`_ActiveLinkCorrelator` on `FCk_Fragment_CrowdAgent_PathFollowData`;
  the waypoint cursor issues Begin when it advances ONTO an Entry waypoint and Complete when it advances PAST the
  matching Exit (the correlator = the agent's `_PathSerial` combined with the link id, so a route swap cancels the old
  traversal through the EndPlay/abandon path); the install stamps the metadata array from the GroundNav result. No
  second install path: nothing here writes `_Waypoints` or the status.
- **(F7) The veto rides the GroundNav path REQUEST (A), id- and tag-shaped, deny-set + cost-map (no sentinel).**
  `FCk_Request_GroundNavPath_FindPath` gains `TSet<int32> _DeniedLinkIds`, `FGameplayTagContainer _DeniedLinkUserTypeTags`
  (a link whose `_UserTypeTag` matches any is denied — "this agent cannot use ladders"), and `TMap<int32, float>
  _LinkCostMultipliers` (per-query multiplier REPLACING the authored one for that id; refused below 1.0 at the request
  boundary with `Failed_NotEnqueued`, the F13 admissibility argument). Threaded into `FCk_GroundNav_PathCostParams` beside
  `_PlateCostMultipliers` and onto the shared seed; DENY is applied in `Neighbors` (the crossing is skipped, no node
  minted); the cost rewrite is applied at `Cost`'s one call site of `Get_LinkTraversalCost` (Fi-g — do not change that
  function's signature). The crowd's per-agent params (`FCk_Fragment_CrowdAgent_ParamsData` or its nav params — the
  executor finds the struct the crowd's `Add` reads) gain the same three fields, copied onto the request at the GroundNav
  dispatch (`CkCrowdAgent_HandleRequests_Processor.cpp:~305-308`); the Recast branch ignores them (recorded seam gap).
  The neutral `FCk_Nav_QueryFilterOverlay` is NOT extended (an `int32` id in a tag-keyed overlay; the seam the crowd
  closed stays closed) — FEATURE_MATRIX F2.2's "part of the compiled filter" is read as "at the point plate-edge filters
  run", which `Neighbors` is.
- **(F7a — ruled on Unit C's STOP, 2026-09-04)** The crowd's veto carrier is `FCk_Fragment_CrowdAgent_ParamsData`
  (`CkCrowdAgent_Fragment_Data.h:158`, the `InParams` the dispatch already reads at `HandleRequests_Processor.cpp:297-298`;
  the `FCk_Fragment_GroundNavPath_ParamsData` the crowd builds from radius alone has no authoring seam), and the copy is
  made at BOTH GroundNav dispatch sites — the primary (`:~305-308`) and the Recast-shadow one (`:~381-384`) — so the shadow
  comparison stays like-for-like. Applied by the orchestrator after Unit D-crowd releases the file. Unit C's deviations
  accepted (a request-aware `Get_CostParams` overload; the seed copy in `PathSearch.cpp`; `HasTag` on the single
  `_UserTypeTag` so a parent tag denies its children; `Get_LinkSpanUu` shared by the authored and the rewritten price).

- **(F8) Invalidation is BOTH (C): bounds as the floor, exact by link identity for a link-only publish.** The path caches
  `_LastCorridorLinkIds` (stable ids resolved against the plan's own field, beside `_LastCorridorKeys`);
  `Get_ChangedLinkEndTiles` also returns the changed stable ids; the link derive publishes a GroundNav-side SIDECAR on the
  world-field registry entry (`{epoch, changed link ids, link-only = true}`) beside the neutral rebuild box (the neutral
  queue is NOT widened); `FProcessor_GroundNavPath_InvalidateOnRebuilt` — already provider-specific — reads the sidecar:
  for a link-only publish a corridor is flagged only when its cached ids intersect the changed ids; any other publish flags
  by bounds as today. The Layer-2 pin then holds by construction: the agent whose path uses the disabled link replans
  exactly once, the agent on an unrelated path through the same tile does not.
- **(F8a — ruled on Unit E's finding, 2026-09-04)** A per-entry note that says only "the newest publish was link-only"
  is unsound when a repair and a link derive publish in one tick (the queue then holds the repair's box under a
  link-only note) and when two toggles land before an agent replans. Ruling: `FCk_GroundNav_PublishNote` describes the
  RUN of link-only publishes since the last geometry publish — `{_Epoch (newest), _LastGeometryEpoch (the epoch of the
  last build/repair publish), _ChangedLinkIdsSinceGeometry (accumulated over the run, sorted)}`; a build or repair
  publish resets the accumulation and stamps `_LastGeometryEpoch` with its own epoch; a link derive appends its changed
  ids. The invalidator narrows a corridor ONLY when `corridorEpoch >= _LastGeometryEpoch` (every publish since the plan
  was link-only), flagging iff its cached ids intersect the accumulated ids; otherwise the bounds floor. The same-tick
  repair + derive case falls to bounds (corridor epoch < the repair's), the double-toggle case stays exact. Unit E's
  deviations accepted (note looked up by location through the one entry selector; a defaulted `Publish` parameter;
  conservative narrowing predicate; sorted ids).

- **(F6a, F8b — ruled on Unit F's STOPs, 2026-09-04)** (F6a) The crowd had no link LOCOMOTION: `FProcessor_CrowdAgent_ConstrainToNavmesh`
  routes every grounded frame through `Try_MoveAlongSurface`, whose contract is to stay ON the walkable set, so a body
  aimed at a link's exit stays pinned at its entry and the crossing never completes. Ruling: while
  `FTag_CrowdAgent_TraversingLink` is set the constraint applies the steered displacement as-is (the ladder/drop/jump
  segment is not walkable ground by definition) and marks the body on-mesh; the constraint resumes the frame the cursor
  walks off the exit and the crossing completes. One Transform writer per agent is preserved (the branch lives inside
  the constraint, beside its `Disabled`-mode branch). Applied by the orchestrator; pin 1's withheld assertions (Completed
  `Succeeded` exactly once, not traversing afterwards) are re-added. Animation-driven pacing of a crossing is game code's,
  reached through the two signals. (F8b) Re-enabling a link does NOT repath agents that detoured around it — their
  corridors carry no link id and exact invalidation narrows the re-enable away; a consumer that wants the shortcut back
  issues its own replan. Recorded as a limitation in the module doc; pin 2 asserts the disable half only. Unit F's
  dispositions accepted (no gym veto toggle — the station owns no agent; resolution rows below the keyed toggle;
  replans counted per agent by `OnPathReady`).

- **(F14c — ruled on the veto pin's diagnosis, 2026-09-04)** A link whose two endpoints are COLLINEAR with the route (start,
  entry, exit, goal on one line) is crossed by the search but the funnel (`Do_Funnel`, a stock simple stupid funnel) emits
  an apex only where the funnel closes — a degenerate portal on the apex-to-goal line closes nothing — so neither endpoint
  became a waypoint, the exact-equality stamp matched nothing, and `Get_LinksOnPath` answered 0 on a route that DID cross
  (`P5-5B-Final1.log`: the allowed agent published 2 waypoints / 3 expansions). That breaks 5A's contract ("the funnel
  emits the link's start and end as distinct consecutive waypoints and never interpolates across it") and would leave the
  crowd's handshake silent on such a crossing. Ruling: after the string pull, the post-process INSERTS every link portal
  point the funnel did not emit into the polyline at the segment it lies on (exact points, in corridor order, entry before
  exit), before the corner offset and the stamp; the ONE funnel stays untouched. Layer-1 pin
  `LinkSearch.CollinearLinkStillEmitsBothEndpoints`. The veto pin's lane is ALSO moved off the link's line (a fixture should
  not lean on the fix), and the handshake pin re-targets to a different goal because a same-goal `Request_MoveTo` is the
  crowd's documented no-op (`HandleRequests_Processor.cpp:203-210`) — no route swap, no cancel.

- **Multi-world:** every new piece of state is on an entity (the traversal fragment on the traverser, the veto on the
  request, the ids on the path) or on the world-keyed registry entry (the sidecar, dropped with the entry) — nothing
  process-wide.
- **Units and order:** Batch 1 (parallel, disjoint): A (Volume/* state API + `LinkState.*` tests), B (metadata + queries:
  `Search/CkGroundNav_PathPostProcess.{h,cpp}`, `Path/CkGroundNavPath_Fragment_Data.h` + `_Utils.{h,cpp}` +
  `_Processor.cpp` `DoPublish_Success` only, `PathLinkMetadata.*` tests), D-transport (CkNavigation handshake +
  `NavSurface.LinkTraversal.*` tests). Gate. Batch 2 (parallel, disjoint): C (veto: `Search/*`, the request carrier in
  `Path/CkGroundNavPath_Fragment_Data.h` + `Get_CostParams` in `Path/CkGroundNavPath_Processor.cpp`, the crowd dispatch
  copy in `CkCrowdAgent_HandleRequests_Processor.cpp` + the crowd params struct, `LinkVeto.*` tests), E (invalidation:
  `Path/CkGroundNavPath_Invalidate_Processor.*`, `Path/CkGroundNavPath_Fragment.h`, `Field/CkGroundNav_FieldLinks.*`,
  `Facade/CkGroundNav_WorldFieldRegistry.*` sidecar, the LinkDerive notify in `Volume/CkGroundNavVolume_Processor.cpp`,
  `PathInvalidation.*` tests), D-crowd (crowd tag/fields, the waypoint-cursor Begin/Complete, the install stamp —
  `CkCrowdAgent_Fragment*.h`, `CkCrowdAgent_OnGroundNavPathResolved_Processor.*`, the follow/steer processor that
  advances `_WaypointIndex`; NEVER `HandleRequests_Processor.cpp`, which is C's; `CrowdAgent.LinkTraversal.*` tests).
  Gate. Then F (two AS pins: `Link_TraversalHandshakeFiresExactlyOnce`, `Link_DisableReplansOnlyTheAgentsUsingIt`; the
  Links station gains a veto toggle; the module doc), the comment audit, the finals (`GroundNav`, `Nav`, `Crowd`).

Findings accepted: Fi-a..Fi-r as recorded in the design doc (§11); Fi-e — CkCrowd has no off-mesh-link handling today, so
the crowd half of step 4 is greenfield and is designed above; Fi-m — MIGRATION_SEAM's "twelve" is stale (fourteen).



- **[NN-D71]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-04, session 9) **5A forks ruled on `scratchpad/design_5A_NavLinks.md`** (the
  design pass is header-verified, 17 forks, 12 findings; the ruling text is mirrored in `scratchpad/rulings_5A.md`):

Paths from the CkGroundNav module root (`Plugins/CkFoundation/Source/CkGroundNav/Public/CkGroundNav/`) unless written in full.

- **(F1) The link record is a reflected `USTRUCT(BlueprintType)` at global scope**, `FCk_GroundNav_LinkRecord` in a new
  `Bake/CkGroundNav_LinkTypes.h`, copying `Bake/CkGroundNav_MarkupTypes.h`'s idioms verbatim (GENERATED_BODY + CK_GENERATED_BODY,
  private UPROPERTYs with AllowPrivateAccess, `int32 _Id = INDEX_NONE`, `ECk_EnableDisable _Enable`, epoch as a bare `int64`
  with the same comment, CK_PROPERTY_GET for identity / CK_PROPERTY for the mutable ones, CK_DEFINE_CONSTRUCTORS on the
  essentials, `CK_DEFINE_CUSTOM_FORMATTER_ENUM` on `ECk_GroundNav_LinkDirection`). PHASE_5's plain-`ck::` sketch is
  superseded (Fi1 resolved: three environments need reflection). The DERIVED resolution is a plain `ck::groundnav` value
  struct with a defaulted `operator==`, never reflected.
- **(F2, F4) Two values, markup-shaped.** The AUTHORED record lives on the volume (`FFragment_GroundNavVolume_Links`, one
  `FCk_GroundNav_LinkEntry {FCk_Handle _LinkEntity; FCk_GroundNav_LinkRecord _Record;}` per link, `_NextId` monotone and
  never reused) and is copied by value into `FCk_GroundNav_FieldParams::_Links` beside `_MarkupRecords` at every build,
  repair and derive (so a plain `Request_Build` never un-links the world, exactly as it never unpaints it). The RESOLVED
  output is a field-level array `FCk_GroundNav_Field::_ResolvedLinks` beside `_SeamPortals` — one `FCk_GroundNav_ResolvedLink`
  per authored record, in authored-id order, carrying: the stable `_Id`, `_Start`/`_End` world points, per-end
  `FCk_GroundNav_SurfaceRef` + `int32` flat plate (INDEX_NONE when unresolved), per-end `ECk_GroundNav_QueryStatus`-shaped
  resolution status, `_Direction`, the two multipliers, `_ClearanceUu`, `_AreaTag`, `_Enable`. Neither a per-tile array nor
  a portal kind (design §1b/§1c: a re-baked tile would lose them and the byte-identity pin would become a claim about
  injection order). The field-level array is re-derived wholesale on every publish, like the seam portals.
- **(F2b — ruled on Unit A's second STOP, 2026-09-04)** `Get_ProjectPoint` (`Query/CkGroundNav_Query_Projection.h:44-47`) and
  `Get_FlatPlateIndex` (`Query/CkGroundNav_Query_Reachability.h:20-24`) are `Query/`-level, and no `Field/` file — header OR
  implementation — includes `Query/`. Resolving an authored link IS a projection over the field being composed, and the three
  composition points are `Field/`-level. Ruling: **`Field/CkGroundNav_FieldLinks.h` declares the resolver with no `Query/`
  include; `Field/CkGroundNav_FieldLinks.cpp` is the ONE implementation file under `Field/` that includes `Query/`**
  (`CkGroundNav_Query_Projection.h`, `CkGroundNav_Query_Reachability.h`), with a file-head comment stating why (a link endpoint
  is a projection query; the header layering — no `Field/` HEADER includes `Query/` — is untouched, so no header cycle and no
  UHT ordering change) and one sentence in the module doc's layering statement. Rejected: moving projection + `QueryCore` down
  to `Field/` (a 20-file move for no functional gain), a resolver callback injected into the composers (indirection for one
  caller), a second projection (forbidden). Unit A's deviations accepted: links hashed in list order (ids monotone, the list
  IS ascending-id order — documented in `Fingerprint.h`), `_RequestedAtEpoch` not hashed (content only, the markup precedent),
  `Get_ContentFingerprint`'s 6th parameter defaulted (no non-test caller), per-end status defaulting to `NoSurface`.

- **(F3) Identity is two-part.** The AUTHORING id is stable, volume-scoped, monotone, never reused (the markup rule,
  `Volume/CkGroundNavVolume_Fragment.h:263-265`); it is what 5B's overlay and every request key on. Staleness is a property
  of the FIELD a resolution lives in, not of the id: a `_ResolvedLinks` entry (and the `_LinkIndex` a crossing carries) is
  valid only for the field it was derived on, exactly like a flat plate id (`Field/CkGroundNav_Field.h:172-175`). PHASE_5's
  epoch-bearing `FCk_GroundNav_LinkId` is superseded (Fi3 resolved); no `FCk_GroundNav_LinkId` type is minted.
- **(F5) No `FCk_GroundNav_PlateId`.** Resolved plates are `int32` flat plate indices (`Query/CkGroundNav_Query_Reachability.h:20-24`)
  named `_StartFlatPlate` / `_EndFlatPlate`, beside the per-end `FCk_GroundNav_SurfaceRef`.
- **(F6) Projection parameters live on the record, one set for both ends:** `_ProjectionMode` (default `Closest`),
  `_ProjectionHorizontalExtentUu` (default 50), `_ProjectionVerticalExtentUu` (default 100, used for both up and down).
  Closest with a vertical extent covers a ledge top, a drop's floor and a ladder's foot; no new projection mode.
- **(F7) Up vectors do not ship in 5A.** No consumer; nothing is persisted yet, so 5B adding them costs a shape change and
  no migration.
- **(F8) A link is volume-scoped; both endpoints must lie inside the volume's world bounds.** A request whose endpoint lies
  outside completes `Failed` at admission with one `Display` line. Cross-volume links are unrepresentable until something
  composes two fields — recorded for P8's promotion list, not built here. Authoring is CkGroundNav-volume-only in 5A: the
  provider table gains NO link capability (Fi7); the neutral link request is owed at the seam before promotion and is
  filed with the 5C FORK-1 question ([P5-B1]).
- **(F9) A link change is a DERIVE (S1):** admission raises `FTag_GroundNavVolume_LinksDirty`; `FProcessor_GroundNavVolume_LinkDerive`
  (same group as `MarkupCostDerive`, with an explicit dependency edge so the cost derive runs first when both are owed)
  calls `Get_FieldWithLinks`: copy the published field, re-resolve every record in `_Params._Links` through F1.13
  projection, re-run `DoLabel_Reachability`, swap the pointer through the same publish. Honest property: ZERO geometry
  probes (projection reads the published field's cells, never the backend) and a bounded number of cell reads; it does NOT
  claim the cost derive's pinned zero-CELL-read property. A volume with nothing published clears the tag and waits — the
  next build resolves the records it holds (a link authored before the first bake is never lost). A build and a repair
  resolve links themselves, between `DoDerive_SeamPortals` and `DoLabel_Reachability` (`Field/CkGroundNav_FieldBuild.cpp:140`,
  `Field/CkGroundNav_FieldRepair.cpp:253-254`, `Field/CkGroundNav_Field.cpp:877`). `Get_IsSettled` gains the `LinksDirty`
  clause and the link request queue (Fi11); the provider table's `_IsSurfaceSettled` fold inherits it. Epochs on a link derive: for every link
  whose resolved entry CHANGED against the published field's entry of the same id (added, removed, enabled/disabled, or
  resolved differently), the tiles holding its two resolved ends bump their epoch (and the field's), so F1.34's
  invalidation-by-bounds reaches corridors through those tiles — over-invalidation accepted in 5A, refined by 5B; an
  unchanged link moves nothing. Reachability's union is undirected: a Forward-only link still joins the two labels
  (labels promise only that a DIFFERENT label is provably unreachable).
- **(F10) An endpoint over unbaked ground is HELD, never dropped:** its end status is `Unbuilt`, the link contributes no
  crossing and no label, and the next publish re-resolves it. Only `NoSurface` / `Blocked` make a link UNRESOLVED; even
  then the record stays on the volume (authored data is never lost) and only the field's graph drops it.
  `FCk_GroundNav_Field::_UnresolvedLinkCount` (modelled on `_UnmatchedSeamStubCount`) counts links with at least one end
  not `Success`, and reads exactly one in the hole pin.
- **(F11) A dropped link never warns.** It is a status: the count, the per-end status in the resolved entry, the snapshot,
  a red draw, the console command, and `Get_IsLinkLive` false. One `Display` line per publish when the count is non-zero.
- **(F12) Admission is by authored clearance, through `Get_IsAdmitted` unchanged:** `_ClearanceUu` on the record, default
  `FCk_GroundNav_LinkRecord::kAdmitsAnyAgentClearanceUu` (a named large constant), narrowed by the author for a ladder or
  a crawl. No exemption path.
- **(F13) Per-direction cost is a MULTIPLIER ≥ 1 on the link's Euclidean span**, priced through `Get_LegCost`'s
  factor machinery, so every edge still costs at least its length and `DoGet_HeuristicTo_Goal` stays admissible at w = 1
  with no change. Admission refuses a multiplier below 1.0 (`Failed`). The crossover test is analytic: the link is taken
  when span × multiplier beats the detour length. A link cheaper than its own straight-line span (a teleporter) is out of
  generation-1 scope and is recorded as a Tier-3 candidate, not built. The 5A→5B gate's concept fence is untouched.
- **(F14) `FCk_GroundNav_Crossing` gains `int32 _LinkIndex = INDEX_NONE`** (index into `_ResolvedLinks`; a lattice crossing
  carries INDEX_NONE). `Make_CrossingKey` hashes it and `Get_IsCrossingSettled` compares it. For a link crossing
  `_Left == _Right ==` the ENTRY endpoint and `_ClearanceUu` is the record's; the graph reads the exit endpoint and the
  direction's multiplier from the resolved entry. A bidirectional link emits one crossing from each end plate; a
  one-directional link emits only start→end. The node model gains an arrival point and a departure point (equal for a
  lattice crossing, start/end for a link); `Cost(From, To)` adds `span × multiplier` when From is a link node and prices
  the on-plate leg from From's departure point to To's arrival point; the heuristic reads the departure point. The
  back-plate skip in `Neighbors` does not apply to link crossings (a ladder beside a ramp is a different route between
  the same two plates) — pinned. `DoBuild_Corridor` pushes TWO consecutive degenerate funnel portals (entry, exit) for a
  link crossing so the ONE funnel bends at both without a second implementation (`Query/CkGroundNav_Funnel.*` untouched);
  `Get_CornerOffset` exempts link endpoints.
- **(F14a, F14b — ruled on Unit B's STOPs, 2026-09-04)** (F14a) `FCk_GroundNav_FunnelPortal` is declared in the fenced
  `Query/CkGroundNav_Funnel.h:18-23`. The fence protects the ALGORITHM (one string-pull, `Get_StringPull` untouched); the portal
  VALUE gaining an inert `int32 _LinkIndex = INDEX_NONE` the funnel never reads is a data-shape change, and it is ruled in — the
  one edit `Funnel.h` takes. Rejected: a parallel index array on the result (`SearchTypes.h:158-163` argues against), a second
  corridor-portal type converted at the call (a copy per query). The corner-offset exemption: `Get_CornerOffset` (`PathPostProcess.h:124-130`)
  gains a `TConstArrayView<FVector> InPinnedWaypoints` — the entry and exit points of every link portal in the corridor — and a
  waypoint EXACTLY equal to a pinned point (both copies of the same resolved entry, so exact comparison, the closed-square rule's
  reasoning) is emitted where it is. (F14b) A link's traverse `span × multiplier` is charged on the INCOMING edge: `Cost(From, To)`
  adds To's traverse (the multiplier for To's entry direction) when To is a link node, and the on-plate leg runs From's departure
  point → To's ENTRY point as before; a link node's g therefore already holds its traverse, the heuristic reads its departure
  point, and `DoGet_FinalLegCost` / the partial-corridor extraction price only the leg from the departure point. This is the
  consistent shape (h(prev) ≤ leg + span + h(link) by the triangle inequality); charging on the outgoing edge was inconsistent
  and left a goal-terminating link unpriced (`PathSearch.cpp:500` + `IsGoal` at `PlatePortalGraph.cpp:274`). Unit B's deviations
  accepted: `Get_TransitionPoint` → `Get_DeparturePoint`; `Path/CkGroundNavPath_Processor.cpp:153-161`'s hand-rolled crossing key
  replaced by `Make_CrossingKey` (the header already said there is one place that decides it).

- **(F15) Reachability labels reflect ENABLED links at the publish that resolved them.** `_Enable` is authored state on the
  record (as for markup); disabling raises the link derive, which excludes the link from crossings and from the union
  pass and republishes — so F2.1's "a disabled link is invisible to search and to reachability" holds in 5A through that
  republish. Whether 5B layers a cheaper per-world overlay on top, and how a link toggle invalidates exactly the paths
  that use it, is 5B's design question (Fi2, Fi9 re-examined there; FEATURE_MATRIX F2.2's "no epoch bump" is read
  against this ruling at 5B entry).
- **(F8a, F9a, F15a — ruled on Unit C's STOPs, 2026-09-04)** (F8a) Link admission refuses through `CK_ENSURE_IF_NOT` +
  completion `Failed`, the markup drain's own idiom (`Processor.cpp:366-373,379-386,419-427` has no Display rejection path);
  F8's "one Display line" is amended to that — an endpoint outside the volume is an authoring error and is loud. (F9a) The link
  derive's wait: on `NeedsBuild` (armed, not started) it clears `LinksDirty` and waits, because `StartBuild` snapshots the
  volume's CURRENT link entries; while `BuildInProgress`, `NeedsRepair` or `RepairInProgress` it KEEPS the tag and returns —
  a running build snapshotted its records at start and `Request_BeginRepair` takes markup records only (links are inherited
  from the source field's params), so a link landing mid-build or mid-repair would otherwise be cleared and never resolved.
  With `RunAfter<Build, Repair, MarkupCostDerive>` the kept tag re-runs the derive in the tick the build or repair publishes,
  against the field it produced, replacing its inherited `_Params._Links` with the current entries. Nothing published and no
  build coming: clear and wait (the first build reads the entries). (F15a) `Get_IsLinkLive` answers FALSE for a disabled
  record, mirroring `Get_IsMarkupLive` (`Facade/CkGroundNav_NavSurfaceAdapter.cpp:595-596`): live means the published field
  reflects the record AND the record is in effect; a fixture that disabled a link waits on `Get_IsSettled`, not on liveness.
  STOP-1 accepted: `DeriveResolvesAnAddedLinkWithoutABuild` and `DeriveOrderRunsCostBeforeLinks` cannot exist headlessly (no
  published field without a physics world; no test-only installer by design, `Test_GroundNav_Settled.cpp:7-12`) — the derive's
  zero-probe property is pinned at the field level (`Test_GroundNav_Links.cpp:614`) and the end-to-end "a link becomes live with
  no build" plus the "paint and link in one tick, both live after settle" ordering proof move to the AS pin (Unit D2).
  Deviations accepted: `Get_LinkEntries` (view) beside `Get_LinkRecords` (reflected copy), `TryGet_LinkRecord`, the two
  `Get_IsLinkLive` overloads, `FVector::Equals` for the equal-endpoint refusal, the composer's single Display line.

- **(F16) Gym: a station on the existing tuning-range controller** (`CkGroundNavGym_TuningRange_PlayerController.as`),
  a drop and a ladder, `[EDITOR-VERIFY]`; no second gym.
- **(F16a, D2 dispositions — ruled on Unit D2's STOPs, 2026-09-04)** (F16a) The tuning-range gym bakes DEBUG-owned fields
  through `ck.GroundNav.BakeAt` / `BakeFieldAt` (`CkGroundNavGym_TuningRange_PlayerController.as:576-597` →
  `Debug/CkGroundNav_DebugDraw.cpp:1553-1561`) and owns no volume, so `Request_Link` has nothing to aim at there. Ruling: the
  Links station MINTS ITS OWN VOLUME (`utils_ground_nav_volume::Add` + `Request_Build` over the station's own bounds, the way
  the PIE pins do), waits on `utils_nav_surface::Get_IsSurfaceSettled()`, then authors the drop and the ladder; the other
  stations keep their debug bake untouched; draw mode 7 and `ck.GroundNav.LinksAt` read PUBLISHED fields, which is what the
  station has. Still one gym (F16 holds). (D2-1) `Get_IsLinkLive(entity)` becomes a `UFUNCTION(BlueprintPure)` on
  `UCk_Utils_GroundNavVolume_UE` (mirroring `Get_IsMarkupLive`'s binding), and the field-level C++ overload is renamed
  `Get_IsLinkLiveOnField` because UHT rejects an overloaded UFUNCTION name — applied by the orchestrator. (D2-2) `Get_IsSettled`
  stays C++-only by its own stated reason; the AS pins wait on `utils_nav_surface::Get_IsSurfaceSettled()`, the ruled fold of it
  (F9, `Facade/CkGroundNav_NavSurfaceAdapter.cpp:334`). Finding accepted: `Script/Generated` already carried `Request_Link` /
  `Request_ReleaseLink` / `Get_LinkRecords` / `Get_UnresolvedLinkCount` after the Gate 5A-BC boot — the binding regenerates on
  every editor boot, so the UFUNCTION lands in AS on the next gate. `Plugins/CkFoundation/Script/CLAUDE.md` is cited by the
  root doc but the file is `Script/ARCHITECTURE.md`.

- **(F17) Enumeration order in `Get_CrossingsFrom`: this tile's portals, then the field's seams, then links** (by
  `_ResolvedLinks` index, forward before backward for a bidirectional link) — pinned by a slicing-equivalence test.

- **(F2a — ruled on Unit A's STOP, 2026-09-04)** `Field/` may not include `Query/` (the module's one-way layering: nine
  `Query/` headers include `Field/`, none the reverse; `Bake/CkGroundNav_MarkupTypes.h:80-83` states the same rule one layer
  down), and `FCk_GroundNav_SurfaceRef` lived in `Query/CkGroundNav_QueryTypes.h:62`. Ruling: **`FCk_GroundNav_SurfaceRef` moves
  DOWN to `Field/CkGroundNav_FieldTypes.h`** — it addresses a cell of the field, which is the thing `Field/` defines — and
  `Query/CkGroundNav_QueryTypes.h` includes `Field/CkGroundNav_FieldTypes.h` so its 37 users and `Search/`'s two keep compiling
  unchanged. No inlined copy of its five ints, no `Field/` → `Query/` include, no second layering rule. `FCk_GroundNav_ResolvedLink`
  lives in `Field/CkGroundNav_FieldTypes.h` as the brief says (that file is bare LF — preserved).

Findings accepted as written: Fi4 (no plate-id type), Fi5 (stale concept line; the `static_assert` at

**A5 entry measurements — approach (orchestrator ruling, mirrors A4's [NN-D48] discipline):**
- Funnel improvement floor: GroundNav's own number — on the hermetic reference scenes, the polyline
  through crossing midpoints along the flood fill's predecessor chain (the RAW plate-sequence path) vs
  `Get_StringPull` over the same chain vs the visibility-graph oracle; the floor is the MIN improvement
  over all measured pairs. New C++ test `Test_GroundNav_FunnelImprovement.cpp`, `[A5-FUNNEL]` lines.
- Per-query time + queries per frame: Recast, origin floor, 128 entities (the product's ~110–130 NPC
  target) each issuing `Request_FindPath`; frames-to-all-terminal, completions per frame vs
  `Get_MaxPathQueriesPerFrame()` (8), per-request latency. New AS autotest
  `CkAutoTest_NavSurface_RecastBudgets_PathThroughput`, `[RECAST-BUDGET]` lines.
- The three §5 constants are measured as PARITY targets against Recast on runtime-spawned fixtures on
  the second `NavMeshBoundsVolume` band (0, 110000, 0): ramp-vs-level route choice at 1.2/1.5/2.0×
  detours (k_slope), waypoint-to-wall and waypoint-to-centre-line distances in a bent corridor
  (k_wall), inside-corner waypoint offset as a multiple of agent radius plus the distance
  `OffsetFromCorners` passes today (k_corner). New AS autotest
  `CkAutoTest_NavSurface_RecastBudgets_CostFixtures`.
- Gate for all three: `--test-pattern` `GroundNav` then `RecastBudgets` (or `NavSurface`), `--parallel 1
  --no-nullrhi --discover-fresh`; numbers tabled below once the log exists.

**A5 (1/3) — FUNNEL IMPROVEMENT FLOOR MEASURED (2026-09-02, `Saved/Logs/P3-A5-Gate1.log`, build +
`GroundNav` 155/155 — Total rose from 154 by the new `Funnel_ImprovementOverRawCrossingChain`, zero
ensures).** Method: on each hermetic reference scene, the raw plate-sequence path (start → crossing
midpoints along the flood fill's predecessor chain → goal) vs `Get_StringPull` over the same chain, at
radius 0, checked against the visibility-graph oracle (`Test_GroundNav_ReferencePaths.h`).

| Scene | pairs | crossings/pair | improvement min / mean / max | pulled vs oracle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| query scene (deck, walls) | 6 | 2 | 0.431% / 12.156% / 35.304% | delta 0.000 on 6/6 |
| doorway (60 uu gap) | 4 | 3–4 | 1.507% / 7.202% / 17.665% | delta 0.000 on 4/4 |
| two islands (one seam) | 4 | 1 | 0.000% / 0.024% / 0.064% | delta 0.000 on 4/4 |

**Floor recorded for A5 / 3B exit:** (a) the string-pulled length equals the visibility-graph optimum
(delta 0.000 on 14/14 pairs) and is never longer than the raw polyline; (b) on every route with ≥ 2
crossings the improvement is ≥ 0.43% (min over 10 such pairs; mean 10.2%); (c) a single straight
crossing legitimately improves 0% — the raw midpoint already lies on the optimum — so the floor is
stated per (a)+(b), not as one percentage over open terrain. 3B's funnel must reproduce (a) exactly.
Observation: the L corridor in `Test_GroundNav_Funnel.cpp` is a hand-authored portal list with no
baked field behind it, so it has no crossing chain; the doorway scene stands in for it here.

**A5 (2/3) — PER-QUERY TIME + QUERIES PER FRAME MEASURED on the Recast path (2026-09-02,
`Saved/Logs/P3-A5-Recast.log`, test-only `RecastBudgets` pattern, `CkAutoTest_NavSurface_RecastBudgets_PathThroughput`
green).** Fixture: the origin floor (rim ±1000), 128 entities (the product's ~110–130 NPC target) each
issuing one `Request_FindPath` across the floor in the same frame; endpoints pre-projected.

| Measurement | Recast number | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| drain of 128 queries | 128/128 Ready in **2 frames**; 0 partial, 0 failed | all 128 completed in ONE frame |
| completions per frame vs `Get_MaxPathQueriesPerFrame()` = 8 | min = max = mean = **128** | the per-frame budget of 8 does NOT bound same-frame `Request_FindPath` completions on the Recast path — it governs the deferred/parked queue only (read `CkNav_Processor.cpp` before assuming otherwise); 3C's GroundNav branch must not assume 8/frame is enforced upstream |
| queue latency | 1 frame min/max/mean | issue frame → terminal frame |
| per-query Recast search time (`FCk_Nav_PathDiagnostics::_LastQueryDurationMs`) | min 0.0017 ms · mean **0.0023 ms** · max 0.0277 ms | a single-polygon floor; the number is a floor, not a scene cost |

**Budget recorded for A5 "search cost within budget":** on the origin floor at 128 agents, GroundNav's
per-query search time must stay within the same order as Recast's on the same fixture — recorded
reference 0.0023 ms mean / 0.0277 ms max per query, 128 queries in one frame — and the honest interior
budget is owed on the second band's fixtures once 3A can be timed there (a one-polygon floor cannot
exercise a graph search). Queries per frame: the existing setting is 8; GroundNav's driver slices under
its own `FCk_Time` budget and the crowd fork's dispatch cadence is unchanged.
**A5 (3/3) — COST-CONSTANT PARITY TARGETS MEASURED on the Recast path (2026-09-02,
`Saved/Logs/P3-A5-Recast5.log`, test-only `RecastBudgets` pattern 3/3 green, zero AS warnings, zero
ensures; `CkAutoTest_NavSurface_RecastBudgets_CostFixtures`).** Fixtures are runtime-spawned cubes on the
second band; the test first discovers the band's baked extent by walking projection outward, then fits.
Runs 1–4 failed on the test's own staging guards (double-scaled slab ends; a liveness predicate that
logged nothing; probe boxes too small; endpoints outside the volume) — each fixed from the log, none
from Recast.

**FINDING for the maintainer:** the second `NavMeshBoundsVolume` bakes **±1500 uu**, not the requested
±3000 (`band bake extent discovered: +X=1500 -X=1500 +Y=1500 -Y=1500`, floor laid to ±2875). Not a
blocker — the fixtures scale to it (humpScale 0.612, rise/run 91.8/153.0 = the same 0.6 slope) — but
any future band fixture must assume ±1500 until the volume is widened.

| Constant | Fixture (scaled) | Recast observation | Parity target for 3B |
|---|---|---|---|
| k_slope | hump (rise/run 0.6) vs level detour at 1.2× / 1.5× / 2.0× | route = **over-the-hump at all three** (planar 1696 / 1006 / 650 uu vs detour 2035 / 1509 / 1299; 2 waypoints, maxZ 10–25 uu) | **0.0** — Recast prices slope not at all; a positive k_slope is a deliberate divergence, to be measured in shadow |
| k_wall | 300 uu corridor, one right-angle bend, leg 625 | minWall **48.5 uu = 1.39 R**, meanWall 116.2; centre-line distance min 0 / mean 37.2 (3 waypoints) | **0.0** bias; Recast's corners sit at ~1.4 R from the wall (radius erosion + string pull), which is the corner clearance a k_wall = 0 funnel with R inset reproduces to within ~0.4 R |
| k_corner | pillar half 262.5, diagonal route | inside-corner waypoint **54.3 uu = 1.55 R** from the vertex; shipped `OffsetFromCorners` distance = **0** (`CkNav_Processor.cpp:446`) | **0.0 offset shipped**; the raw 1.55 R is erosion geometry, so a funnel that insets by R and offsets by `k_corner·R` with k_corner ≈ 0.55 would match Recast's corner distance — a measured suggestion, not a default; the doc's 1.0 default stays a tunable |

per triangle from its clipped Z extent (`CkGroundNav_Rasterize.cpp:321-338`), so a closed box whose TOP
face lies above the volume ceiling contributes no face inside the field; the floor's top face survives
underneath it and those columns bake as OPEN GROUND — the exact failure the closed-collision contract
guards against, reached by volume clipping instead of authoring, and therefore with no `OPEN
COLLISION` warning. Any body that straddles the volume's Z bounds is silently permeable. Fixture fixed
(wall Z −150..450 inside the volume's −200..500; a `DoCheck_SealIsInTheField` guard proves the seal by
projection + facade reachability before dispatch).

fork was extended, not modified** (no crowd episode test changed result). Zero ensures. The stall twin's
new GUARD A fired exactly as designed: the wall's underside sat 150 uu BELOW the slab top, and the
rasteriser knows faces only, so the slab's top face stayed walkable under 450 uu of headroom — the
closed-collision contract requires a resting body's underside FLUSH on the floor (exact-height tie).
Fixture corrected to rest flush; the first fix's "clipped top face" reading was a second, real footgun
but not this one.
**Four-twin rerun (`Saved/Logs/P3-3C-Stall.log`, test-only, pattern `Crowd_GroundNav`): 4 / 4 green, zero
ensures** — the stall walker now sees `published [Unreachable] rev [1]` then `rev [2]` (strict, then the
permissive retry through the same gate) and fails bounded with the goal-failed hold.

**Crowd ordering — static analysis for the crowd owner (read-only Opus, `CkProcessorGraph.cpp:764-936`):**
implicit edges are inserted between two processors that both `TReadWrite` the same fragment with no
explicit path, directed by `FProcessorRegistry::_Descriptors` order = static-init order across TUs
(`CkProcessorRegistration.h:13-25`) — which UBT's adaptive unity build shuffles; with no edge at all the
ready-queue tie-break is alphabetical (`:1341-1345`), so every layout-dependence enters through those
edges, and one edge re-linearises the whole partition. Unordered same-fragment writer pairs in CkCrowd
sit one tier ABOVE the fully-ordered avoidance chain, on the path/block-state cluster: ungrouped
`Nav_HandleRequests` ↔ `OnPathResolved` ↔ `PathPendingWatchdog` (`Nav_PathResult`); `PathRefresh` ↔
`OnPathResolved` ↔ `BlockedRecheck` (`BlockDetect`, `PathFollow`, `DesiredVelocity` — the no-progress
ladder's evidence); `HandleRequests` ↔ `PathRefresh` (`PathFollow`, contradicting the documented
"HandleRequests wins"); the four `On*Resolved` processors pairwise (`PathFollow`); plus benign
`ApplyDisplacement3D` ↔ `ConstrainToNavmesh` and the Diag taps. Recipe (not applied — crowd owner's
call, full Crowd re-gate): (1) `FProcessor_Nav_HandleRequests` gets `Group = FGroup_Gameplay`; (2)
`PathPendingWatchdog RunAfter OnPathResolved`; (3) `PathRefresh RunAfter {HandleRequests, the four
On*Resolved}`; (4) `BlockedRecheck RunAfter {OnPathResolved, PathRefresh}`; (5) chain the four resolve
processors; (6–7) hygiene edges. Cheapest confirmation: one boot with the unresolved-write policy set
to Strict, which names every pair as an Error. Filed under § Blockers as a crowd-owner item; the
campaign's crowd delta-zero claims stand on the consumer gates with this test listed as a flake.

path/block-state cluster + `FProcessor_Nav_HandleRequests` into `FGroup_Gameplay`), rebuild, and re-gate
`Crowd` + `Nav` + `GroundNav` before shipping. In flight.

Reading: the crowd suite has NO GroundNav field, so its 1350 rows measure coverage, not search (a real
crowd A/B needs GroundNav volumes over the crowd fixtures — PHASE_8 promotion evidence); on fielded
fixtures the providers agree on success 6/6, route length within 28 uu (about one 25 uu cell), the
endpoint 10 uu apart vertically (Recast's projected height vs GroundNav's surface Z), GroundNav emitting
up to two more waypoints (funnel corner vs Recast's string-pull), and GroundNav answering in 0.010–0.015
ms against Recast's 0.011–0.025 ms. Divergences are recorded, never tuned ([NN-D54]-style reading of 3D-4:
scoped now, full-suite at campaign end). Report assembled by `scratchpad/shadow_report.py` from the
`[SHADOW-CMP]` lines; `ck.GroundNav.ShadowReport` / `Get_ShadowReport` give the in-world equivalent.
**NarrowGap ROOT CAUSE (read-only Opus RCA, `scratchpad/rca_NarrowGap.md`) — the ordering recipe is NOT
the cause.** VERIFIED: the identical "walker REACHED" failure is in `Saved/Logs/BuildTest.log` from
2026-08-31 17:11, before GroundNav's search, before 3D, before the recipe; solo pass/fail is 3/7
pre-recipe vs 0/3 post-recipe (p ≈ 0.19, indistinguishable); the scheduler's tie-break is LEXICAL
processor name (`CkProcessorGraph.cpp:1342-1344`), registration order only directs the implicit edges.
Two failure modes, one fixture defect: the "parked" blocker is a plain `utils_crowd_agent::Add` with no
goal, no anchor, no `Request_Stop` — and an Idle agent yields `_PushApartIdleYield = 0.25` to push-apart
(`CkCrowdAgent_PushApart_Processor.cpp:67-70`: only a `GoalFailedHold` is an anchor). The walker presses
for ~30 s and shoves it. (C1, INFERRED, → "UNBOUNDED"): each drift past 21 cm repaints the blocker's
stationary-markup disc (`StationaryMarkup_Processor.cpp:124-131`), Recast re-confirms it with a fresh
serial, `PathRefresh` re-plans (`PathRefresh_Processor.cpp:1036-1048`) and RESETS the block ladder —
`_StrictStandingCrowdPlanFailed = false`, `DoResetProgressWindow()` — so `BlockDetect` (needs an unbroken
3 s window, ×2 stall re-paths, ×3 blocked rechecks ≈ 20–25 s against the 35 s budget) never escalates.
(C2, INFERRED, → "REACHED"): the permissive plan legitimately routes THROUGH the cost-only stationary
markup; 110 cm gap − 2×42 cm bodies leaves 13 cm per side, and with the blocker yielding the pair can
squeeze past (REACHED at 15–35 s = a random walk). The test encodes an assumption the framework never
made (an idle agent is immovable), AND PathRefresh has a real defect: a re-plan triggered by the SAME
obstruction's repaint refunds the no-progress ladder and discards the `NoCrowdFreeRouteExisted` bit.
Discriminating experiment E1 (one instrumented run logging blocker drift + revision steps) in flight.
Fix plan under [NN-D61]: logic — PathRefresh's fresh-markup re-plan resets only the distance baseline,
not the ladder or the strict-standing-crowd verdict, when the disc belongs to the recorded obstruction;
test — assert a blocker-drift ceiling (the `BlockedDetours` sibling already does) and hold the blocker
with a real anchor. The recipe stays.
**E1 result (`P3-3D-E1a.log` green, `P3-3D-E1b.log` red): the shove theory is REFUTED** — blocker drift
≤ 6 uu in both runs, re-plans 0–2. The measured dynamics: green = monotone approach, stall, goal-blocked
hold at 15 s, `OnGoalFailed` at 29.5 s with `noCrowdFreeRoute=true`; red = the walker is KICKED BACK
50–60 uu per contact by push-apart, re-approaches for ~9 s (each re-approach beats the previous best
remaining distance by more than `Get_BlockDetectionProgressEpsilonCm`, so it counts as progress), the
first 3 s no-progress window opens only at ~24 s, the first stall re-plan lands at 27.5 s, and the
bounded failure would arrive near 40 s — past the test's 35 s press budget → "UNBOUNDED". The REACHED
mode is push-apart's soft separation letting the walker tunnel through the parked body. Neither is
ordering.
- **[NN-D62]** (MAINTAINER, 2026-09-03) **"I don't care about breaking existing crowds."** Ruling: BOTH fixes,
  logic first — a walker must never penetrate a stationary-markup-confirmed agent (confirmed-stationary
  yields 0 in push-apart, the mover's overlap against it is resolved hard per frame, with a navmesh
  interaction rule), plus the block ladder tweak if a pressing walker still fails to trip no-progress in
  bounded time; and the test hardened (press budget 35 → 60 s, timeout 45 → 75 s, blocker-drift ceiling
  ≤ 15 uu, the `[NARROWGAP]` measurement line kept). The Crowd gate becomes a RE-BASELINE: every crowd
  test whose result changes is read against the new intended behaviour and its expectation updated or a
  real defect fixed, recorded per test; `Nav` and `GroundNav` stay delta-zero.
- **[NN-D63]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-03) **Hard body vs confirmed-stationary agents — shape, ruled from the
  header-verified design (`scratchpad/design_CrowdHardSeparation.md`).** (1) Hardness is a property of the
  PAIR: exactly one side carries the new `FTag_CrowdAgent_StationaryMarkupConfirmed` (set at the line
  `_ConfirmedOnMesh` becomes true in PathRefresh's confirm pre-pass, cleared in `Remove_Markup` on every
  unpaint / drift-repaint / teardown); two confirmed bodies, or two movers, keep the damped dtCrowd model —
  a symmetric rule would make any overlap between two settled agents permanently unresolvable
  (`PushApart_AgentStaysOnNavmesh` spawns two idle agents 72 uu overlapped and requires ≥ 80 uu). (2) The
  mover's overlap against a hard body is resolved as an exact per-frame CONSTRAINT including the
  displacement already staged (`InPending.Get_Displacement()`), so the inbound normal component is
  cancelled before it lands — the walker stops at contact instead of penetrating and being ejected; the
  hard body yields 0. (3) The navmesh stays authoritative for position: `ConstrainToNavmesh` slides /
  clamps / holds; a mover pinned between a body and a wall stops. (4) No steering/avoidance change: the
  permissive plan routes through the cost disc by design and the ladder must measure zero progress. (5)
  No block-ladder tuning: with the body frozen the kick-back (which re-seeded the progress baseline and
  refunded both counters) and the drift repaint (which made PathRefresh clear the strict verdict) both
  disappear; predicted bounded failure ≈ 25–28 s. (6) Master switch `ECk_CrowdStationaryHardBodyMode`
  (Enabled) in the crowd project settings. (7) Confirmed-only counts as furniture (not painted-only, not
  Idle). (8) Neighbour radius stays approximated by self radius (deferred; every fixture is 42 uu). (9)
  The doctrine texts claiming "zero interpenetration is not an invariant" (module Claude.md § Limitations,
  `Separation_Convergence.as` banner) gain the exception. Tests predicted to change intent are enumerated
  in the design §C; the Crowd gate is read per test against it.
**Hard-body batch landed (U1 logic: tag + PathRefresh stamp + `Remove_Markup` clear + push-apart hard
branch; U2: `ECk_CrowdStationaryHardBodyMode` setting, module Claude.md §§ Tags / Tunables / Limitations /
Navmesh-constraint, the `Separation_Convergence` banner; test: budget 60 s, timeout 75 s, drift ceiling
15 uu, `[NARROWGAP]` telemetry). Gate 7 (`P3-3D-Gate7.log`, build + `Crowd`): 0 build errors, 114/118 —
`Grounding_StationaryAgentReGrounds` GREEN (it was a flake), the baseline pair + `FacesOrigin` unchanged,
NarrowGap red on the NEW guard: "SHOVED: the parked blocker drifted 15.97 uu (ceiling 15.0) after 8.5 s"
with drift 0.0 at 5.5 s — push-apart no longer moves the confirmed body, so a second writer moves it under
sustained contact (INFERRED: separation steering on the idle blocker through its own integrator, which
the old kick-back never sustained). Being closed at the source (the hard body must be inert to
separation-induced motion while still repelling others). No other crowd test changed result.
Actual drift source (VERIFIED by reading, `ConstrainToNavmesh_Processor.cpp:82`, `ApplyOffset:33`,
`PushApart:184`, `AccelClamp:46-48`, `Steering_Processor.h:27`): separation/avoidance are provably inert
for an Idle agent — the only XY motion primitive is `PendingDisplacement`, written by the integrator (zero
for Idle) and push-apart. The hole was in the hard branch itself: stationary markup measures PHYSICAL
stillness, so the walker stopped dead at contact paints at +1.5 s and confirms ~0.5 s later — both sides
confirmed → the damped model → the presser (yield 1.0) shoves the parked body. Fix: the hard side must be
confirmed AND Idle (`PushApart_Processor.cpp:94-98, :128-131`); a confirmed-but-Walking presser is the
mover. **Gate 8 (`P3-3D-Gate8.log`, build + `Crowd`): 0 build errors, 114/118 — NarrowGap GREEN
(`GOALFAILED t=30.0 blockerDrift=0.0 … noCrowdFreeRoute=true`, replans 2, goal-blocked hold from 20.5 s);
the four reds = the baseline pair + the two known flakes (`StationaryAgentReGrounds` green at Gate 7, red
here — its flake history stands). Zero ensures. No crowd test changed intent; the design's §C "at risk"
tests (BunchUp, WallSegments, PathNetworkStationaryDetour, MidWalkDetours, InsideBandPlansOut) all green.**
**Five solo NarrowGap runs on the Gate 8 artifact (`P3-3D-H1..H5.log`): 2 green (GOALFAILED at 57.5 s /
49.0 s, drift 3.98 / 0.02 uu), 3 red — H1/H3 "SHOVED" at 15.4 / 16.6 uu after 29.5 / 26.5 s (a residual
creep ≈ 0.5 uu/s that the in-suite run did not show; H3 never reached the hold), H2 "CHURN" 5 reversals
vs the ceiling of 4 (each blocked-recheck resume now retreats ~15 uu and re-approaches).** Solo runs are
slower (44–57 s to fail vs 30 s in-suite), so frame rate is a variable; a branch still moves the blocker
at high frame rates. Telemetry extended (blocker state / confirmed flag / speed, walker confirmed /
speed, 2.5 s cadence) for two diagnostic runs before any threshold is touched.
**Instrumented solo runs (`P3-3D-T1..T3.log`, hard-body mode Enabled confirmed at dispatch):** T1/T2 GREEN
at 29.5 / 30.0 s with blocker drift 0.0, blocker Idle + confirmed + speed 0 throughout; T3 SHOVED 17.8 uu
at 20.5 s with **blocker speed 0.0 on every sample while its position drifted** — a position write
without velocity (INFERRED: `ConstrainToNavmesh` re-projecting the parked agent laterally after each
navmesh rebuild that the WALKER's own markup churn triggers). The bounce seen in every run: the walker,
held still by the hard body, paints its OWN stationary disc after 1.5 s (`walkerConf=true` at 5.5 s),
then ~2 s later is driven at 221 cm/s and lands 50 uu back with its disc removed — it flees its own
band (the inside-band / escape logic), which is the source of the CHURN reversals. The paint itself is
by design (`StationaryMarkup_Processor.cpp:102-103`: a blocked walker must plug a corridor for others),
so the fixes target (A) an agent's own disc never counting as a band it must escape and (B) a
confirmed-stationary Idle agent being re-grounded vertically only on a rebuild. In flight.
Both of those hypotheses REFUTED by reading: an agent's own disc is already excluded from every escape
path (`PathRefresh_Processor.cpp:223-224, :492-494, :957-958`), and the zero-displacement grounding path
is structurally vertical-only (`ConstrainToNavmesh_Algorithm.h:89`; push-apart writes `PendingDisplacement`,
never velocity, so `blockerSpeed=0.0` says nothing). VERIFIED mechanisms: (A) the blocker's disc is
`_StationaryMarkupExtentMultiplier = 2.0` × 42 = 84 uu while the hard contact holds the walker at
83.95 uu — inside the band by the slop, so every re-plan builds an escape prefix
(`HandleRequests_Processor.cpp:323-352`) and walks out at full speed, then unpaints and returns (the old
soft resolver bounced the walker clear of the edge, so this never latched); (B) `GoalBlocked` is
co-resident with `Idle` (`Fragment.h:291-293`), so once the walker reaches the goal-blocked hold both
sides satisfy confirmed+Idle, the pair falls back to the damped model and the blocker yields 0.25 —
17.8 uu by 20.5 s in T3 matches.
- **[NN-D64]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-03) (A) "inside a band" means the centre is more than
  `kBandContactToleranceUu = 1.0` past the disc edge — touching a band from outside is being blocked by
  it, not standing in it; one shared predicate for the escape prefix, the escaped query start and the
  plan-out. (B) The hard side of a pair is confirmed AND Idle AND NOT GoalBlocked — stationary by intent,
  not by frustration; GoalFailedHold keeps its existing anchor rule, evaluated first.

- **[NN-D66]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-03) 4A forks ruled on `scratchpad/design_4A_Markup.md`: (F1) cost
  markup is stamped per plate, any-overlap-wins, and never splits a plate — the zero-probe gate and
  `_PlateIndex` stability (seam stubs, portals, labels) both forbid a split; the sub-plate escape hatch is
  the per-query `_PlateCostMultipliers` table ([NN-D58]); named risk: an agent-sized crowd cost paint
  prices a whole plate. (F2) plate policy is an `int32 _AreaPolicyIndex` into a per-tile deduplicated
  `_AreaPolicies` table plus a `float _CostMultiplier`; the plate stays POD. (F3) markup identity across
  the provider table is the markup entity handle; no new id type. (F4) GroundNav mirrors accepted records
  onto the VOLUME entity; the markup entity carries a value back-pointer `{volume entity, record id}`.
  (F5) `Do_SurfaceRevision` becomes `Get_AggregatedTileEpochSum` in 4A (one line); 4B adds the
  monotonicity test. (F6) a cost-only derive is a field copy, not a bake, and bypasses the probe budget;
  its copy cost is a tracked number. Tag → policy resolution is NEUTRAL: `ck::nav_surface::Register_AreaPolicy(tag,
  FCk_NavSurface_AreaPolicy{Kind, CostMultiplier})` in CkNavigation, registered beside every existing
  `ck::nav_surface_recast::Register_AreaTag` site so one tag carries both its Recast class and its neutral
  meaning (CkNavigation cannot depend on CkGroundNav, and CkCrowd owns four tags of its own): `Nav.Area.Impassable`
  → Walkability; `Nav.Area.Restricted` → Cost × 1.0 (its `UNavArea` sets no `DefaultCost`); the crowd's
  four mirror their `UNavArea::DefaultCost` (Agent → the standing toll, AvoidanceVolume 4096, CostOnly 1024,
  HardExclude 16384 — a cost on Recast, so a cost here). An unregistered tag is rejected at admission with
  an ensure and `Failed_NotEnqueued`, as Recast rejects an unregistered area class. Walkability markup rejects
  the covered spans in `DoFilter_Walkability` (before layers) and re-bakes the intersected tiles through the
  existing build path; local repair is 4C. Findings registered: markup is not provider-dispatched today
  (both `DoHandleRequest`'s body and `Get_IsMarkupLive` call `ck::nav_surface_recast::*`; the table needs
  two more entries and Recast registers both in the same commit); `Get_ContentFingerprint` is called from
  nothing but its own test, so 4A adds markup to the frozen enumeration + perturbation test and does NOT
  claim admission-skip (F1.11's stored hash + skip stay owed); the plate-merge's third criterion is
  documented twice and implemented nowhere — 4A implements it.
- **[NN-D67]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-03) The A6 moved-obstacle baseline is measured in the fixture
  vocabulary — an impassable MARKUP box moved 600 uu (drop + repaint in one frame) — not a runtime mesh.

  spawned so its mesh precedes registration and registered through `OnActorRegistered`, bumps the surface
  revision by one per move (Recast's dynamic tile rebuild fires) yet the spot under it keeps projecting
  for 400 frames, and carves only on a `RebuildAll`. Which octree/export condition drops the runtime
  mesh from a dirty-tile rebuild is an engine-source question, deferred to the maintainer (ask for the
  file path). The crowd/queue obstacle suite paints markup, so the markup baseline is the one 4C/4D
  compare against; the geometry caveat is recorded beside the number.

**A6 BUDGETS MEASURED (Recast, 2026-09-03, `P4-A6-Recast2-recovered.log`, `--test-pattern RecastBudgets --parallel 1
--no-nullrhi --discover-fresh`, 5 found / 5 green on `P4-A6-Recast3.log` after the moved-obstacle test was rewritten to the markup box per
[NN-D67]; `P4-A6-Recast2-recovered.log` carries the runtime-mesh evidence):**

| Number | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Markup-live latency (`Nav.Area.Restricted` paint → `Get_IsMarkupLive` true) | 2 frames, 0.0167 s; min = max = 2 over 5 paints | `MarkupLiveLatency` |
| Impassable-hole latency (`Nav.Area.Impassable` paint → tight-extent projection at the box centre fails) | 3 frames, 0.025 s; min = max = 3 over 5 paints | `MarkupLiveLatency` |
| Unpaint repair latency (markup destroyed → the spot projects again) | 2 frames, 0.0167 s over 5 | `MarkupLiveLatency` |
| Surface revision per paint / unpaint | +1 per Restricted paint, +2 per Impassable paint, +1 per drop (5 → 26 over the run) | `MarkupLiveLatency` |
| Moved-markup-box repair latency (impassable box dropped + repainted 600 uu away in one frame → old spot projects again / new spot stops projecting) | 2 frames, 0.0167 s for BOTH halves; min = max = 2 over 5 moves, 5/5 landed; first-paint carve 2 frames; revision +1 per move (26 → 33) | `MovedObstacleRepairLatency` |

Two consequences the A6 tests surfaced, recorded not asserted: `Get_IsMarkupLive` can never read true for
`Nav.Area.Impassable` on Recast — the tag maps to `UNavArea_Null`, which removes the polygons the flag
would read (`CkNavSurface_RecastAdapter.cpp:672-696`), so the impassable number is taken off the
consequence; and `Get_IsReachable` cannot see a 300 uu hole because it projects both endpoints with the
project-wide 500 uu extent (`CkNavSurface_RecastAdapter.cpp:561`, `CkNav_ProjectSettings.h:34`).

**4A exit criteria — MET:** disabling a markup restores prior policy byte-identically against a field baked without
it (`Bake.Markup_DisablingAMarkupRestoresTheFieldByteIdentically`); a cost-only markup performs zero geometry
probes (`Bake.Markup_CostOnlyDeriveSpendsZeroProbesAndChangesOnlyPolicy` asserts `_ProbesSpent == 0` and every
non-policy array byte-equal); impassable box -> exactly the covered cells (`Markup_ImpassableBoxMakesExactlyTheCoveredCellsImpassable`);
overlapping cost -> max + union; live false -> true only when every covering tile republished
(`Volume.MarkupLive_*`, two-tile laggard pinned); markup in an unbuilt region recorded; invalid shape / unregistered
tag rejected with no partial state and one completion (`Volume.Markup_Rejects*`); the race pin is real (above);
Gate 4A-2 verdict: cost-only -> zero probes, walkability -> re-bake -> continue. Every new UFUNCTION has C++ and
AngelScript call sites (`Request_AreaMarkup`/`Get_IsMarkupLive` unchanged on the neutral surface; the volume's
`Request_AreaMarkup`/`Request_ReleaseAreaMarkup` used by the admission tests and the pin); Blueprint per [NN-D55].

**Rulings applied and deviations accepted ([NN-D66]):** the neutral registry flushes separately from Recast's (a
neutral table must not depend on a Recast read); crowd tag costs read off each `UNavArea` CDO at seed time; the
walkability re-bake is WHOLE-VOLUME in 4A because `FCk_Request_GroundNavVolume_Build` has no bounds form
(local repair is 4C; recorded, not hidden); `_AreaPolicies` lives on the plate field, not the tile; the cost
stamp runs after the halo crop (the published lattice is the one a derive can reconstruct); `Get_AreaMultiplier`
takes the field explicitly (the shared data deliberately holds no field); the markup record's epoch is a plain
`int64` in the bake layer (`Bake/` may not include `Field/`); a disabled markup reads NOT live on both providers
(Recast tears its painter down; the two must agree); volumes register with the world-field registry at Setup
with a null field so a paint before the first build is accepted; the derive restamps every tile and bumps a
tile where its policy changed OR an unobserved enabled record reaches it (so a no-op paint still reads live).

**Findings for later phases:** (1) F1.11's stored per-tile hash + admission skip are still NOT wired;
`Get_ContentFingerprint` now enumerates markup and is called from nothing but its tests; (2) the entity-level
positive live path cannot be exercised headlessly (a published field needs a Jolt world); the PIE pin covers
it; (3) `Plugins/CkFoundation/CLAUDE.md:459` links `Script/CLAUDE.md`, which was renamed to
`Script/ARCHITECTURE.md`; doc drift, one-line fix owed outside this campaign's files; (4) the race window on a
tiny field is shorter than a frame; any future pin on markup timing must slice the bake; (5) `Get_IsReachable`
projects with the project-wide extent and cannot see a hole narrower than 1000 uu (A6 finding, unchanged).

**4B exit criteria — MET:** `Get_SurfaceRevision` monotone per publish on GroundNav (`Revision.TileEpochSumRisesOnEveryInterleaving`,
`TileCountIsInvariantAcrossRebuilds`, `WorldRevisionDoesNotFallWhenAVolumeIsTornDown`); `BindTo_OnSurfaceRebuilt`
fires exactly once per publish with the changed bounds (`NavSurface.RebuiltFiresExactlyOncePerQueuedPublish`,
`RebuiltDrainsToEmptyAndRefiresNothing`, `FallbackFiresOnceWithUnknownBoundsWhenOnlyTheRevisionMoved`;
`Revision.ChangedTileBoundsIsTheUnionOfRepublishedTilesOnly`); push invalidation against cached corridor BOUNDS
with the margin exact (`Invalidation.*` x7); `PathRefresh` consumes the tag with plan repair before a cold
search (`Path.RepairPlanModeReusesTheCachedCorridor`, `RepairWithoutACachedCorridorPlansCold`,
`CorridorBoundsCoverEveryWaypointInflatedByRadiusPlusMargin`); a consumer re-plans exactly once per drift and
never per frame, without leaving `Walking` (the PIE pin, proven against its bypass); Recast unchanged (Crowd
delta-zero, the neutral `RebuildAdvancesRevisionAndSignals.as` green on Recast with only the payload widened).
Re-plan latency: 3 frames from live to the installed route vs the A6 Recast markup-box baseline of 2 — logged,
not asserted, per the pin's own rationale (ordering, not correctness).

**Rulings applied and deviations accepted ([NN-D68]):** the drain's two helpers are public so the poll fallback
is testable headlessly; a null provider table no longer early-outs the drain (a queue a provider pushed still
drains); the changed-bounds union guards on BUILT tiles (a failed tile carries the epoch but degenerate
bounds); `_LastCorridorEpoch` joined the two named corridor fields (the repair seam needs the epoch the corridor
was planned against, which nothing stored); the corridor box spans the field's Z slab (a plate has no absolute
Z); the repair verdict was promoted to a global reflected enum so the result reaches BP/AS; the crowd gate
keys on `_ActiveProvider == GroundNav`, not on the path fragment's presence — SHADOW mode gives a Recast agent
a live GroundNav corridor too (U6's finding); `Request_NavigationPath` gained a defaulted plan-mode parameter
so every internal re-dispatch stays provider-aware; the adjacent-paint pin uses a COST markup because under
4A's whole-volume re-bake an impassable paint republishes every tile and therefore flags every corridor in
the volume (correct, coarse; 4C narrows it).

**Findings for later phases:** (1) a headless test cannot install a published field on a volume entity
(`_Field` is friend-private and the only writer needs a Jolt world) — the cost-derive-queues-one-broadcast
pin is covered by the PIE pins instead; a test-only installer is worth adding when 4C's repair tests need one;
(2) the repath tag is cleared only by a Walking, non-asleep crowd agent — an Idle agent spends it on one
correct repair when it next walks, a non-crowd path holder keeps it inertly (benign; a GroundNav-side sweep
would remove the wart); (3) `Crowd_SteeringPerf` can red on a Recast start-projection warning at a test
boundary (dirty-tile rebuild in flight from the previous test's teardown) — flake, green solo;
(4) `CkCrowd` still calls `ck::nav_surface_recast::Get_IsAreaLiveAt` directly in its markup-confirm pass
(P5/P8); (5) `Source/CkGroundNav/CLAUDE.md` gained the invalidation rows; `Source/CLAUDE.md`'s CkGroundNav
tier row now matches the Build.cs (`AStar`, `Navigation` were missing).

**4B comment audit:** 5 findings (2 STALE contract comments, 3 REWRITE plan-flavoured phrasings incl. an
identifier prefix), applied before the final gate; the final `GroundNav` re-ran on the audited artifact.

- **[NN-D69]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-03) 4C forks ruled on `scratchpad/design_4C_LocalRepair.md`: (F1) a
  dedicated `FCk_Request_GroundNavVolume_Repair` carrying one world `FBox` (a moved body = the union of its
  old and new bounds, the caller's contract; a markup update unions old and new record bounds itself);
  `Request_Build` keeps meaning "everything". (F2) dirty bounds coalesce as ONE unioned box while
  `NeedsRepair` is set and are SNAPSHOTTED when the repair opens — a box arriving mid-repair opens the next
  repair, never widens one in flight (the in-house VoxelNav reference re-reads its pending box per slice,
  which would break sliced == one-shot; a latent CkVoxelNav defect, filed outside this campaign).
  (F3) the closure pass re-runs over EVERY tile in ascending index order with a fresh checked set, so
  `_OpenBodies` is byte-identical to a full bake; the cheaper carry-forward needs a canonical order applied
  to the bake too and is a later optimisation. (F4) seam portals, edge boundary and reachability labels
  recompute fully (label numbering is whole-field; zero probes). (F5) the field comparator is extracted to a
  shared test header and gains `_OpenBodies`, `_TileEdgeBoundary`; tile epochs stay excluded because a repair
  stamps only re-baked tiles (that is what makes `Get_ChangedTileBounds` exact and keeps the sum monotone).
  (F6) the walkability paint routes through repair, retiring 4A's whole-volume rebuild. Tiles re-bake iff
  their bounds meet the dirty box inflated in XY by the halo (`ceil(MaxClearance / cell) * cell` = 200 uu at
  defaults; Z not inflated — the halo is XY-only); each through the identical fetch shape a full bake uses;
  a repair during a full build waits and is SUPERSEDED (completes `Succeeded`) when the build publishes; a
  forced build during a repair cancels it (`Failed_Cancelled`); a repair with no published field fails; the
  repair holds its own reference to the source field, its epoch is fixed at open, a racing publish is
  detected at swap and the repair discarded. Latency claim: on a small field a repair fits one slice at the
  default budget, so the PIE number is probes and scaling, not frames; the moved-markup-box pin logs its
  frames against A6's 2 and asserts <= 2. Findings registered: the PHASE_4 §6 sketch is under-specified
  (one processor, bare `FGroup_Transform` without the Jolt ordering; the house pattern is StartRepair +
  Repair with the exclusion set); a repaired tile that then fails must publish carrying its failure (a
  pin covers it); the geometry world-revision check is world-wide and static-only, so a geometry-driven
  multi-frame repair must capture the revision AFTER the move at open (markup never trips it); CkJolt has
  no body-moved event (a bounds-carrying static-scene notification at its seven `Request_NoteStaticSceneChanged`
  sites is a later phase); `_ProbeBudgetPerTick` defaults to 1,500,000 (about 16 reference tiles), so
  sliced-vs-one-shot is only meaningful under an explicit small budget. Units: U1 `Field/CkGroundNav_FieldRepair`
  (pure) and U2 volume request/fragments/utils in parallel; U3 the StartRepair/Repair processors + the
  markup rewiring; U4 debug snapshot/draw + `ck.GroundNav.RepairAt`; U5 hermetic pins + the shared
  comparator; U6 PIE pins (door toggle, moved markup box). One gate.

`origin/feature/ck-navigation`. Units: U1 pure field repair (`Field/CkGroundNav_FieldRepair`), U1b current
records into the repair and the cost derive, U2 request/fragments/utils, U3 StartRepair/Repair processors +
the markup route, U4 debug snapshot/draw + `ck.GroundNav.RepairAt`, U5a shared field comparator, U5b hermetic
pins, U6 PIE pins, plus a 4A-test re-pin and a release fix.

| Gate | Log | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Gate 4C-1 (build) | `P4-4C-Gate1.log` | 25 errors, all in the 4A admission/live tests: the deleted walkability-dirty tag and the markup drain's new arity — re-pinned to the new rule |
| Gate 4C-2 `GroundNav` | `P4-4C-Gate2.log` | 254 found (242 -> 254) / 252 green / 2 red = the two PIE pins: (a) both read their broadcast count in the step after liveness, but the signal fires in the fire phase after the publish; (b) the moved box only ever re-baked the NEW columns and the old spot never reopened — a release names an entity already in teardown and the drain rejected it as invalid, so the record leaked and its ground was never repaired (a 4A defect: every markup destroyed through `Request_DestroyEntity` had been leaking on the GroundNav volume) |
| **Gate 4C-3 `GroundNav`** | `P4-4C-Gate3.log` | **255 / 255, zero ensures**; door: close 9 frames / open 9 frames every round, changed bounds = exactly the 9-tile union (X -600..600 of a 2000 uu volume), never the whole 5x5; moved box: both halves 19 frames every move (18 tiles at one tile a tick + the finishing pass), changed bounds = columns 1..6, columns 0 and 7 never touched; hermetic `Repair.*` x9 green incl. `RepairedFieldMatchesAFullRebake` byte-identical over every array with epochs excluded, `MovedObstacleChangesOnlyWhereItMoved` `[REPAIR-BUDGET] tiles=3 of 9 probes=134757 fullBakeProbes=397539`, `SlicedRepairMatchesOneShotRepair`, `HeldPreRepairFieldIsUnchanged`, `StaleGeometryFailsClosed`, `RepairBakesUnderTheCallersRecordsNotTheSourceFields`, `Markup_ReleaseNamingADestroyedEntityStillRemovesTheRecord` |

**4C exit criteria — MET:** repaired == full rebake byte-identical (`Repair.RepairedFieldMatchesAFullRebake`); a moved
obstacle changes the field only where it moved (`Repair.MovedObstacleChangesOnlyWhereItMoved` + the PIE moved-box pin's
untouched columns); sliced == one-shot (`Repair.SlicedRepairMatchesOneShotRepair`); a held `TSharedPtr<const>` pre-repair
field is unchanged (`Repair.HeldPreRepairFieldIsUnchanged`); zero-tile and invalid boxes (`ZeroTileDirtyBoxCompletesWithoutChange`);
stale geometry fails closed; the repair bakes under the caller's records; moved-obstacle repair latency: 19 frames for
18 tiles at the test's one-tile-a-tick slice (logged, not asserted — at the default budget the whole repair is one
slice, so the number that scales is probes: 134,757 vs 397,539 for a full bake of the 3x3 fixture); gym door pin
(`Repair_DoorToggleRepairsLocally`); the walkability paint no longer re-bakes the whole volume ([NN-D66]'s recorded
deviation retired).

**Rulings applied ([NN-D69]) and deviations accepted:** tiles are placed from the field params so a failed tile still
selects (a failed tile carries degenerate bounds); the repair returns stage results so budget and stale-geometry are
expressible; the geometry revision is unsigned to match the backend; the closure pass runs once at the end over every
tile in index order (first-touch order preserved); a zero-tile repair completes at open; `StartRepair` snapshots the
pending box and parked requests together; a build arming while a repair is in flight cancels it (any build request,
not only `ForceRestart` — removes the epoch hazard of a build stamped before a repair landing after it); the cost
derive now runs after `Repair`; the moved-box PIE fixture is 8x3 (a 6x3 lattice is swallowed by the halo); the two PIE
pins wait on the rebuilt signal as a named condition after liveness.

**Findings for later phases:** (1) a repair raised by a walkability+cost kind change while a repair spans ticks is
discarded once by the cost derive's publish and retried (converges, bounded) — a gym pin if retags prove common;
(2) `RepairedTileThatFailsPublishesItsFailure` is unwritable: the stub backend has no failure injection and the bake
has no per-tile failure path from geometry; (3) the in-house CkVoxelNav reference re-reads its pending box per repair
slice (would break sliced == one-shot there) — filed outside this campaign; (4) CkJolt has no body-moved event
(seven `Request_NoteStaticSceneChanged` sites are where a bounds-carrying notification would go); (5) the moved-box
Recast baseline (2 + 2 frames) is a same-frame drop+repaint of a modifier on a tiny navmesh — GroundNav's 19 is the
sliced fixture, not the default budget; the honest comparison is probes and scaling, recorded above.

**4C comment audit:** 6 findings (2 DELETE, 2 REWRITE, 2 STALE), applied before Gate 4C-3.

  present and no build in flight; GroundNav = every volume built, not building, no `NeedsRepair` /
  `RepairInProgress` / `MarkupCostDirty`, request queues empty. `Request_SurfaceRebuild_ForTesting` stays a

  stanza in `Config/DefaultCkFoundation.ini` (the toolbox has no argument passthrough), reverted before the
  final gates; per-test `Request_SetProvider` is the fallback only if the ini default does not reach the
  test boot. (F3) the field over the shared level's origin floor is spawned at runtime by a new shared
  AngelScript helper (`Script/Common/CkAutoTest_GroundNavFixture.as`), never placed in the `.umap`; the
  floor's collision closure is checked by the first bake (an open body is a warning the harness escalates).
  (F4) the debug field cache becomes a world-keyed map cleared at `OnWorldCleanup` (the registry's own
  idiom). (F5) replacing a Recast-direct SETTLE predicate (`utils_nav::Try_ProjectOntoNavmesh`,
  `utils_nav::Request_NavigationRebuild_ForTesting`) with its neutral twin (`utils_nav_surface::Try_ProjectPoint`,
  `Request_SurfaceRebuild_ForTesting` + `Get_IsSurfaceSettled`) IS a permitted provider-neutral repair: only
  the addressee of the question changes; geometry, budgets, timeouts and assertions stay byte-identical
  (PHASE_4 §9's fence targets those); it edits ten green files and owes a Recast re-gate before the
  crossover run. (F6) sites whose SUBJECT is the crowd's own markup painter (`UCk_NavAreaMarkup_UE` +
  `ck::nav_surface_recast::Get_IsAreaLiveAt` — stationary discs, avoidance volumes) are Recast-only in
  PHASE_4 with a named seam gap against B2, handed to P5/P8: sites 1, 2, 5 (+ the two gyms are
  `[EDITOR-VERIFY]` only). PHASE_4 §8 criterion 12 ("full suite delta-zero") yields to [NN-D54]: the phase
  gate is the scoped `GroundNav` / `Nav` / `Crowd` / `Queue` patterns; the one full suite runs at campaign
  end. 4D-1's grep is rewritten type-shaped over CkGroundNav AND CkNavigation with a documented allowlist
  (the world-field registry, the provider table, the two per-world mirrors, the two policy registrars —
  world-keyed and cleared at world cleanup, or immutable after seed) plus a Layer-1 pin
  (`MultiWorld.*`). Findings registered: the phase's grep as written cannot return zero (cvars, console
  registrars); the registry deliberately never unpublishes a volume; `GDeferredNavRequests` is already
  gone; the multi-PIE harness runs on `/Engine/Maps/Entry` with no floor (a two-world pin builds its own
  geometry per world); `ServerAndClientsIndependent` net mode has no live user; nine of the ten painting
  autotests settle through Recast-direct projection — the single blocking finding for F1.37; the Iris
  ensure `DataStreamChannel.cpp:244` fires on every `Nav` run and is named in any "teardown ensure-free"
  claim. Units: U1 world-keyed debug cache, U2 settled capability (CkNavigation + Recast), U4 multi-world
  pins, in parallel; U3 GroundNav's settled body; U5 the origin-volume helper + `[GROUNDNAV-CROSSOVER]`
  line; U6 the settle substitution across sites 2-9 and 13 (one file each); U7 the multi-PIE pin. Gates:
  `GroundNav`, then `Nav`/`Crowd`/`Queue` on Recast (delta-zero for U6), then the crossover run under the
  ini, then the ini reverted and the final patterns.
