**4D exit criteria — MET:** multi-PIE isolation green (`MultiWorld.*` x6 + `Ck_AutoTest_Net_GroundNav_TwoWorldsDoNotShareFields`:
a paint in one world moves nothing in the other, each world hears only its own band's rebuilds); teardown
ensure-free on GroundNav's side (the one ensure in every `Nav` run is Iris `DataStreamChannel.cpp:244`, named,
pre-existing); no process-wide provider state — the type-shaped grep over CkGroundNav + CkNavigation finds
exactly the allowlist: the world-field registry, the debug field cache, the two per-world provider/shadow
mirrors (all world-keyed and cleared at `OnWorldCleanup`) and the three code-shaped registries (provider
tables, area policies, Recast tag tables — seeded once, never written by a world), plus bind-once booleans;
the deterministic test hook is a kick + the named `Get_IsSurfaceSettled` condition (the Settle pin: settled
implies live and the hole cut, no hop counts); the `UNavArea_Null` vocabulary is enumerated above with a
result per site; three environments for `Get_IsSurfaceSettled` — C++ (`Test_NavSurface_Settled.cpp`),
AngelScript (the fixture helper), Blueprint per [NN-D55].

**Rulings applied ([NN-D70]) and deviations accepted:** the crossover ran under the temporary ini stanza (F2);
the origin field is staged at runtime by the helper (F3) — and, beyond F3, by every crossover site itself,
gated on the world's provider so the Recast run stages nothing (one Display line at end-play is the only
Recast-side change); the helper's half-extent is 1000, the level's own navmesh bounds, not 1600: a wall that
runs off the navmesh on Recast has to run off the field here or it leaves a corridor no fixture expects
(site 6 solo showed exactly that — the route went round the wall's south end); 4B's "the registry never
unpublishes" mechanism for a monotone revision is RETIRED — a volume leaves the registry at end-play and its
tile-epoch sum is carried as retired revision, so `WorldRevisionDoesNotFallWhenAVolumeIsTornDown` still holds
exactly while a destroyed volume answers no query (the pin's last two assertions were flipped to say so);
a build takes over pending repair regions when it STARTS (its record snapshot), never at its publish, and a
region or walkability paint raised while a build runs stays pending across the publish and repairs the field
it produced. The phase's "full suite delta-zero" criterion yields to [NN-D54] (scoped patterns; one full suite
at campaign end).

**Findings for later phases:** (1) the crowd's own markup (stationary discs, avoidance volumes) is
`UCk_NavAreaMarkup_UE` + `ck::nav_surface_recast::Get_IsAreaLiveAt` — Recast-only; sites 1, 2, 4, 5 and the
two gyms wait on a neutral area-live-at capability and a neutral crowd painter (P5/P8, the B2 seam gap);
(2) the rebuild re-plan of a live episode now advances the request revision like every other re-plan (block-detect resume, permissive retry) — site 7's observable, and the reason a stale answer to the replaced plan can no longer install; the 4B pin still counts exactly one route swap; (3) `Stall_UnreachableGoalFailsBounded` on GroundNav reports OnGoalReached at t=5.4s for a goal the late-painted wall (|y| <= 1400 across a +/-1000 field) has sealed off — either the repair re-plan installs a partial route whose reachable end the crowd treats as arrival, or the crowd's arrival never consults reachability by label; the native twin (site 12) passes because its wall is baked before the walk. Diagnose in the crowd-on-GroundNav work (P5/P8) — record only here; (4) `TryGet_Field` still falls back to a world's FIRST field for a
point inside none of them — harmless with one volume per world, worth a bounds-strict twin when volumes
multiply; (5) the whole `Crowd`/`Queue` suites under the GroundNav default carry many reds outside the
enumerated sites — tests that paint nothing and stage no field walk on nothing; that is the un-migrated
remainder for P8, not crossover evidence; (6) the fixture-vs-field extent lesson generalises: a GroundNav
fixture must reproduce the Recast fixture's BOUNDS, not only its geometry; (7) verbose volume logging is
absent — every build/repair outcome below Display is invisible in a gate log, so the discriminators had to
be tests; (8) `Test_GroundNav_MultiWorld.cpp`'s `TryGet_Field` fallback pin documents the first-field rule.

**4D comment audit:** two passes — 20 findings applied before Gate 4D-3, then 19 (13 STALE-REWRITE, 6 REWRITE, 0 DELETE) over the registry, build-takeover, fixture-extent and pin changes, applied before Gate 4D-5; every final pattern ran on the audited artifact



**3D exit criteria — MET:** shadow mode operational (setting + per-world mirror, dispatch after the Recast
request, ignore gate first in the resolve processor); Gate 3D-1 — installed paths byte-identical with
shadow on (`Shadow_InstalledPathIsByteIdenticalToRecast` green; `Crowd` and `Nav` patterns identical
shadow-on vs off); the diagnostics fragment is values-only (grep = 0 hits on the fragment/report files,
`static_assert(is_trivially_copyable)` on the leaf structs, the Layer-1 copy-then-destroy test);
`FProcessor_GroundNav_ShadowCompare` registered and the counters move (`Shadow_CountersMoveOnAShadowRun`);
the divergence report has a diff-stable schema (`Shadow_ReportSchemaIsStable`) and the scoped sweep
report is pasted above per [NN-D56]/[NN-D60]; the full-suite shadow report is owed at campaign end.

## PHASE_3 — 3A plate-portal graph + A* (2026-09-02, session 8)

**Landed (CkFoundation, ungated until Gate 2c):** `Search/CkGroundNav_SearchTypes.h` (status enum,
`FCk_GroundNav_PathQuery`, `FCk_GroundNav_PathResult`), `Search/CkGroundNav_PlatePortalGraph.{h,cpp}`
(shared seed, crossing-arrival node pool shared through a pointer because `TSearchState` hides its
by-value graph copy, `Get_CrossingTransitionPoint` = interval midpoint, `static_assert` on
`astar::AStarGraph`), `Search/CkGroundNav_PathSearch.{h,cpp}` (pre-search order Unbuilt → Blocked →
NoStartSurface → NoGoalSurface → Unreachable(labels, 0 expansions) → same-plate Ready → search; ends
resolved at radius 0 like the flood fill; every `ContinueSearch` clamped to the remaining expansion
budget so one-shot and sliced runs execute the identical iteration sequence; `_MaxExpansions = N`
ADMITS exactly N), `CkAStar` added to `CkGroundNav.Build.cs`, `ck.GroundNav.PathAt X Y Z TX TY TZ` in
the debug draw + its CLAUDE.md row. CkTests: `Test_GroundNav_PathSearch.cpp` (9 Search tests) +
pinned rows in `Test_GroundNav_ReferenceNumbers.cpp`.

**Gate 2 (`P3-3A-Gate2.log`):** 11 compile errors, all mechanical (unqualified `FCk_GroundNav_QueryCost`
in the driver's private namespace; two test files `using`-declaring the GLOBAL-scope status enum from
`ck::groundnav`). Fixed by the orchestrator.
**Gate 2b (`P3-3A-Gate2b.log`, build + `GroundNav`):** 164 found (154 + funnel test + 9 Search), **163
green, 1 red** = `ExpansionCap_ReportsBudgetExceeded`: the test assumed `_MaxExpansions = 1` always
bites; under the ruled semantics (cap N admits N) a route finishing at exactly 1 expansion is Ready.
Test being rewritten to pin the ruling (k−1 → BudgetExceeded, k → Ready). Zero ensures.
`Sliced_EqualsOneShot` and `OneShot_MatchesAnalyticOptimum` GREEN first time — the 3A exit criteria.

**Numbers (Gate 2b):** every reference pair string-pulls to the oracle exactly; expansions 2 on all five
query-scene pairs (cells read 16–26), 3/5/3/5 on the doorway pairs (21–31). Pinned: query route
2 / 16, doorway route 3 / 21 (also in VALIDATION.md A5).
**Observation for 3B:** `_SearchCost` is the A* g-cost to the goal-plate NODE, i.e. through transition
midpoints and WITHOUT the final leg to the goal point (e.g. 1218.6 vs pulled 1300.0); optimality is
unaffected because the goal node's heuristic is exact within a convex plate, but the reported number
must gain the last leg when 3B finalises the cost model.

**Gate 2c (`Saved/Logs/P3-3A-Gate2c.log`, build + `GroundNav`, `--discover-fresh`): 164 / 164 green,
zero ensures, the four pinned reference numbers asserted.** Cap tests now pin the ruling (k−1 →
BudgetExceeded with no corridor; k → Ready with the identical corridor).
**3A exit criteria — MET:** `static_assert` pins `FCk_GroundNav_PlatePortalGraph` to `astar::AStarGraph`
(`CkGroundNav_PlatePortalGraph.h`, compiled); sliced search == one-shot search exactly
(`Sliced_EqualsOneShot`: status, corridor, crossings, search cost, expansion count, at
MaxIterations = 1 and at a 1 µs budget); expansion counts recorded as tracked regressions (above and
VALIDATION.md A5); Gate 3A-3's four cases (optimum within the bound at w = 1, cross-component reject
with zero expansions, sliced == one-shot, node cap and corridor cap each terminate with

index is carried explicitly and never recomputed from the point, a refused step moves the point onto
the refused edge and zeroes that axis of the remaining motion (so a concave corner ends in two
slides), and the final clamp into the current cell's closed square absorbs rounding only. Its five
declared choices are adopted: leg origin kept apart from the current point; the raycast's plate
early-out yields to the cost cap when the cap would bind inside the plate; cost seeded from the start
resolution; `_LastSurface` filled on a clear ray too; `k`-prefixed constants. Design note recorded:
a step into an UNBUILT neighbour tile is refused like a wall (fail closed) and the walk still reports
Success at the point it reached — a consumer that needs to know why it stopped asks the region
status. G (`Test_GroundNav_Query_SurfaceWalk.cpp` 712 lines / 6 tests, `Test_GroundNav_Query_Raycast.cpp`
566 lines / 6 tests): ACCEPTED; its fixture deviations are all corrections of the brief against the
real field extents (the flat scene is one 800 uu tile; the deck/ground pair sits in one tile so layer
indices compare), and the early-out refusal test SEARCHES for a plate whose minimum clearance is
below the cap rather than hardcoding one, so a moved box cannot let it pass for the wrong reason.

## ADVERSARIAL REVIEW OF P1 — six findings, four confirmed by the orchestrator (2026-09-01, session 4)

A Fable-tier review agent was dispatched against the whole of CkGroundNav in a fresh context, with a
frontloaded brief (file inventory + every ruled decision quoted) so it never had to search. It read
~3,400 lines: all headers, all of `Field/`, `Portals.cpp`, the Volume processor, both Jolt backend
files, and `Rasterize.cpp`. It stopped at the P4/P5 boundary; `Walkability.cpp`, `Clearance.cpp` and
`Plates.cpp` are UNREAD by the review.

**NOTHING IS FIXED YET. Nothing is committed. The fix batch is the maintainer's call** — each fix is
small but any of them costs a rebuild and a re-gate, so they should land as ONE batch, not four.

### Confirmed by the orchestrator's own inspection (not taken on the agent's word)

**[P1-R1] MAJOR — `Get_AreProvablyDisconnected` violates its own stated contract.**
`Field/CkGroundNav_Field.cpp:172`. The guard catches only `INDEX_NONE` labels. A plate in a BUILT
tile adjacent to an UNBUILT one carries a perfectly valid label, so two plates on opposite sides of
an unbuilt tile get different labels and the function answers `true` — "provably disconnected" — when
the truth is "not built yet". The comment three lines above says refusing on a hole in the data is
exactly what it must not do. **This is concern (e) confirmed: [NN-D33] preserves unbuilt-vs-blocked at
TILE STATUS level, and the reachability API then throws the distinction away.**
The agent's fix (return `false` whenever `Get_BuiltTileCount() < Get_TileCount()`) is correct but
blunt — it disables the facility during any streaming build. The finer fix, and the one worth doing:
mark components touching an unbuilt tile edge as unprovable.

**[P1-R2] MAJOR — no EndPlay processor; a caller waiting on a mid-flight build is stranded.**
`Volume/`. Confirmed: four processors only (Setup / HandleRequests / StartBuild / Build), no
destroy-time drain. Three carry `TExclude<FTag_DestroyEntity_Initiate>`, which is HALF the pattern —
requests stop being processed on destroy, but `_PendingRequest`'s delegate and everything queued in
`_Requests` never fire. Breaks the framework's exactly-once completion guarantee. Fix: an
`FProcessor_GroundNavVolume_EndPlay` firing `Failed_Cancelled` on the pending request and every
queued one, and resetting the backend there.

**[P1-R3] MAJOR — `Request_ReleaseCompletedField` does not spend the build.**
`Field/CkGroundNav_FieldBuild.cpp:122`. It moves out of `_Partial` and never touches `_Status`, so
`Get_IsComplete()` stays true and a second call returns a NON-NULL, EMPTY field — which reads exactly
like a world whose tiles have no floor, the failure `Get_CompletedField`'s own doc names. `_Build` is
publicly readable via `CK_PROPERTY_GET`, so a consumer path can reach it. One-line fix: reset
`_Status` after the `MoveTemp`.

**[P1-R4] MINOR (but a real out-of-bounds read) — `Get_ReachabilityLabel` indexes past the end.**
`Field/CkGroundNav_Field.cpp:161`. `_TilePlateOffsets` holds `TileCount+1` entries, so
`IsValidIndex(TileCount)` PASSES and the next line indexes `[TileCount+1]`. Guard must be
`InTileIndex + 1 < _TilePlateOffsets.Num()`.

### Relayed but NOT verified by the orchestrator — both point at the same root

**[P1-R5] MAJOR (unverified) — a sliced build reads a MUTABLE static world.**
Tile N and tile N+1 bake on different frames against a Jolt world that nothing snapshots (the session
holds it weakly and re-reads per tile). If the static world mutates between slices — a streamed cell,
a runtime bake/remove — the two tiles disagree about their shared seam columns, `Get_MatchingStub`'s
exact-Z compare fails, and the seam portal SILENTLY VANISHES: the field then reads *blocked*, not
*unbuilt*, which is precisely the silent class [NN-D33] exists to prevent.

**[P1-R6] QUESTION (unverified) — the rasterize span tie-break is triangle-order-dependent.**
`Bake/CkGroundNav_Rasterize.cpp:113-118`. `DoInsert_Span` keeps the INCOMING span's normal and
`_IsWalkable` on an exact `_MaxZ` tie. Interval merging is confluent so `_MaxZ` is order-safe;
walkability is not. Two tiles query Jolt with DIFFERENT halo AABBs, and traversal order across
different boxes is not contractually identical, so the same seam column could rasterize walkable in
one tile's halo and demoted in the other's. Fix direction: break equal-`_MaxZ` ties on CONTENT
(prefer the walkable contribution, or order by quantized normal) rather than on arrival order.

**The root both share, and the most important thing this review produced: every exactness guarantee
P1 claims is pinned against the STUB backend.** `Tile_SeamAgreesWithAWiderBake`, the byte-identical
rebuild, `Budget_SlicedBakeMatchesOneShotBake` — all of them run on a backend that never mutates and
whose iteration order is fixed. **The Jolt path's determinism is ASSUMED, not asserted.** Closing that
is test-infrastructure work, not a patch, and it is the honest limit on what P1's green gate proves.

### Verdicts on the eight concerns put to the review

- **(a) halo/crop corner arithmetic — CLEAN.** `Do_MaskHalo`'s window and the halo shifts are
  consistent; `Get_TileIndex` range-guards so a last-column tile cannot wrap to the next row; and
  4-connectivity means no diagonal crossing exists to drop or double-count. A corner cell emits one
  stub per axis, each composed once.
- **(b) cap-clamp escape paths — CLEAN.** All three publish paths verified clamped: cropped cells
  (`TileBake.cpp:370`), tile portals (`:420`), seam stubs clamped at collection (`:172-176`), and seam
  portals take min-then-max over already-clamped stub values. The third path I worried about is safe.
- **(c) `TUniquePtr` backend inside an ECS fragment — SOUND.** Fragment pools are globally
  `in_place_delete`, so they are pointer-stable and never relocate; move-only is accepted by the
  tombstone pools. All Jolt reads sit inside the WaitForAsync→Step window, the session pins the world
  per call, and `BodyLockRead` is scoped inside the extraction — nothing is held across a frame. The
  real cross-frame hazard here is DATA CONSISTENCY, not thread safety: that is [P1-R5].
- **(d) epoch-as-SUM — SOUND TODAY, revisit later.** No per-tile rebuild path exists yet, so every
  tile carries the field epoch and the sum is trivially monotone; no consumer treats it as an
  identity. When partial rebuild lands, `(max, dirty-count)` or a hash becomes the honest identity.
- **(e) reachability honesty across Unbuilt — NOT HONEST.** See [P1-R1].
- **(f) [NN-D34]'s tile-granular slice budget — INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED; the review recommends NOT
  vetoing it.** The overshoot is genuinely bounded by one tile (budget checked before a tile starts),
  the at-least-one-tile-per-slice rule prevents a stall on a sub-tile budget, and per-tile probe
  counts cannot vary with slice size because geometry is fetched once per tile and no stage
  checkpoints. **The maintainer's veto window stays open, but the deviation now has a second opinion
  behind it.**
- **(g) the exact-integer reference pin — KEEP IT**, with one change: split the probe assertion from
  the plate assertion, so a probe-only change cannot ride the plate assertion for free. Its known
  weakness is that `ProbesSpent` counts loop iterations in several stages, so an innocent loop
  restructure re-pins it — acceptable only if every re-pin must explain its delta in the commit.
- **(h) Ck conventions — one break causing a real defect** ([P1-R2]'s missing EndPlay drain), and one
  cosmetic: `FProcessor_GroundNavVolume_Setup` (`Processor.cpp:86-97`) uses the doctrine-banned
  empty-ensure-body plus separate `if (NOT ...)` shape. Harmless as written, but it is the exact shape
  the root CLAUDE.md says not to write.

### ORCHESTRATOR PASS OVER `Walkability.cpp` — the review's largest gap, now read (2026-09-01, session 4)

Read in full by the orchestrator after the review stopped at the P4/P5 boundary. This file matters
more than its size suggests: [NN-D30] ruling 2 makes its connection field the SOLE definition of
adjacency for every stage downstream.

**[P1-R7] MAJOR (design) — `ProbesSpent` is a heterogeneous unit that does not track cost, and the
slice budget is denominated in it.**
`Bake/CkGroundNav_Walkability.cpp:378` sets this stage's probe count to
`InOutSpans.Get_TotalSpanCount()` — one probe per span. But the stage's actual work is
`DoFilter_Ledges` (spans x 4 directions x neighbour column height) plus `DoBuild_Connections`
(spans x 4 directions x neighbour column height, TWICE — build then mutual-agreement pass). A column
holding six layers costs many times what a flat one does, and the reported number is identical.
Consequences, in the order they bite:
1. **[NN-D34]'s budget is denominated in a unit that does not measure work.** "At most N probes per
   slice" gates tile STARTS on a number that under-reports a layered tile's true cost by a large
   factor, so the budget will behave very differently on a multi-storey scene than on a flat one.
   This does NOT invalidate [NN-D34]'s determinism argument — the count is still exactly reproducible
   per tile — but it does undercut the budget's usefulness as a COST control.
2. **Different stages denominate probes differently** (rasterization counts triangle work, this stage
   counts spans), and `DoBake_Field` sums them. The aggregate is a mixed unit.
3. **Concern (g) is affected**: `Test_GroundNav_ReferenceNumbers.cpp` pins probes=117962, a number
   that is stable and reproducible but is not measuring what its name claims.
Recommendation: keep the pin (it still catches drift), but either rename the field to something
honest about being a reproducibility token rather than a cost, or make the stages agree on a unit
before PHASE_3 tries to budget against it.

**[P1-R8] MINOR — undocumented sort invariant.** `DoFilter_LowClearance` computes headroom as
`Column[Index + 1]._MinZ - Span._MaxZ`, which is only correct if every column is sorted ascending by
Z. Nothing asserts it and no comment states it. Rasterization appears to maintain it; the invariant
should be written down (or ensured) since three stages now depend on it.

**[P1-R9] MINOR — an exact-tie step prefers the LOWER span, silently.**
`Get_ConnectableSpanIndex` breaks ties with `Delta >= BestDelta`, so on an exact tie the
earlier-in-column (lower Z) candidate wins. Deterministic and therefore not a correctness bug, but
"on an exact tie you step DOWN rather than up" is a real semantic that is nowhere stated.

**[P1-R10] amplifies [P1-R6]'s severity, and Fable's cap==0 note.** `DoFilter_Ledges` treats an
OUT-OF-LATTICE neighbour as SUPPORT (`Walkability.cpp:231-233` — the invalid-column `continue`
does not increment `DroppingSides`). That is consistent with [NN-D27]'s "the field border counts as
blocked" (an obstacle IS something to stand against), and it is harmless for tiles **only because
the ledge filter has a 1-cell radius and the halo is at least 1 cell** — so a tile's own cells are
never on the lattice rim. **If `_MaxClearanceUu` is 0, `HaloCells` is 0**, every tile cell lands on
the rim, and the tile border is then BOTH under-demoted for ledges AND emits zero seam stubs. So the
cap==0 guard the review filed as minor is protecting two failure modes, not one. `Get_AreParamsValid`
should require a strictly positive cap.

### Questions the read RESOLVES (no finding)

- **The mutual-agreement pass is safe despite mutating in place.** A directed edge is written only by
  its own (cell, direction) visit, so a clear can never invalidate a check that already passed, and a
  clear on one end correctly forces the clear on the other. Verified by case analysis over visit
  order, not assumed.
- **`DoFilter_Ledges` really is order-independent**, as [NN-D26] claims: `Get_HasSideSupport` reads
  GEOMETRY (`_MaxZ`/`_MinZ`) and never `_IsWalkable`, and demotions are collected then applied. A
  demotion cannot change a verdict the scan has not reached.
- **`Get_HasSideSupport`'s two-sided bound is correct and well-reasoned** — the lower bound makes it
  a FALL test rather than a height-difference test, and the upper bound stops a floor overhead from
  counting as support.
- **The `SurvivingCount` return is a DIRECTED edge count** (every mutual edge increments twice), which
  agrees with `Get_TotalConnectionCount`'s convention. Consistent, and the value is discarded by
  `DoFilter_Walkability` anyway.

### What this review did NOT cover

`Walkability.cpp`, `Clearance.cpp`, `Plates.cpp`, Volume `Utils`, `AgentProfile`, `Fingerprint`,
`GeometryBatch`, `SpanField`, `Layers`, `BakeTypes`, every test file, all debug/gym code, and PHASE_0
(`NavSurface/**` + the Recast adapter, scoped out as shipped in an earlier phase). A second pass over
`Walkability.cpp` is the highest-value remaining read, since [NN-D30] ruling 2 makes its connection
field the SOLE definition of adjacency for everything downstream.

### TEST A (PIE, tuning range) — field bake RUNS; the blue boxes were hidden by a gym bug (2026-09-01, session 5)

The maintainer ran the continuation prompt's Test A and saw no tile boxes. The PIE log
(`Saved/Logs/CkPlugins-backup-2026.09.01-22.32.13.log`, lines 3702-3881) shows why: Y DID bake a tiled
field, but the gym's T key called `DoBake()` — a REGION bake — on every mode step, so the field was
replaced before draw mode 5 was reached. Mode 5 then drew a region snapshot, which has no tiles. The
prompt's step order ("press Y, then T until mode 5") could never have worked as written.

**Fix (uncommitted, CkTests):** `Script/CkGroundNav/CkGroundNavGym_TuningRange_PlayerController.as`
now remembers the kind of the last bake; T and the seven tunable keys re-run THAT kind via `DoRebake()`.
R still forces a region bake, Y a field bake, O (reset) a region bake. Labels updated. AS-only, no
rebuild. Test A must be re-run: **Y, then T to mode 5** (or T to mode 5 first, then Y).

**The two summary blocks the log holds, verbatim** (region = R at 22:30:03; field = Y at 22:30:44):

| | region (R) | tiled field (Y, 800 uu tiles) |
|---|---|---|
| region | centre (200, 0, 120) half-extent (1500, 1500, 400) | centre (300, 100, 120) half-extent (1600, 1600, 400) |
| lattice | 120 x 120 at 25 uu, 2 layers | **0 x 0** (not reported for fields — see gap below) |
| geometry | 204 triangles in, 0 dropped | **0 in** (not reported) |
| walkable cells | 15693, 763 REJECTED | 17677, **0 REJECTED** (not reported) |
| plates | 25 (627.7 cells/plate) | 56 (315.7 cells/plate) |
| portals | 29 crossings, 1 change floor, tightest 25.0 uu | 36 crossings, 0 change floor, tightest 25.0 uu |
| tiles | 0 of 0, 0 seams | **16 of 16 built, 50 seams** |
| clearance | 775.0 uu | 200.0 uu (saturates at the cap, [NN-D32]) |
| time | 8.3 ms | 23.6 ms |

**Verdicts.**
- **All 16 tiles built, 50 seams > 0.** The seam pipeline runs on real Jolt geometry. No RED tile can
  have been seen yet — mode 5 never drew a field — so that half of Test A is still owed.
- **Walkable cells AGREE EXACTLY.** The field snaps the region up to whole tiles (3000 -> 3200 uu per
  axis, 128 x 128), and the level's ground plane floors both lattices entirely. Region residual over a
  full layer: 15693 - 14400 = **1293**. Field residual: 17677 - 16384 = **1293**. The non-floor content
  (platform deck, catwalk, stair tops) is identical cell for cell, so the halo neither leaks into the
  published tile nor lets the crop eat real cells. This is the "close?" question of Test A, answered
  from the log rather than by eye.
- **Plates 56 vs 25** — expected; tile borders split plates, 16 tiles.
- **"1 change floor" vs "0" — most likely NOT a defect, unverified.** Layers are tile-local ([NN-D31]:
  a cross-layer crossing needs a SELF-OVERLAPPING component). In the region bake the floor, the stairs,
  the platform and the catwalk are one self-overlapping component, so the split produces one cross-layer
  crossing. In the tile holding the top step (X 300..1100) the stairs' foot lies outside the halo, so
  the stair+platform component never touches the floor component and simply lands in layer 1 with no
  crossing to split. Same connectivity, different layer bookkeeping. To confirm: mode 4 after Y should
  show the same crossings as after R minus the mast.
- **Reporting gap (cosmetic, C++):** `Make_DebugSnapshotFromField` never fills `_LatticeSizeX/Y`,
  `_SourceTriangleCount`, `_SpanCount` or `_RejectedCellCount`, so a field summary reads
  "0 x 0 columns, 0 triangles, 0 rasterized, 0 REJECTED". Follow-up, not in the fix batch (needs a
  rebuild).

**RE-RUN AFTER THE GYM FIX — PASS (2026-09-01, session 5).** With the fixed gym, Y then T to mode 5
drew the 4 x 4 lattice of BLUE tile boxes (all built; the unbuilt colour is a red-brown box and none
appeared) with thick seam lines on every interior edge. Seam colour is clearance, not status: RED =
the widest seams (open floor), BLUE = the tightest (beside the stairs and the pinch). The 1D
`[EDITOR-VERIFY]` for the tiled bake is CLOSED.

**Cross-run reproducibility on the JOLT path — a data point, not a closure of [P1-R5]/[P1-R6].** Seven
field bakes across two editor processes (one at 22:30:44 in the rotated log, six at 22:38:22-25 in
`Saved/Logs/CkPlugins.log`) all reported identical numbers: 17677 walkable, 56 plates, 36 portals,
16/16 tiles, 50 seams. That is the Jolt backend agreeing with itself across boots on a static scene.
It says nothing about a world that mutates between slices ([P1-R5]) and only weakly about halo-AABB
traversal order ([P1-R6]) on this one scene; the gap stays recorded.

## Blockers

- **[P6-B1] — facade point generation for F2.11 (OPEN, maintainer).** The neutral facade has no point-generation capability
  (`CkNavSurface_Utils.h:34-245`, 14 entries); the generators are GroundNav-native (`Query/CkGroundNav_Query_Points.h:25,37`).
  The PerfLab survey (`CkPerfLab_WorldSurvey_Builder.cpp`) therefore stays on its fixed lattice through the facade's
  projection (F2.11 recorded DONE-ALREADY under [NN-D80] F6). Options: (a) fold two generator capabilities (random in
  radius, random by path distance) into the [P5-B1] ruling as entries 15–16; (b) leave PerfLab on the lattice for good and
  strike the generator clause of F2.11. Recommendation (a), ruled together with [P5-B1].

- **[P5-B2] OPEN — MAINTAINER RULING NEEDED for 5D row 1 (an editor-world GroundNav field).** Everything else an editor-world
  volume needs already works; the one blocker is that no Jolt static world exists in an `EWorldType::Editor` world (the
  two CkJolt subsystems inherit `Game || PIE`, the static sweep runs from `OnWorldBeginPlay`, `FProcessor_JoltStaticActor_*`
  is `RuntimeOnly`). Options: (7a-1) extend CkJolt to editor worlds behind an opt-in project setting — a CkJolt world-lifetime
  decision; (7a-2) a second `ICk_GroundNav_GeometryBackend` reading UE collision directly in the editor — a second geometry
  source whose bake is not byte-identical to PIE's, with its own world-revision contract; (7c) leave the editor snap on
  Recast and carry "a non-Recast provider can answer the editor snap" as a P8 retirement precondition. **Recommendation:
  7a-1 behind an opt-in** — one geometry source, one bake. Until ruled, 7c stands ([NN-D75]).

- **[P5-B1] OPEN — MAINTAINER RULING NEEDED before 5C row (a) and the oracle's fallback half can move: the facade has no
  synchronous path query.** Verified on `scratchpad/design_5C5D_PathNetworkReconcile_A7.md` §1.6: `FCk_NavSurface_ProviderTable`
  (`CkNavSurface_ProviderTable.h:20-77`) holds 14 capabilities, none a path; the only path query is
  `UCk_Utils_GroundNavPath_UE::Request_FindPath` (`CkGroundNavPath_Utils.h:51-69`) — deferred, entity-scoped, frame-sliced,
  GroundNav-namespaced — while `Resolve_OffPathLeg` (`CkPathNetwork_Processor.cpp:168-333`, called `:1323`) and
  `Try_ResolveNavmeshSegment`'s detour fallback (`:410`, inside a per-segment loop) are synchronous and return inside one
  `DoHandleRequest` pass. FEATURE_MATRIX F2.3(a) (`:1821`) presumes a facade path query that does not exist. PHASE_5 §5's
  gate says "the oracle needs a query the facade does not expose → STOP; do not add a facade capability; the twelve are
  frozen by [NN-D8]" (the count is stale — the table holds 14 after [NN-D66]'s markup trio and [NN-D70]'s settled). Options:
  (1a) a 15th capability `_FindPathSync(world, query) -> FCk_Nav_PathResult`, bounded by a node budget — Recast = the
  existing `FCk_Nav_Algorithm::FindPathSync`, GroundNav = a one-shot A* + funnel over the published field (the search and
  funnel exist; the deferred processor is a slicer over the same machinery); (1b) restructure the PathNetwork follower into a
  multi-frame episode over `Request_FindPath` — changes the request shape, the per-frame budget semantics (`:165-167`) and
  the 15 autotests' settle assumptions, against §10's "surgical in foreign modules"; (1c) leave the connector on Recast this
  campaign and migrate only the raycast half — narrows F2.3 and defers the retirement gate `MIGRATION_SEAM.md` §4; (1d) a
  synchronous GroundNav-only entry with a provider branch inside PathNetwork — the coupling the facade exists to prevent.
  **Orchestrator recommendation: (1a)**, with FORK-2 (`FindDistanceToWall`, `:812,:884`) admitted in the same ruling as a
  16th capability or reimplemented on `_BoundarySegments` (verdict equivalence unproven). The maintainer decides; 5C does
  not open on either half until then. 5A and 5B proceed meanwhile (they need nothing from the seam); a neutral LINK
  authoring request (design Fi7: no link capability exists on the table) is filed here too, owed before promotion.

- **[P2-R1] OPEN — P1 DEFECT found by the 2A fixture design: the clearance transform treats every
  walkable cell of a layer as free space, so a solid whose top is walkable does not depress the
  clearance of the floor beside it.** Evidence, verified by the orchestrator: `Bake/CkGroundNav_Clearance.cpp`
  seeds `IsWalkable = InLayers.Get_OccupancyAt(X, Y, Layer) > 0` and relaxes from any neighbour;
  `DoFilter_LowClearance` gives a column's TOPMOST span infinite headroom (`Walkability.cpp:190-192`),
  so a 300 uu wall's or pillar's top is walkable; layer extraction packs any component into the lowest
  layer whose footprint it does not intersect (`Layers.cpp:222-247`), and the floor has no walkable
  cell under the solid (its underside wins the tie, [NN-D37]), so the wall-top island lands in the
  FLOOR's layer; the transform then sees those cells as free. Consequence: `clearance` beside any
  wall, pillar, crate or platform edge is NOT "distance to the nearest cell an agent cannot stand on"
  (F1.5) — the floor reads open right up to the wall, so a fat body is admitted flush against it, and
  a corridor between two solids reads no pinch at all. Why every P1 gate is green: every clearance and
  portal fixture (`Test_GroundNav_Clearance.cpp`, `Test_GroundNav_Portals.cpp`) models a wall as
  ABSENT floor (a hole), never as a raised solid; the reference scene has real walls and a pillar and
  its comment "so clearance varies away from every wall" is false, but nothing pins those values. At
  the default ledge sensitivity 1.0 a thin wall top is demoted and the defect hides; any top wider than
  two cells keeps an interior island and the defect returns — every crate, every platform.
  **Recommended fix (a Clearance-stage change, so PHASE_2 §9 makes it a STOP):** relax only across
  CONNECTED neighbours — a from-cell contributes as free space only when the connection field links
  the two spans (diagonals through either L-path), otherwise it contributes as a blocked cell — which
  is Recast's erosion semantics (every boundary erodes, ledges included) and keeps "the connection
  field is the only adjacency" ([NN-D30] r2). `DoCompute_Clearance` gains the span and connection
  fields; the reference probe pin moves by the connectivity reads and is re-pinned with the delta
  explained; the existing hole-only fixtures are unaffected; three new fixtures pin it (raised box
  beside a floor depresses clearance; a curb under the step height does not; a step over it does, on
  both sides). Known limitation kept: a cross-layer crossing already reads a false pinch on the layer
  the ramp leaves, before and after this fix. **Maintainer: "Yes to fixing the clearance transform now"** — fixed under [NN-D47] in the same
  batch as 2A (uncompiled until the editor is free). The 2A projection admission test probes the
  hole rim, where clearance was already correct.
- **[P2-B1] OPEN — the shared Recast-agreement fixtures cannot be both isolated and Recast-visible
  in the AutoTests level as it stands.** Found by the session-7 research unit, verified in its
  citations: the level ships exactly ONE `NavMeshBoundsVolume_1` reaching roughly |x|,|y| ≤ 1000
  around the origin (asserted in four modules' tests, e.g. `CkAutoTest_Crowd_Stall_UnreachableGoalFailsBounded.as:19-21`,
  `CkAutoTest_NavSurface_RaycastCrossesFloorAndStopsAtEdge.as:12-13`); every isolation band in the
  suite (3000…102000) lies outside it, and no AngelScript API can add or move nav bounds at runtime.
  Every existing NavSurface autotest reads the pre-baked origin floor; `Request_SetActorNavigationRegistered`
  has zero `.as` call sites; no test in the suite has ever put one actor into both Recast and Jolt.
  Options:
  (a) **Origin-floor agreement (no content change).** The level floor `StaticMeshActor_1` IS geometry
      both engines can see: Recast already has it; a test pushes it into Jolt for its own duration
      (`utils_jolt_static_world::Request_BakeActor` / `Request_RemoveActor` at teardown), bakes a
      GroundNav volume over `Get_SurfaceBounds()`, and samples both paths at N random points and
      segments inside the bounds. Measures projection height, walk-to-rim endpoints, raycast-to-rim
      hits, and boundary segments = the floor rim. Does NOT measure interior walls or holes. Risk: a
      transient Jolt body at the origin during the test (removed at teardown).
  (b) **A second `NavMeshBoundsVolume` in `AutoTests_CkTests_Level` over an isolation band** (e.g.
      centred (0, 110000, 0), 6000×6000×1000) — a one-time editor content edit only the maintainer can
      make. Unlocks shared actor fixtures with walls and holes (spawn `AStaticMeshActor` + BlockAll +
      `Request_BakeActor` + `Request_SurfaceRebuild_ForTesting`) for P2 AND for PHASE_3's shadow
      fixtures and PHASE_8's parity maps, which hit the identical constraint.
  (c) Place spawned fixtures at the origin — REJECTED: contaminates the busiest shared area (all seven
      NavSurface tests, every Crowd stall/avoidance test, PathNetwork's Y-sweep).
  **Orchestrator recommendation: (a) now for the step-0 budgets that a flat floor can measure,
  plus (b) as a maintainer action for the interior-obstacle budgets; the walk budget ("the tightest in
  the campaign") is recorded against (a) first and re-measured on (b) when it exists.** **Maintainer: "Yes to second navmeshboundsvolume"** — they place it (suggested centre (0, 110000, 0),
  half-extents 3000 x 3000 x 500) and save the level; the shared interior-obstacle fixtures are
  authored in that band once it exists. The three P1 [EDITOR-VERIFY] items: DEFERRED by the maintainer ("I am unable to test P1 yet, we need to defer it") — they stay owed in "P1 CLOSE-OUT". ### [NN-D29] Gate 1C-2 freeze — `PlaneFitToleranceUu` 10.0, `NormalConeDegrees` 10.0

**Recorded retroactively on 2026-09-01 (session 4).** The freeze was ruled and applied in session 3
but never entered this file — the continuation prompt carried it instead. That is a checkpoint-invariant
miss, not a new decision; the values below are re-verified against the shipped code, not re-derived.

- **`PlaneFitToleranceUu` = 10.0** (one cell height at the default config). Usable window is roughly
  `[1.0, shallowest riser)`. The floor at ~1.0 uu is normal-quantization drift: normals are int8, and
  a long ramp's fitted plane drifts ~0.979 uu from truth over 48 cells (Finding 4 below). At or above
  the riser height, treads merge and the step stops existing downstream (Finding 2 below).
- **`NormalConeDegrees` = 10.0.** Measured inert above ~3 degrees on every fixture, including the two
  built after the original sweep specifically to make it bite (`Make_RampMeetsLanding`,
  `Make_RollingGround` in `Test_GroundNav_Plates.cpp:128,157`). Normals that differ also diverge in
  height, so the plane-fit tolerance rejects the merge first. Below ~3 degrees it only fragments
  nominally-flat ground. Making the cone matter would be a change to *how the plane is fitted* — a
  design call, not a value, and out of scope for P1.
- **Finding 3's contradiction ruled:** Gate 1C-2's wording calls one-plate-per-tread a "shatter", but
  for a staircase that IS the correct decomposition, and PHASE_1's own acceptance criterion (12–24
  plates for the 12-tread fixture) agrees. Real shatter would be one plate per cell — 768 on that
  fixture, which occurs at no setting. The gate's shatter definition is what is wrong; the
  decomposition is not.

**Evidence re-verified this session:** `CkGroundNav_Plates.h:43,53` carry both defaults with the
rationale in the header contract; `Test_GroundNav_Plates.cpp` contains both post-sweep fixtures and
is green in the 36/36 gate of record.

### [P1-C1] Gate 1C-2 sweep — measured 2026-08-31, session 3

Fixtures: the 12-tread staircase (100 uu treads, 15 uu risers, 48x16 cells) and the 9-degree ramp
(48x16 cells), both at 25 uu cells / 10 uu cell height. Sweep as specified: tolerance
0.25-2.0 x cell height in 0.25 steps, cone 5-30 degrees in 5-degree steps. 48 pairs.

The grid collapses to four distinct rows, because two of the three swept dimensions turned out inert:

| tolerance | cone | stair plates | stair max residual | stair max range | stair collapse | ramp plates | ramp max residual | ramp collapse |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.50-12.50 | 5-30 (all) | 12 | 0.000 | 0.000 | 64.00 | 1 | 0.979 | 768.00 |
| 15.00-20.00 | 5-30 (all) | 6 | 7.500 | 15.000 | 128.00 | 1 | 0.979 | 768.00 |

**Finding 1 — the normal cone is entirely inert on the named fixtures, so this sweep cannot inform
it.** Every cone value from 5 to 30 degrees produces byte-identical output. The reason is structural,
not a bug: the staircase's treads are all flat and the ramp is a single plane, so neither fixture
contains any normal variation for a cone to admit or reject. **The fixtures named by PHASE_1 cannot
pin this value.** Pinning it needs a fixture whose surface normals actually vary — a ramp meeting a
flat landing, or a domed/curved surface. That is a fixture the phase does not specify, so adding one
is an orchestrator call, not an executor call.

**Finding 2 — the tolerance has exactly one transition, and it is at the riser height.** Below
15.00 uu the staircase is 12 plates (one per tread) with zero residual and zero height range. At and
above 15.00 uu, pairs of treads merge: 6 plates, 7.5 uu residual, and a 15 uu height range — a plate
claiming to be one surface across a full riser.

**Finding 3 — shatter and over-merge have NO overlapping window on this fixture, which Gate 1C-2
names as the condition that makes the merge criteria themselves an orchestrator decision.** With the
gate's own definitions, every tolerance below 15 "shatters" (one plate per tread) and every tolerance
at or above 15 over-merges (a plate spanning a riser). There is no pair that does neither.

**However, the executor's read is that the gate's shatter definition is the thing that is wrong, not
the decomposition.** For a staircase, one plate per tread IS the correct answer — the treads are at
different heights and there is genuinely nothing to merge across a riser. Ordinary "shatter" would be
one plate per CELL (768 here), which never occurs at any setting. PHASE_1's own acceptance criterion
agrees: it accepts 12-24 plates for this fixture, and 12 is inside that band. So the executor reads
tolerance 2.50-12.50 as the CORRECT window and 15.00+ as the failure, whereas Gate 1C-2's wording
reads both as failures. **This contradiction between two lines of the phase doc is exactly the fork
that returns to the orchestrator.**

**Finding 4 — a floor on the tolerance the sweep range does not reach.** The ramp holds at 1 plate
everywhere, with a 0.979 uu residual that comes from normal quantization: the seed cell's int8 normal
differs slightly from the ramp's true plane and the error accumulates over 48 cells. A tolerance
below roughly 1.0 uu would therefore shatter a long ramp. The swept range bottoms out at 2.50, so it
never sees this, but it constrains any future decision to tighten below the swept floor.

**Finding 5 — collapse ratio.** Staircase 64.0 cells/plate, ramp 768.0. The in-house volumetric
precedent is 91,752 -> 359 = 255.6. The ramp beats it and the staircase is well under, but both
fixtures are trivial and uniform; neither is evidence about a real scene. A generated-scene collapse
measurement is still owed, and per PHASE_1 a poor ratio is a PHASE_3 budget finding, not a
correctness failure.

**What the executor is NOT doing:** picking either value. Recording only. Note that
`FCk_GroundNav_MergeTunables` currently defaults to 0.0/0.0 (the illustrative values from PHASE_1),
which is the conservative end — it merges only what is already coplanar and identically oriented, and
produces the correct 12-plate staircase and 1-plate ramp. The shipped tests run at those defaults and
are green, so nothing is blocked from proceeding EXCEPT the freeze itself.

### PHASE_6 frontload — tuning-range gym (2026-08-31)

`CkTests/Script/CkGroundNav/CkGroundNavGym_TuningRange_{GameMode,PlayerController}.as`,
registered in `Common/CkTests_GymRegistry.as` as "GroundNav Tuning Range".

Scene is runtime-spawned boxes, every one of them pushed into the Jolt static world
via `utils_jolt_static_world::Request_BakeActor` — the GroundNav backend reads Jolt
and nothing else, so an actor Recast can see is still free space to this bake.

Each feature is sized so ONE tunable decides it:

| Feature | Tunable it answers |
|---|---|
| flat floor, 2400x2400 | trivial plate merge |
| 12 steps, 100uu run / 20uu rise | `PlaneFitToleranceUu` — 20 sits between the panel's 10 and 25 |
| platform at Z=240, 220uu headroom | layer split; `AgentHeightUu` past 220 collapses it to one |
| 75uu catwalk, 240uu drop both sides | `LedgeSensitivity` — narrowest thing the filter can erase |
| two pillars, 160uu gap | clearance — the only pinch in an open field |

Control panel owns the cvar values (pushes, never reads back) and re-bakes on every
change. Console typing still works; the panel just overwrites on the next keypress.

Verification: both `.as` files compiled clean under live AngelScript hot-reload in the
running editor (full reload, zero errors), and `CkTests_GymRegistry` then compiled
resolving the new GameMode. Published — CkTests `00e33ae9`, root `8fd8997`.

Still `[EDITOR-VERIFY]`: nobody has run `ck.GroundNav.Bake` yet. The Jolt extraction
path (`Get_StaticTrianglesInAABox`) has never executed outside the headless stub.

### RESOLVED, NOT A BUG — walkable-cell count vs the lattice ceiling (2026-09-01)

A live bake of the tuning-range gym reported:

    region   : centre (200, 0, 120)  half-extent (1500, 1500, 400)
    spans    : 66680 rasterized -> 63050 walkable cells (draw capped)
    layers   : 2
    plates   : 25 (collapse 2522.0 cells/plate, ...)

A 3000uu region at 25uu cells is a 120x120 = 14400-column lattice
(`Rasterize.cpp:157` sizes from `InRegion.GetSize()`; `Layers.cpp:187` copies it).
`Make_DebugSnapshot` counts cells with a `layer < LayerCount` / `Y < SizeY` /
`X < SizeX` loop, so with 2 layers the count cannot exceed 28800. It reported
63050 — 2.19x the ceiling. Note `66680 - 2702 rejected = 63978`, near 63050,
which suggests the figure is counting walkable SPANS rather than cells.

TAINTED: `_WalkableCellCount` and the `collapse N cells/plate` ratio derived
from it. Independently sourced and unaffected: layer count, plate count,
worst residual, worst height spread, clearance, triangle count.

RULED OUT by reading:
- Printf argument misalignment from the new region line — 20 specifiers, 20
  args, matching order; the region values themselves print correctly.
- Swapped cell size / cell height at the config call site —
  `CK_DEFINE_CONSTRUCTORS(..., _CellSizeUu, _CellHeightUu)` matches the
  brace-init at the call site.

RULED OUT by test: `Snapshot_CellCountCannotExceedTheLattice` (new, green in the
36/36 gate) asserts the ceiling on a synthetic 20x20 two-storey fixture and
PASSES — so the counting code is sound on synthetic geometry and the fault is
specific to the live Jolt path, or to the reading of the live numbers.

NEXT: the summary now prints `lattice : X x Y columns at N uu, L layer(s) ->
S cell slots`. One live bake puts that line beside the cell count and
discriminates. Not yet run.

Published: CkFoundation `3f12e6be9` · CkTests `d491f4b6` · root `9a81198`.

RESOLUTION: the anomalous bake ran at `CellSizeUu = 12.5`, not 25 — the cvar
push immediately preceding it in `CkPlugins-backup-2026.09.01-07.05.58.log`
reads `ck.GroundNav.Debug.CellSizeUu = "12.5"`. That is a 240 x 240 = 57600
column lattice, 115200 cell slots across 2 layers, and 63050 sits well inside
it. The ceiling was never violated. The 4x ratio between the two bakes is
exactly the 2x cell-size change squared.

The error was mine: I read the cell size off a SCREENSHOT of the panel taken
at a different moment than the log line I was analysing, and treated the two
as one bake. Nothing in the bake code was at fault.

Kept from the investigation, both worth having on their own merits:
- the `lattice` summary line, which makes cell size impossible to mis-read
  off a stale panel again — it is now printed by the same bake it describes;
- `Snapshot_CellCountCannotExceedTheLattice`, a real invariant regardless.

Corrected alongside it: an earlier claim that the level's own floor was
filling the bake region is also wrong. `geometry: 204 triangles` is exactly
the gym's 17 boxes x 12 triangles, so the region contains the gym scene and
nothing else, and the clearance ramp is not being swamped by outside floor.

**S10-3 — the three non-Tuning gyms explain themselves (2026-09-06, maintainer finding while walking Repair/Routing/Links):**
"what am I supposed to be doing, the draw-mode shortcuts don't exist, can't bake". Cause: the walkthrough lived only in the
station description, R/T/B were wired only in the Tuning gym, and Routing drew nothing until key 2. Fix (AS only, CkTests):
`CkGroundNavGym_Common.as` gains `Get_WalkthroughRows` (WHAT TO DO header + numbered steps) and a shared GROUND block
(`Get_GroundRows` / `Get_GroundKey`: R `Request_Rebuild`, T draw-mode cycle 0-7 with a Colours legend, B `ck.GroundNav.Clear`);
Repair/Routing/Links append both, carry `_DrawModeIndex` seeded from their old fixed mode, and renumber their row constants
(layout comments in each). Routing bakes a whole-volume picture on settle (was: only on key 2). Repair's nudge redraws the
picture in the same frame instead of through the publish-wait's ten-second ceiling (`Request_BakeActor` adds the moved body
synchronously - `CkJoltStaticWorld_Subsystem.cpp:1136-1137`) - the maintainer's "old top of the cube still shows after the
nudge" was that ceiling, expected for <=10s and now gone. CkTests `CLAUDE.md` gym paragraph updated. The editor soft-reloaded

**S10-4 walk findings (maintainer, 2026-09-06, PIE):**
- **Links: walkers stop on U-off (correct) and do not resume on U-on.** ROOT CAUSE, from the code: a goal that fails puts the
  agent in `FTag_CrowdAgent_GoalFailedHold`, and `CkCrowdAgent_HandleRequests_Processor.cpp:182-196` REJECTS a plain MoveTo
  to the same goal (within 20cm, no new correlation id, no `ForceRepath`) while that hold stands - the retry re-asks the very
  goal it failed towards, so it was refused and logged ("same failed-held goal without ... ForceRepath"). This is also what
  the old Links gym's "re-enable and it does not re-path until its next request (F8b)" was. FIX in the frame: the walker's
  `Request_MoveTo` takes `InForceRepath`; `Request_Retry` and `Request_Replan` pass true (a first leg / turn-round are new
  goals and need none). Obstacle and Markup's replan-after-publish ride on the same fix. Compile boot
  `S10-4-ForceRepath.log` 3/3 exit 0. Crowd behaviour itself is by design; not a crowd change.
- **Walk: W1's route through the pillars is jagged, with jogs in open floor.** ASSESSMENT (mechanism from
  `CkGroundNav_PathPostProcess.h`, not yet confirmed against the plates in PIE): the search answers a corridor of
  rectangular PLATES; the funnel emits an apex only where the string bends round a portal vertex, and with a slab cut into
  rectangles by four pillars those vertices are plate CORNERS that sit in open floor; the corner-offset pass then pushes each
  apex a radius (`_CornerOffsetK` = 1.0) along its bisector, which is the visible jog. Expected under the current pipeline,
  and a quality gap: no pass string-pulls across the UNION of the corridor's plates. Check: in the Walk gym press T to mode
  0 (plates) / 4 (portals) and see whether every kink sits on a plate corner. Candidate fix (a unit, not started): a
  line-of-sight shortcut pass after the corner offset - drop interior waypoints while the field's raycast between their
  neighbours is clear - or a portal-edge funnel that walks the shared EDGE rather than its endpoints.

**[NN-D103] Route quality joins the campaign (maintainer, 2026-09-06: "close to perfect product").** The jagged Walk route is
a unit now, not a follow-up. Reference read from D:\Repos\JoltNav (Jolt/Mercuna-derived middleware): (1) a funnel over
portal EDGES of a polygon mesh (`MerNavGraph2DQueryGround.cpp:120-260`) whose vertices are obstacle-derived, so the funnel alone
hugs real corners; (2) a raycast shortcut during FOLLOWING (`MerPathPointList2D.cpp` `UpdateStartInternal`: from the body to
the furthest visible waypoint, bounded by a shortcut distance, cost-aware via `averageCostMultiplier * distance`); (3) parabolic
curve smoothing with curvature thresholds (`MerCurveBuilder2D`, ~1900 lines) = their "dynamically generated smooth paths".
Our gap is of a different kind: rectangular plates put portal vertices in open floor, so our (correct) funnel bends at false
corners. Design pass dispatched (read-only Opus) -> `research/design_PathShortcut.md`: options A plan-time LOS shortcut,
B follow-time shortcut, C funnel/search change, D curve smoothing; cost bounds in our primitives; ANY wall-clock number
comes from a benchmark on the B4 metric + a C++ timing pin, never estimated.

**[NN-D104] Route quality ruled on `research/design_PathShortcut.md` (orchestrator, 2026-09-06).** The design corrected the
assessment: the funnel is correct; the jog is (1a) A* pricing legs portal-MIDPOINT to midpoint so the chosen plate chain
bends where a straighter chain exists (`CkGroundNav_PlatePortalGraph.h:160-170`, `.cpp:392-398`), (1b) `Get_Inset`
shrinking every portal by the agent radius even where a rectangle boundary ends in open floor (`Funnel.cpp:92-110`), and
(1c) `Get_CornerOffset` pushing a false apex a further radius with `Get_IsCornerAccepted` answering true in open floor
(`PathPostProcess.cpp:252-256`). JoltNav ships the same funnel, a one-cell inset (radius eroded at build), and a plan-time
cost-aware `StraightenPath` - which is option (A). RULINGS: F-1 (A) alone, (B) follow-time parked behind a default-off cvar
until the B4 benchmark; F-2 cost-awareness = the weighted-raycast budget (U1), not a plate-set test; F-3 C2 (funnel-aware
leg pricing) PARKED as a named decision - it changes the node key, the admissibility argument and every recorded search
budget; if (A) leaves routes jagged because the chosen chain never contained the straight line, C2 returns as a phase;
F-4 span cap: `0` = off, `MAX_int32` = unbounded, default unbounded, the benchmark argues otherwise; F-5 one field per agent
radius recorded against the profile-variant work; C1 (inset) PARKED - `Get_Inset` is shared with the flood fill and every
distance pin. **F-6 (orchestrator): the shortcut stage runs BEFORE the corner offset**, not after as designed - removing
false corners first is what guarantees the offset only ever moves REAL corners, and it retires (1c) without touching the
accept rule; link endpoints stay pinned through both. Units dispatched in parallel, disjoint files: U1 plate-cost-weighted
raycast (opt-in map on the raycast query; empty = byte-identical), U2 `Get_Shortcut` stage + `_ShortcutSpanCap`, U3 C++
pins (four-pillar slab fixture; no false corners; link endpoints survive; cost never lowered; idempotent) + the
count-pinning benchmark test on the B4 metric, U4 the AS pin mirroring the Walk gym scene. No wall-clock number is claimed
anywhere; the benchmark answers it.

**S10-5 units RETURNED (2026-09-06), uncompiled, awaiting U3 + the gate:**
- **Links "walkers rise into the sky" ROOT CAUSE (declared CAUSE):** `FTag_CrowdAgent_TraversingLink` has one remover
  (`Steering::DoCancelActiveLinkTraversal`), reachable only while the agent is Walking with a Ready route; four terminals
  leave Walking without ending the crossing (BlockDetect block + stall re-path, PathRefresh, the shared slot's Failed
  branch). With the tag latched, `ConstrainToNavmesh::DoConstrain` stood down entirely AND `MarkOnMesh()`d - an ungrounded
  3D body reported as grounded, gaining a +Z leg per toggle from the ladder's own steering direction and the trailing
  walker's push-apart; `StartProjectFailed` was the consequence (a body between a link's ends projects onto no cell). FIX
  (CkCrowd, +42/-9): the stand-down requires Walking + the tag; a tagged non-Walking agent has its crossing ended there and
  falls through to the grounded pass (recovers to the reachable link end - the foot mid-ladder, by the step-up cap); the
  cancel removes the tag above the correlator guard so tag and correlator cannot disagree. Behaviour change: a stall or
  disc re-path mid-climb now ends the crossing and drops to the foot instead of hovering. Repro AS
  `CkAutoTest_GroundNav_Link_DisabledMidCrossingHoldsTheBodyAndResumesOnEnable.as` (763 lines: begun signal + >=60uu up the
  ladder, disable, 120-frame hold asserting no climb / tag gone / on a link end / cancelled once, re-enable + forced
  MoveTo -> reached on the deck). `--discover-fresh` needed. Follow-up: `Source/CkCrowd/CLAUDE.md` contract line.
- **U1** (+72/-6): `_PlateCostMultipliers` on the raycast query, same type/index space as the search table; identity for
  an empty table traced through all four `kCostMultiplier` sites. **U2** (+246/-4): `Get_Shortcut` at
  `PathPostProcess.cpp:435-505`, order funnel -> link endpoints -> SHORTCUT -> corner offset -> skip-first -> fill -> stamp
  (`:717-748`), `_ShortcutSpanCap` (0 off, MAX unbounded default), budget = max merged multiplier x chord x (1+1e-5 slack
  for float/double rounding), idempotence proved for the unbounded cap only. **U2 found the units seam**: the ray priced
  table entries only while the budget used the merged plate price -> inert cap at runtime (empty table). ORCHESTRATOR
  RECONCILIATION (a checker's waypoint-only patch was rejected as leaving crossed-but-unnamed plates at 1.0):
  `_UseBakedPlateCost` on the raycast query, default false (byte-identical); when set an unnamed plate weighs its own
  baked `_CostMultiplier` and a named one max(baked, table) = `Get_AreaMultiplier`'s rule; the shortcut's ray sets it.
  **U4**: `CkAutoTest_GroundNav_Path_FourPillarRouteHasNoFalseCorners.as` (500 lines; closest-boundary is not bound to AS,
  so a 16-ray 93uu probe ring off `Try_SurfaceRaycast` stands in; length gate skips because pillar 0 stands on the lane).
  U2 also found the design's designated over-shortcut guard (`LCorridorThreeWaypointsAllClearOfBoundary`) runs the funnel
  directly and never the plan; a sibling through `Get_PathPlan` is owed in U3's file.

**S10-5 GATE 1 (`S10-5-Gate1-GroundNav.log` + five families, 2026-09-06): build clean; every red outside the two NEW pins is the
recorded baseline by name** (Crowd 136/133: OffMeshWalkerHolds + Queue_CrowdAdapter x2; Nav 578/574: the PathNetworkFollower pair
+ the two new pins; Queue 52/48: the four; CkTestsGym 3/3; CrowdDebugger 26/26). GroundNav 415/413: the two new pins red, both
diagnosed from their own reports:
- `FourPillar_RouteHasNoFalseCorners`: one false corner survived at (-401.6,-243), 269uu from any wall. CAUSE = my F-6 order
  ruling. Before the corner offset the funnel's apexes hug their walls at exactly one radius, so the chord between two of them
  passed pillar 2 at ~32uu (< the 34uu radius) and the radius-aware ray refused it; after the offset the same chord clears by
  ~60uu. **[NN-D104] F-6 REVERSED: the shortcut runs AFTER the corner offset, as designed.** A false corner the offset pushed
  out costs nothing - the shortcut drops it whole. Also folded in the review finding: the budget is now the replaced stretch's
  OWN cost under the fill's rule (per segment: XY length x the greater endpoint multiplier), not the dearest replaced
  multiplier x chord, which admitted a dearer chord than the detour it removed. Idempotence now holds to within the 1e-5 slack.
- `Link_DisabledMidCrossingHoldsTheBodyAndResumesOnEnable`: THE FIX WORKS - report line: begun=1 completed=1 Failed_Cancelled,
  interruptZ 61.1, maxHoldZ 65.4 (no climb), restZ 0.0 (the ladder foot), offNavmesh=false, goalFailures=0, and the walker
  resumed to Ready after the re-enable. The red was repro strictness: the tag was still up on the first held frame(s) before
  the grounding pass ended the crossing; the hold now judges the tag from the frame the completion signal fired. A second
  failed assertion in that step is unnamed by the harness; the report line now prints both correlators and the tag flag.
**S10-6 GATE (`S10-6-Gate1-GroundNav.log` + five families, 2026-09-06): build clean; GroundNav 415/414 - all five C++ pins green
(FourPillar_RouteHasNoFalseCorners, Shortcut_KeepsEveryLinkEndpoint, Shortcut_NeverLowersThePlateCostPaid, Shortcut_IsIdempotent,
Shortcut_RaycastCountIsStableAndRecorded) and the AS FourPillar pin green; Crowd 136/133, Nav 578/575, Queue 52/48, CkTestsGym 3/3,
CrowdDebugger 26/26 - every red outside the repro is the recorded baseline by name.** U3's four counts pinned from this run:
funnelled 5, bare pass 5, plan off 5, plan on 4 (the bare pass over the funnel drops nothing, the offset-first plan drops the corner).
The one red is the repro's LAST assertion, and it is a crowd defect, not strictness: report line `begun=1 completed=1
Failed_Cancelled interruptZ 61.3 maxHoldZ 66.3 restZ 0.0 offNavmesh=false traversingAfterEnd=false goalFailures=0` - the physical
fix holds (both correlators agree, the tag is gone after the ending), but the walker never reported its goal failed while the
deck was an island, although the log shows four `ground route failed` lines (No Start Surface, then Unreachable x3). CAUSE
(read, not inferred): PathRefresh's `[REBUILD-REPLAN]` re-plans a LIVE route without the PathPending tag (by design, so the
body keeps walking the stale polyline); `OnGroundNavPathResolved`'s Fail branch writes the Failed status and defers the tag
transition + the single OnGoalFailed to `OnPathResolved` - whose view REQUIRES PathPending. A failed repair was therefore
consumed by nobody: the body stood Walking on a Failed slot, no GoalFailedHold, no OnGoalFailed, the watchdog reading it as
live, and the gym's same-goal retry refused as the walking no-op (which is ALSO why the Links gym's U re-enable never
resumed before ForceRepath). FIX (CkCrowd): (1) the Fail branch steps Walking -> PathPending when the tag is absent, the same
step every other re-plan takes, so the owner consumes the failure next frame (strict -> permissive retry included, then
Idle + hold + one OnGoalFailed); (2) `[REBUILD-REPLAN]` now also requires Walking - the installed identity and provider
outlive the episode, so an arrived or goal-failed agent was re-planned on every rebuild for the seam to drop, and a fresh
plan in flight was superseded by a "repair" of the previous goal (bonus, undo = drop the one `Has<Walking>` term). Repro:
`Get_ExpectedLogErrors` registers the owner's `(path failed:` Warning like `Crowd_GroundNav_NoRouteFailsClean` does, and the
report line now lists every failure reason heard. Doc: the rebuild re-plan paragraph in `Source/CkCrowd/Claude.md`. Open
ruling for the maintainer: a repair dispatched while the body is MID-CROSSING plans from off-surface and reports
`StartProjectFailed` rather than the island's `FindPathNoPath`; deferring the repair until the crossing ends would give the
honest reason but leave the body climbing a disabled link to its far end - not taken here.

**S11 REVIEW of the pushed S10-4..S10-8 batch (workflow `wf_74e5b0dd-040`, read-only: 5 lenses x 6 findings, 2 refuters per finding, critic; 62 agents): 20 CONFIRMED (both refuters), 8 CONTESTED (one refuter, always on materiality), 0 refuted.** Orchestrator-confirmed by reading before any ruling: (1) [crowd/bug] the S10-6 'bonus' Walking term in `[REBUILD-REPLAN]` is dead - PathRefresh's view (`_Processor.h:47-58`) already requires Walking - and the doc/commit/PROGRESS claim of an arrived-agent re-plan describes a state that never existed; the REAL defect is pre-existing: `FTag_GroundNavPath_RepathRequired` latches on a non-Walking agent (raised by the invalidator with no movement gate, consumed only under the Walking view), fires as a spurious Repair on the NEXT episode, and the install seam's dedup guard (`OnGroundNavPathResolved.cpp:99-107`, same epoch + goal) DROPS that repair's answer, so the slot stays Pending, Steering zeroes velocity, the watchdog reads Walking-without-PathPending as live: an agent idle through a rebuild FREEZES on its next MoveTo (no test covers idle->rebuild->MoveTo). (2) [crowd/risk] a repair HALTS the walker for its latency: `MarkPathPending` parks the slot, Steering (`:76-81`) zeroes velocity on any non-Ready/Partial status; the batch's doc says it keeps walking. (3) [as-scripts/bug] the Obstacle gym arms its overlay refresh AFTER the repair advanced the epoch, so redraw + `Request_ReplanAll` wait for the 10 s poll ceiling (Markup arms at the request, correctly). (4) [groundnav/bug x2] three pricing rules for one chord (budget Dist2D x endpoint-max; ray per-cell; fill `Get_LegCost` 3D+slope) and no slope/3D term anywhere in the shortcut - a design ruling, dispatched as a read-only design pass -> `research/design_ShortcutBudgetUnits.md`. (5) `_ShortcutSpanCap` has no fragment/processor/cvar plumbing (unlike `_CornerOffsetK`): the pass cannot be switched off or bounded at runtime. (6) test-strength: IsIdempotent runs on a no-op input; KeepsEveryLinkEndpoint cannot fail on the pinning rule (the wall refuses every chord); NeverLowers' 2nd assertion is a tautology; the count pin's name says raycasts; the AS pin's length step always skips; the repro's tag assertion reads the handshake, not the crowd tag; enable+MoveTo in one step (contested: one refuter traced the order deterministic - kept as hardening). (7) hygiene: Tuning Range header names the deleted gyms, Obstacle prose 300 vs 150uu, Common.as orphans (6 helpers with only their definition), Links verdict 'crossed' = routed, slack comment, stand-down bullet 'only ender', 'converges from arbitrary state' (the reach bound is the real limit). CRITIC: (a) a zero-cost replaced stretch (`Get_ReplacedStretchCostUu` = 0 for XY-coincident points) sets `_MaxCost = 0`, which SurfaceWalk reads as cap OFF (`:661`) - the chord is admitted unbounded instead of refused; (b) the two other raycast callers (facade adapter, debug draw) keep the pre-batch semantics by design (opt-in); (c) the generator re-emits a hand-authored wrapper whenever the test file fails to compile (detection is by live UClass) - filed. DISPOSITION: fix workflow `wf_cfc9be23-488` running with four disjoint units (crowd: dead term + dispatch-time clear + Steering follows a Pending slot while Walking-without-PathPending + Claude.md; plumbing: `_ShortcutSpanCap` fragment/processor/cvar + cap<2 = off + doc; gyms: Obstacle arming at request, prose, Tuning Range header, Common orphans, Links wording; tests: the six pins + `UCk_Utils_CrowdAgent_UE::Get_IsTraversingLink`), each unit reviewed read-only against its brief. After it: the dedup-guard residual (a slot parked Pending is WAITING for that answer - do not drop it), the zero-budget refusal, a new AS pin idle->rebuild->MoveTo, then chain S11-2. Filed, not fixed: the repro does not discriminate the stand-down guard (its crossing is ended by the pre-existing Fail-branch cancel) - a scenario where a stall/disc re-path ends Walking mid-climb is owed; the Steering per-frame `Try_Remove` nit.

**[NN-D105] Shortcut budget units ruled on `research/design_ShortcutBudgetUnits.md` (orchestrator, 2026-09-06, session 11).** The design corrected the finding's framing: the FILL (`Get_FilledWaypoints` via `Get_LegCost`) already carries 3D length and the slope penalty - it is the budget and the ray that were never taught them - and stated the defect in one line: the chord's cost function, the budget's summand and the published cost are three different functions, so the header's idempotence lemma quantifies over the wrong one and holds only where every replaced multiplier is equal, which is exactly why the six shipped pins are green (all run at `_SlopePenaltyK = 0` on uniformly priced ground). Worked examples: a dear plate under a chord's far endpoint admits the chord at 1130 and bills it at 3800 (+100%), and a second run drops a point the first kept; on `Bake_RampVsLevelScene` at `kSteepSlopePenaltyK = 8` the pass hands back the leg the search refused at 1.82x its cost. RULINGS: F-1 ship **O5** = O2's per-segment ray budget (each replaced segment cast once per pass, cached; fallback to endpoint-max x Dist2D where a segment's own ray is not clear, the fallback COUNTED on the report line) PLUS a fill-metric conjunct (the chord's `Get_LegCost` at max endpoint multiplier <= the stretch's `Get_LegCost` sum) - both conjuncts are 'one function both sides over an additive budget', so idempotence holds by the additivity argument and `_CostFromStart` is monotone by construction; O1 rides inside the fill conjunct for free; F-2 O3 (per-cell slope inside the raycast) PARKED - it makes the ray stricter than the fill and opens a second mismatch unless the budget moves with it; F-3 O4 (chord priced by endpoints only) stays REJECTED with the design's proof: on the priced-band fixture it accepts the 700uu chord straight through the 100x plate and the pin naming the property goes GREEN while the property is false; F-4 a zero budget REFUSES the chord (a `_MaxCost` of 0 is 'no cap' to SurfaceWalk:661); F-5 the lemma at `PathPostProcess.h:182-188` is rewritten to name its two functions; F-6 cost bound recorded as counts only: (n-1)(n-2)/2 + (n-1) casts per pass, <= 10 at the pinned n = 5, once per completed search. New pins: `Shortcut_CostNeverRisesWhenAnEndpointStandsOnDearGround` (four-pillar slab + a priced plate under the east post; cost monotone + idempotence under a non-uniform table - red before, green after) and `Shortcut_RespectsTheSlopePenalty` on a new `Make_RampGapPillarScene` (RampVsLevel + one 150uu-tall box in the east gap; its geometry precondition is asserted so the pin cannot pass vacuously). Counterargument recorded: unfalsifiable on shipping fixtures and n-1 extra casts per completed query; accepted because the contract (lemma + the NeverLowers pin's name) is false as shipped and the demo gyms already ship markup and slope. Unit dispatched (S11-2 batch).

**S11-8 OPENED (2026-09-06 ~12:50 PDT): the §3.D follow-ups, ruled before dispatch (orchestrator read every file named below first).**
- D.8 DONE on disk (ungated): `CkGroundNavGym_Demo.as` `_LinkIdsCrossed` -> `_LinkIdsRouted`, `Get_LinkIdsEverCrossed` -> `Get_LinkIdsEverRouted` (+ the two Links PlayerController call sites); the comment says a plan that NAMES a link, not a body that finished walking it.
- **[S11-D106] O2 discriminator pin = SWEEP-SELECTED on the shared four-pillar slab, named `Shortcut_BudgetSeesGroundNoWaypointStandsOn` (the design's name).** Derivation: with plates priced m=4 and the endpoints on 1.0 ground, the retired endpoint-max arithmetic refused a chord iff `ray(near->far) > |near-dropped| + |dropped-far|` (plain length x 1.0), and O5 admits it iff `ray(near->far) <= ray(near->dropped) + ray(dropped->far)` (fill conjunct aside). Both hold together whenever the chord crosses SOME dear ground the detour crosses at least as much of - a per-(corner, tile) coin flip on this slab (S11-6 measured the losing side by 0.47 on tile 7). So the pin sweeps every interior non-pinned corner of the uniform-table pre-shortcut polyline x probe fractions {0.25, 0.5, 0.75} along its chord, prices the probed plate (excluding the plates under near/dropped/far) at 4x, casts the three uncapped rays, and takes the FIRST (corner, plate) satisfying both inequalities with >= 1 uu margin; it then asserts `Get_Shortcut({near, dropped, far}, {}, ...)` under that table answers TWO points (the chord taken), reports every candidate row `[SHORTCUT-O2]`, and FAILS LOUDLY (not skips) when the sweep is empty. A purpose-built scene is NOT built unless the sweep is empty (the executor STOPs with the table; the orchestrator rules then). Why not a scene first: the slab's real route hosts the case if any (corner, tile) pair has the right sign, and the sweep's table is the diagnostic either way.
- **[S11-D107] D.3 stand-down guard repro = a lateral SHOVE mid-climb.** `FProcessor_CrowdAgent_BlockDetect` views Walking + HasProbe with NO TraversingLink exclusion (`_Processor.h:27-39`); its off-path tier (`.cpp:~478-513`, threshold 300 cm, sampled every 0.5 s) calls `DoRepathAtActiveGoal` which REMOVES Walking and adds PathPending (`.cpp:~585-586`) while leaving the TraversingLink tag standing - exactly the "every OTHER way out of Walking" the guard at `ConstrainToNavmesh.cpp:~205-215` exists for. The install seam cancels a stale crossing when the answer lands (`OnGroundNavPathResolved.cpp:~132-134`), so the DISCRIMINATOR is ORDER: the crowd tag (`utils_crowd_agent::Get_IsTraversingLink`) clears while `utils_nav::Get_PathStatus` still reads Pending (guard) rather than on the frame it turns Ready (install). Scenario: the mid-crossing deck + ladder fixture; once the body is up the ladder, `Request_SetTransform` it 400 uu sideways onto the FLOOR (past the threshold, on walkable ground, no Request_Stop); sample every frame; assert (1) the crossing ends with the slot Pending, (2) Z never rises above the shove point + slack until Walking resumes, (3) the walker resumes and arrives, (4) the handshake reports the crossing abandoned. If the answer lands the SAME frame the tag clears, the executor STOPs with the frame trace (the guard may still have run first within the frame; the orchestrator rules, possibly by a `ck.Crowd.*` cadence knob rather than a redesign). New file `Script/CkGroundNav/CkAutoTest_GroundNav_Link_ShovedMidCrossingEndsTheCrossingAndResumes.as`; generated wrapper; `--discover-fresh`; its actor joins the next actor commit.
- **[S11-D108] D.6 = DETECT BY SOURCE in `FCkAutoTestWrapperGenerator`, live-UClass check kept first.** Once per `GenerateAll` pass: `FCkAsSourceScanner::Enumerate_AsSourceFiles(Get_DefaultScanRoots())` (already excludes Generated/), a cheap regex over each file for `class\s+(A\w+_Actor)` candidates, each confirmed comment-aware by `Parse_ClassDeclaration(contents, "A<Name>_Actor", path)` (the scanner blanks comments and strings), into a `TSet<FString>` of bare wrapper names; a test whose wrapper is declared in source is skipped with the path logged even when no live UClass answers. Unit-tested with temp files under `FPaths::ProjectIntermediateDir()` (the scanner tests' idiom: a real declaration, a commented-out one, one inside a string literal, one under a `Generated/` dir). Doc lines in the module's Claude.md collision rule and CkTests CLAUDE.md. Why: the S10-7 loop (a failed boot leaves the generated entry beside the hand-authored one; a class-table check cannot see a wrapper whose file did not compile) closes only if the source itself is the authority.

**[S11-D110] O2 pin gets its purpose-built scene: `Make_DearBandBendScene` = the four-pillar slab's floor with NO pillars (X +-1800, Y +-1200, tiles 800: 5 columns x 3 rows), baked with Make_QueryParams.** The bend is BOUGHT IN THE SEARCH: the query's own `_Cost._PlateCostMultipliers` prices the middle-row tile on the direct line (col 2, row 1) at 100 (the search honours the query table: `CkGroundNav_PathSearch.cpp:304` -> `CkGroundNav_PlatePortalGraph.cpp:234`), so A* detours through row 0 and the funnel bends at that tile's two south corners (portal apexes at (-200,-400) and (600,-400), pushed by the corner offset). The chord across the priced tile is walkable and, in the POST table, cheap - which is the point: the post table prices only the BAND (col 1, row 1), which the detour's first segment crosses more steeply than the chord does. Arithmetic at posts W(-1650, 380) / E(1650, 380): segment W->A1 crosses the band at ~29 deg (841 uu of it), the chord W->A2 at ~19 deg (849 - but 73 uu shorter overall), so `c3 - (c1 + c2) = (L_c - L_d) + 3(X_c - X_d) ~ -73 + 24 = -49` (ray budget ADMITS by ~49) while `c3 - plain detour ~ 3 x 849 - 73` (endpoint-max REFUSES by ~2470); both margins >> the pin's 1 uu. The sweep code stays the selector (f = 0.50 lands on the band; f = 0.75 lands on the priced tile and is a refused row; f = 0.25 lands under W and is excluded); the four-pillar attempt is recorded in the pin's comment as measured-empty, not run. Why not a wall-bent corner: every obstacle corner's chord is blocked; why not the slope: the ray does not charge slope (O3 was not adopted), which would make the pin about that asymmetry. Executor writes the scene + re-targets the pin; the orchestrator gates (build + GroundNav) and adjusts posts/tiles from the `[SHORTCUT-O2]` rows if the first geometry misses.

Orchestrator error, recorded so the grid is never assumed again: **tiles are laid from `_OriginXY`, not from the geometry.**
**[S11-D110] AMENDED - the scene on the FOUR-PILLAR params:** `Make_FourPillarParams()` (columns C0 [-2000,-1200) C1 [-1200,-400) C2 [-400,400) C3 [400,1200) C4 [1200,2000); rows R0 [-1600,-800) R1 [-800,0) R2 [0,800) R3 [800,1600)), the pillar-free floor (X +-1800, Y +-1200). Posts W (-1650, 380) / E (1650, 380) in R2; the SEARCH prices C2xR2 (probe (0, 400)) at 100, so A* detours through R1 (deviation 380 south beats 420 north) and the funnel bends at C2xR2's south corners (-400, 0) and (400, 0), pushed by the corner offset to A1 ~(-424, -24) and A2 ~(424, -24) - a REAL bend (A1 is ~404 uu off the W->E line). THE BAND = C2xR1: the detour's second segment A1->A2 runs the whole 800 of it at Y ~ -24, the chord W->A2 enters R1 only at X ~301 and crosses ~101 uu of it, and no waypoint stands on it (A1 in C1xR1, A2 in C3xR1, W in C0xR2). Rays under {C2xR1: 4x}: c1 ~1291 (plain), c2 ~848 + 3x800 = 3248, c3 ~2113 + 3x101 = 2416, plain detour ~2139 -> endpoint-max REFUSES by ~276, ray budget ADMITS by ~2120; the fill (endpoint-priced) sees 2113 vs 2139 and admits. The chord's own probes (f 0.25/0.50 -> C1xR2, f 0.75 -> C2xR2) never land on the band, so the SWEEP GROWS ONE CANDIDATE SOURCE: the midpoints of the two replaced segments (the ground the budget actually sums), deduplicated against the chord probes - the general form of "dear ground the replaced stretch crosses". Everything else in the sweep stays.
