- **[S12-D1]** (maintainer, 2026-09-06, session 12) **THE DEFAULT PROVIDER FLIPS TO GROUNDNAV; RECAST STAYS AS A SELECTABLE PROVIDER FOR A/B; RETIREMENT (Tier C / PHASE_9) IS STRUCK; THE SHADOW MACHINERY STAYS; 7C STREAMING RETURNS TO SCOPE.** Verbatim, on the rulings question: "Because we are continually on our own branches in root + submodules, I say that we switch to GroundNav and do a clean break. No backward compatibility needed. Of course, we continue to improve and deal with the GroundNav bugs as we go." Then, asked to confirm the combined promotion + retirement grant (PHASE_8 §4's gate says an executor may not accept one; recorded here on the maintainer's own authority): "Is it possible to support Recast as well? I only ask because if it's _not_ a lot of work to support it, then I would like to see it supported for A/B testing throughout the development of various games. Especially when it comes to comparing path planning and perf." The orchestrator's assessment, which the maintainer's condition accepts: keeping Recast is the STATUS QUO (the facade already dispatches either provider per world, `CkNavSurface_ProviderTable.cpp:156-187`; the shadow mode already runs both; Tier C was pure deletion work), so the cost is not the keeping but a TAX: every new facade capability carries a Recast branch ([P5-B1]'s is a wrapper of the existing `FCk_Nav_Algorithm::FindPathSync`), the crowd keeps its two dispatch branches (`CkCrowdAgent_HandleRequests_Processor.cpp:273`, `ConstrainToNavmesh.cpp:102-119`), and the suite must be run under BOTH defaults for the alternative to stay green. The shadow machinery IS the A/B instrument (the crossover reports, `shadow_report.py`), so it stays; on the maintainer's words ("delete it ONLY if your answer to supporting both is 'it's not worth it'") it is kept. B8 is granted by this ruling WITHOUT the PIE walks (the maintainer overrode the package's "after §2.1 items 1-4 read clean"); the walks stay owed as verification, not as a gate. Streaming: "We need to support streaming." -> 7C (F2.16, PHASE_7 §5) returns to scope as product work under the GroundNav default (no longer a retirement precondition, since there is no retirement). Test ground under the flip: "Harness stages it at boot" (recommended and chosen) - the autotest harness bakes one GroundNav field over the shared level's origin floor per PIE world before a test's steps run (today only tests composing `FCkAutoTest_GroundNavFixture` stage one; the level ships only the Recast navmesh, `CkAutoTest_GroundNavFixture.as:7-13`).

RULING: (1) `CkNav_ProjectSettings.h:49` -> `ECk_NavSurface_Provider::GroundNav`, nothing else in that file. (2) `UCk_AutoTest_Base` (`Script/Common/CkAutoTest_Base.as`) gains `bool _AutoStageOriginField = true` (overridable by `default`), a private `FCkAutoTest_GroundNavFixture _HarnessOriginField`, and three UFUNCTION steps; `Run_Steps` INSERTS them at index 0..2 of `_Steps` when `_AutoStageOriginField && utils_nav_surface::Get_Provider() == ECk_NavSurface_Provider::GroundNav` (stage -> wait built 3600 -> wait settled 900, the fixture's documented shape), so every PIE test under the GroundNav default has the origin floor to walk on and the volume dies with the runner entity. Why prepend-by-default rather than dedup in the fixture: dedup would let a test that stages its OWN field after standing a deck (the mid-crossing pins) adopt a field baked BEFORE the deck, a silent drift; an explicit opt-out is one line per file and the reader sees it. (5) Cost accepted: one ~50 ms bake + a settle wait per non-opted-out PIE test; the lane durations before/after are recorded beside the gate. Equivalence pin: the direct call uses the same radius the facade derives. - **[S12-D9]** (orchestrator, 2026-09-06, session 12) **P5-B1-F RULED: ONE neutral filter-definition registry; GroundNav compiles a filter into plate tables WITH a refusal; the crowd's strict phase reaches GroundNav.** Research (Sonnet, read-only, file:line in the unit brief `scratchpad/brief_p5b1f_f1f2.txt`): `FCk_NavFilter_Definition` (`CkNavFilterDefinition_DataAsset.h:15-33`: required / excluded area tags + per-tag cost multipliers) is already provider-neutral in shape but its registry is Recast-owned (`nav_surface_recast::Register_FilterDefinition` / `TryGet_FilterDefinition`, `RecastAdapter.cpp:237-306`), registered from `CkCrowd_NavGameplayTags.cpp:69-71` (AvoidStandingCrowds excludes `Nav.Area.Crowd.Agent`) and `CkNavSurface_GameplayTags.cpp` (Customer); the neutral area-POLICY registry (`CkNavSurface_AreaPolicy.h:31-76`, Walkability | Cost) reaches GroundNav only at bake time (plates carry `_AreaPolicyIndex` + interned area tags, `CkGroundNav_Plates.h:~135`); at query time `Get_PlateCostMultiplier` (`CkGroundNav_Query_SurfaceWalk.cpp:122-152`) is a Max of two multipliers keyed by FLAT PLATE, with NO refusal - every plate is traversable at a price, and `Nav.Area.Crowd.Agent` is a Cost policy, so a standing crowd is never blocked on GroundNav. THE FINDING THAT CHANGES PRIORITY: the crowd's OWN GroundNav branch has no strict / permissive distinction (`CkCrowdAgent_HandleRequests_Processor.cpp:288-289` says so verbatim: "nothing on the request for a filter tag to reach"; `_PlanUsesStrictStandingCrowdFilter` pinned false at :295), so `_NoCrowdFreeRouteExisted` (`OnGoalFailed` payload, read from `Get_StrictStandingCrowdPlanFailed` at four broadcast sites) is PERMANENTLY FALSE on GroundNav - `NarrowGap_NoRouteFailsClean`'s contract cannot hold until this lands. RULING, three units: **F1** (this unit, Opus) the registry moves to `ck::nav_surface` (new `CkNavFilterDefinition_Registry.{h,cpp}`, the AreaPolicy `FRegistrar` pattern; the Recast API is DELETED, no shims; Recast's `Get_CompiledQueryFilter` reads the neutral table); **F2** (same unit) GroundNav's query cost model gains `_DeniedPlates` (search skips a denied neighbour; the raycast blocks at its edge; a denied start is Blocked), pinned by a new `Test_GroundNav_DeniedPlates.cpp`; the adapter compiles (definition + overlay) into {Multipliers, Denied} by walking the field's plates ONCE per (field epoch, tag, overlay) behind a bounded cache, and sets them on the raycast and the path query (projection stays unfiltered - GroundNav's projection has no channel); the equivalence pin gains a filtered case. Why a refusal and not a large multiplier: the strict phase's meaning is "no route through standing bodies", and a finite price lets A* cross when nothing else exists, which is exactly the false success that keeps `_StrictStandingCrowdPlanFailed` false. **F3** (next unit, Opus, after F1/F2 gate): `FCk_Request_GroundNavPath_FindPath` carries the filter tag + overlay; the crowd's GroundNav branch stamps `AvoidStandingCrowds` on its strict attempt and sets `_PlanUsesStrictStandingCrowdFilter` from the same `StationaryStrictWanted` logic Recast uses; pin = `NarrowGap_NoRouteFailsClean` green on GroundNav. PathNetwork needs nothing (it already threads tag + overlay through the facade). Unverified (the research's one gap): whether a tag -> plate-set index already exists in `CkGroundNav_Plates` / `PlatePortalGraph` - the executor looks before scanning.

- **F8, filed with a ruling ([S12-D15], orchestrator):** the `Blocked` first plan is a real semantic gap in P5-B1-F: a standing walker's OWN `Nav.Area.Crowd.Agent` disc is painted under it, the strict filter excludes that area, the plate it stands on lands in `_DeniedPlates`, and the search's denied-START rule (`CkGroundNav_PathSearch.cpp:288-293`) refuses with Blocked - Recast never refuses here (its start projection under the filter lands on the nearest ALLOWED poly, so the body simply leaves). RULING: the START plate is never denied - the search begins on it and may only never RE-ENTER a denied plate; the raycast likewise walks off a denied start plate and blocks only on entering one. Flip the two DeniedPlates pins (`DeniedStartPlateIsBlocked` -> the route leaves the denied start and never re-enters it; `RaycastFromADeniedPlateIsBlocked` -> a ray from a denied plate walks off it and blocks at the next denied plate). Sonnet-tier, one file each side + the pins; gate = build + GroundNav + Crowd + the NarrowGap solo. Until it lands, every strict plan issued while the walker's own disc is live pays one refused search and one permissive retry.

- **F9 MECHANISM (VERIFIED):** the two "a body stands on my destination" rules disagree by 70 uu. The geometric GoalOccupied rule (`CkCrowdAgent_BlockDetect_Processor.cpp:60-77`) blocks only when the neighbour is inside `ClosestApproach = 2R - Arrival` (= 84 - 30 = 54 uu) of the goal - the rigorous bound (a body farther than that cannot stop me reaching my arrival disc). The cluster GoalCrowded rule (`Get_AnchorQualifyingDepth`, `:178-183`) uses `GoalRegionRadius = SelfR + NbrR + Arrival + Pad + depth * (SelfR + NbrR)` = 42+42+30+10 = **124 uu at depth 0**, so a settled neighbour on a DISTINCT goal 120 uu away qualifies as "on my destination" the moment I touch it (`:187-189`, contact test) while nearer my goal than I am (`:191-196`). The hold is then unbounded by design (`BlockedRecheck :748-770` re-validates the same blocker forever; `DoFailMove` only for `NoProgress`, `:776`). Pacing-sensitive because it needs 9 in CONTACT with 11 at the moment 9 is still farther from (500,120) than 11 is - the funnel through the gap produces exactly that when 9 trails 11 closely. Blast radius of any fix: the crowd-bunching campaign's shared-goal pack semantics (BunchUp gyms / `BunchUp_SettlesAtSharedGoal`, `Queue_*`) - the ruling is the maintainer's (options in the S13 report): (a) depth-0 region = the geometric rule's bound (`2R - Arrival + Pad` = 64 uu; rings keep `+ depth * 2R` - a shared-goal anchor within its 30 uu arrival disc still qualifies with 34 uu of drift headroom, ring 1 up to 148 uu); (b) bound the crowd-cause hold (rejected: legitimate queue holds are open-ended by design); (c) widen the test's slot spacing (rejected: distinct goals 120 uu apart are Rewind99 queue slots). Recommendation: (a), pinned by a new autotest (two walkers, distinct goals 120 uu apart, the trailing one arrives) + TraverseCalm green solo x2 + the Crowd family delta vs S12-9.

**U3b REVIEW RETURNED (Opus, fresh context) - the runtime half (index every baked actor) is CLEAN (double-destroy unreachable: `Request_DestroyEntity` and `Request_RemoveBodiesForEntity` are both idempotent; the revision consumer compares by inequality so the moved handler's double bump is unobservable; the pin's arithmetic is sound). The EDITOR-HOOK half has six findings, all Editor-world only:** (1) should-fix `:280` - `_HasSwept = true` at the HEAD of the sweep leaves the add handler open for the sweep's whole duration (an actor spawned into a not-yet-visited level is baked twice) -> a separate `_IsSweeping` gate; (2) should-fix `:858-897` - undo/redo broadcasts NONE of the three delegates (`AActor::PostEditUndo` `ActorEditor.cpp:803`; the add/delete/move broadcasts have exactly one call site each), so delete + Ctrl+Z leaves a hole with the field reading CURRENT (`Get_IsBuildCurrent` `CkGroundNavVolume_Utils.cpp:481`) - fails OPEN, not closed as the header claims; (3) should-fix HEADLINE `:913-921` / `:867` / `:897` - admission extracts under `LevelSweep` but `Request_BakeActor` re-extracts under `ExplicitActor`, which skips the mobility test and the component / actor filters (`CkJoltBakeExtraction.cpp:190-201, 1575`): a Static wall + Movable door actor nudged in the editor bakes the door permanently; (4) should-fix `:617-620` vs the moved handler - a swept actor set to NoCollision then moved then re-enabled flips STALE-POSE bodies back into the scene (the moved handler skipped it because it extracted 0 bodies); (5) should-fix `:419` vs `:1005-1030` - a REQUEST-baked entity is never in its level's list, and the moved handler UNLISTS a swept one, so every actor placed from the content browser (`UActorFactory::CreateActor` -> `PostEditMove(true)` -> the moved handler) or nudged leaks its bodies past an editor sub-level unload (`EditorLevelUtils.cpp:1010`); the naive fix trips `DoAdd_BodiesForLevel`'s `Contains` early-out (`:934-935`); (6) out-of-scope `:837` - `DoHandle_LevelAdded` gates on the PIE mode with no world-type split (the [S12-D14] conflation, now visible). Nits: `DoUnfile`'s dead-weak-key lookup can match a different dead key (`FWeakObjectPtr::operator==` equates any two dead pointers, `WeakObjectPtr.h:172-180`) -> a missed removal, never a wrong destroy; runtime `EndPlay` never unfiles, so dead keys accumulate; the pin's `AddedCube` leg is manual-only by accident of spawn order (one `TestEqual` hardens it); `DoUnlist_EntityFromLevel` is hygiene the pin cannot distinguish.

- **[S13-D24] PENDING - THE MAINTAINER'S (reopens [NN-D66]):** how GroundNav denies at sub-plate granularity. Options: (i) plates split at markup boundaries - bake-time: wire the existing `InCellPolicy` (one line, exact for STATIC markup authored before the bake); dynamic: a markup derive would have to re-plate, invalidating `_PlateIndex` / seam stubs / portals / labels = a tile re-bake per disc paint (what D66 forbade); (ii) a sub-plate denial mask consulted at the crossing test - breaks plate convexity for the funnel (rejected by the investigator as "the obvious fix" that is not); (iii) the crowd's strict standing-crowd filter compiles the agent tag as a strong PRICE on GroundNav rather than a denial - but the S12 `_NoCrowdFreeRouteExisted` contract (`NarrowGap_NoRouteFailsClean`) depends on a strict FAILURE. JoltNav ([S13-D3]): no per-region denial at all - stationary bodies are cut out of the polymesh at their own footprint after 5 s (`bCutOutWhenStationary`); the exact analogue is (i) with re-plating. RECOMMENDATION: (i) bake-time now (static markup exact; a one-line wiring + a pin), and for dynamic discs accept per-plate pricing with the strict phase downgraded to price-only on GroundNav fields whose plates are coarser than the disc (a per-field "denial granularity" the compile can read off `Plate` extents vs the record's extents), keeping the strict failure only where the plate IS the disc. Until ruled: the crowd's strict phase over-denies on coarse fields.

**[S14-D1] D23 AMENDED BY THE MAINTAINER: the volume is authored through `ACk_EntitySpawner_UE` + an EntityScript, not a bespoke actor.** The maintainer asked "is it possible to instead go through the EntitySpawner Actor and have an Entity that defines the volume?" - YES, and it is the better shape (it is exactly how `UCk_CrowdAvoidanceVolume_EntityScript` is placed today, `CkCrowd/AvoidanceVolume/CkCrowdAvoidanceVolume_EntityScript.h:29-52`: `_Params` + `_SpawnTransform` UPROPERTYs, `Construct` = `Transform::Add` then `Utils::Add`). DESIGN (recorded, implementation awaits the go word):
- NEW `UCk_GroundNavVolume_EntityScript` (C++, `UCk_GenericEntityScript_UE`, in CkGroundNav): `_Params` (`FCk_Fragment_GroundNavVolume_ParamsData`, ExposeOnSpawn) + `_SpawnTransform`; the spawner injects its actor transform into `_SpawnTransform` by reflection (`CkEntitySpawner_Actor.cpp:374-404`, `DoInjectActorTransform`; the default-name fallback resolves `_SpawnTransform`). `_VolumeBounds` becomes LOCAL to the spawner's translation (an AABB - rotation is ignored and documented); runtime callers (the AS gyms / tests) pass identity, so their world-space bounds are unchanged.
- ONE function `groundnav::Get_PlacedVolumeParams(_Params, _SpawnTransform)` used by BOTH `Construct` and the cook - the "one implementation, two drivers" rule (PHASE_7 §4 item 1) at the params level.
- The cook commandlet (U4, un-STOPped): enumerate `ACk_EntitySpawner_UE` in the loaded map (`TActorIterator`, as `CkEntitySpawnerEditor_Module.cpp:421` does), cast `Get_EntityScript()` to the volume script, read the params through that one function, drive the pure bake API. NO ECS, NO entity - FORK-2 stands. Identity is unchanged: `{level package, _CookKey}` (`CookedFieldLoad.h:39-95`); a spawner whose script has `_CookKey` None is runtime-only, as the params document.
- BONUS the maintainer's shape brings for free: the spawner already rebuilds an EDITOR PREVIEW entity in `UCk_EditorEcsWorld_Subsystem_UE` (ticks in the editor with processor schedulers, `CkEcsEditor_Subsystem.h:43-62`; rebuilt on place / move / undo / reinstance / map open, `CkEntitySpawner_Actor.cpp:245-300`, `CkEntitySpawnerEditor_Module.cpp:409-445`). A placed volume script therefore constructs a volume entity in the editor world, and with `_EditorStaticWorldMode = LiveExtract` the volume processor would BAKE IT IN THE EDITOR - that is U5 (5D row 1, the editor-world field) arriving through the same door. SUB-FORK (the maintainer's; recommend the second): editor bake always on (every placed volume bakes on map open - the FORK-3 cost on large maps), or GATED on `_EditorStaticWorldMode` (Disabled -> the script's `Construct` in an `EWorldType::Editor` world adds the volume with `_AutoBuildOnSetup = Disable`; LiveExtract -> bakes). The setting is already the switch for editor Jolt worlds ([P5-B2]).
- Pins: U4's `Test_GroundNavCook_Driver.cpp` needs a test map carrying ONE placed spawner with the volume script + a `_CookKey` (the F9 pin's `__ExternalActors__` shape); the runner-staged origin field stays runtime-only (`_CookKey` None) so no existing test moves. `CkAutoTest_GroundNav_Cook_CookedFieldMatchesRuntimeBake.as` as designed.
- Blast: NEW files + one commandlet + one `Build.cs` dep (the script lives in CkGroundNav; only the EDITOR commandlet casts spawners, so `CkGroundNavEditor.Build.cs` gains `CkEntitySpawner`; CkGroundNav itself gains nothing). 7C chunk identity = the spawner's `Get_LevelPlacedIdentity` (`CkEntitySpawner_Actor.cpp:420`) - stable across boots by construction.

**[S14-D2] D24 THE PROPOSED SOLUTION (two halves, PENDING the go word):**
- **Static half (bake time, exact):** build a per-cell policy view for the tile from the STATIC markup records covering it (one int per cell: the interned policy index of the records covering that cell, 0 = none - layer-major exactly as `_CellToPlate`) and pass it as `InCellPolicy` to `DoDecompose_Plates` at `CkGroundNav_TileBake.cpp:325` (the documented third merge criterion, `CkGroundNav_Plates.h:217-236`, today with NO caller). Plates then split on markup boundaries and `Stamp_PlateCostPolicies` (`Plates.cpp:352`) stamps disc-sized plates. One pin: a disc in a tile -> the plate under it is the disc's cell rectangle, its neighbours untagged. Cost: more plates only where static markup is.
- **Dynamic half (crowd discs): STOP DENYING on GroundNav - price + geometric verdict, the [S13-D25] rule generalised to the strict phase.** The crowd's strict phase on GroundNav queries under the BASE (priced) filter; the strict VERDICT is computed on the answer: the route is crowd-free iff no segment passes within (agent radius + disc half-extent) of a CONFIRMED disc; when one does, `Try_BuildStationaryMarkupDetour` (`PathRefresh_Processor.h:136-148`, exists since D25) splices around it; only when the splice fails is the strict phase FAILED (`_StrictStandingCrowdPlanFailed` -> permissive retry -> `_NoCrowdFreeRouteExisted`, `OnPathResolved.cpp:140-175` unchanged). `NarrowGap_NoRouteFailsClean` keeps its contract (a gap fully blocked by bodies has no geometric detour -> strict fails); `StationaryAgentReGrounds`' strict `Unreachable`s and SteeringPerf's `Unreachable expansions [1]` disappear (no plate is ever denied by a disc). Recast keeps its per-polygon exclude (exact already). The `AvoidStandingCrowds` FILTER and its pin stay (denial still exists for static excluded tags); the crowd stops SELECTING it for `Nav.Area.Crowd.Agent` on GroundNav: `Get_PlanPhaseFilter` (`HandleRequests.cpp:50-82`) stamps the base filter + "strict verdict wanted" for the GroundNav branch, the install seam (`OnGroundNavPathResolved`) computes the verdict and feeds `StrictFailedOnRoute`. Files: `HandleRequests.{h,cpp}`, `OnGroundNavPathResolved.cpp`, `OnPathResolved.cpp` (verdict plumbing), a pin `Crowd_StrictPhase_PricedRouteThreadsBetweenDiscs` (two discs 300 uu apart on one 500 uu plate; the strict phase SUCCEEDS between them).
- REJECTED: re-plating a tile per disc confirmation (the dtTileCache / JoltNav `bCutOutWhenStationary` analogue): every confirmation invalidates plate ids, portals, labels and every corridor in the tile - F10's churn multiplied - and [NN-D66] forbade it for the same reason.

**[S14-D3] F10 ROOT CAUSE VERIFIED FROM CODE + THE PROPOSED SOLUTION (PENDING the go word):** a crowd disc paint / unpaint is a markup COST derive (`FProcessor_GroundNavVolume_MarkupCostDerive`, `CkGroundNavVolume_Processor.cpp:1956-2075`) that PUBLISHES AS IF GROUND MOVED: `world_fields::Publish` with no link-only ids (so `DoApply_Publish` advances `_LastGeometryEpoch`, `WorldFieldRegistry.cpp:92-101`) and `Request_NotifySurfaceRebuilt(ChangedBounds)` with ChangedBounds = the union of the changed TILES (`Get_ChangedTileBounds`, :2031-2048). The invalidator (`Invalidate_Processor.cpp:118-140`) then flags every walking corridor intersecting those tiles, and PathRefresh answers each with `[REBUILD-REPLAN] mode=Repair` (`PathRefresh.cpp:980-1016`). 240 agents crossing one 4-tile field, each painting after 1.5 s under 84 uu/s (`CkCrowd_ProjectSettings.h:342-347`) -> every disc flip re-plans EVERY agent in the tile -> they move -> discs flip -> repeat (~240/s); the `EpisodeIsLive` ensures (`PathPendingWatchdog:48-50`) are the side effect. TWO consumers react to one event: GroundNav's invalidator (tile-granular, price-blind) and CkCrowd's own settled-disc pass (`_PathRefreshMarkupSettleSeconds` 0.5 s, geometric, on-route only, with the detour / escape helpers) - the second is the designed one. JoltNav ([S13-D3]): its follower re-paths on OFF-PATH DISTANCE only (`Jolt2DNavigationComponent.h:99`), never on nav-data changes; obstacles cut out after 5 s (`JoltObstacleComponent.cpp:48-49`).
- **SOLUTION: a cost-only derive is not a rebuild.** The derive publishes with a new publish kind (`CostOnly`: `DoApply_Publish` does NOT advance `_LastGeometryEpoch` and records nothing in `_ChangedLinkIdsSinceGeometry`), and the invalidator, seeing a note whose geometry epoch the corridor postdates and no changed links, LEAVES WALKING CORRIDORS ALONE (a corridor priced differently is still walkable; its quality under the new price is CkCrowd's settled-disc pass's job). The D17 in-flight arm KEEPS firing for cost-only publishes (a plan dispatched before the price existed is pricing-blind - that is F1's liveness; `AvoidanceVolume_InitialPathAvoidsExpandedObb` stays green). Walkability derives (links, doors, geometry) publish exactly as today. Files: `WorldFieldRegistry.{h,cpp}` (publish kind), `CkGroundNavVolume_Processor.cpp:2060-2070` (the derive's call), `Invalidate_Processor.cpp` (the note branch), one pin (`PathInvalidation`: a cost-only publish over a walking corridor raises no `RepathRequired`; an in-flight search still arms). Orthogonal TUNING the maintainer may also want: `_StationaryMarkupDelaySeconds` 1.5 -> JoltNav's 5 s (a jammed crossing paints far fewer discs); a number, not a mechanism.
- Expected: SteeringPerf's `[REBUILD-REPLAN]` count drops from ~10,700 / 45 s to the crowd's own settled-disc detours; `EpisodeIsLive` x61 -> 0; the benchmark's perf assertions can finally be read.

**NEXT (on the go word, in this order - each is one Opus unit + a gate):** (1) F10 [S14-D3] (smallest; unblocks SteeringPerf's read); (2) D24 both halves [S14-D2] (unblocks StationaryAgentReGrounds, likely PathNetworkStationaryDetour / F6-d); (3) D23 [S14-D1] the volume script + U4 the cook (+ U5 via the editor preview, gated); then 7C. The maintainer's sub-fork under [S14-D1] (editor bake gated on `LiveExtract` - recommended) rides with (3).

**S14 GO WORD 2026-09-07 ~17:50 PDT ("approved, deliver 4, 3 and 2"): three Opus units DISPATCHED IN PARALLEL on disjoint files - F10 (`brief_s14_f10.txt`: WorldFieldRegistry publish kind + the derive's call + Invalidate + 2 PathInvalidation rows), D24 (`brief_s14_d24.txt`: TileBake cell policy + `Test_GroundNav_PlateSplitAtMarkup.cpp`; the crowd's GroundNav strict phase -> priced + geometric verdict + detour splice at the install seam + `CkAutoTest_Crowd_StrictPhase_PricedRouteThreadsBetweenDiscs.as`), D23+U4 (`brief_s14_d23_u4.txt`: `UCk_GroundNavVolume_EntityScript` + `Get_PlacedVolumeParams` + the cook commandlet enumerating spawners + `Test_GroundNavCook_Driver.cpp` byte-identity DryRun + `CkGroundNavEditor/Claude.md`). **Decision I made rather than asking ([S14-D1] sub-fork): the editor preview bake is GATED on `_EditorStaticWorldMode = LiveExtract` (Disabled -> the placed volume exists with `_AutoBuildOnSetup = Disable`)** - say if you want always-on. **Operational persistence limit:** commandlet preflight rejects invalid or incomplete input before any write, and manifests are saved only after their referenced field/tile packages exist. A filesystem or asset-save failure during the sequential write phase can still leave orphan generated assets from that attempt. The supported recovery is a fresh commandlet recook after correcting the I/O failure; no rollback transaction or automatic deletion is claimed.

**Gate118 planning — showcase, matched A/B, and final acceptance (2026-09-10):** the CTO selected the visual gyms and a real GroundNav-versus-Recast stress comparison as the next work. Six GroundNav gyms already exist (`Tuning Range`, `Walk`, `Links`, `Dynamic Obstacle`, `Markup`, and `GroundNav vs Recast`) plus the separate six-scenario PathNetwork gym. They are functional engineering gyms, but no fresh curated GroundNav capture set proves JoltNav-reference presentation quality. The existing parity gym is behavioral only; `Crowd_SteeringPerf` measures 240 agents without pinning or labeling the provider, and `NavSurface_RecastBudgets_PathThroughput` measures only Recast. Therefore no valid matched performance A/B exists yet.

The closeout order is now fixed: **(1)** capture the current visual baseline, apply a shared presentation pass, preserve each gym's behavior/verdict contract, and obtain human sign-off on fresh 16:9 hero plus diagnostic captures; **(2)** implement one shared authored benchmark fixture with both valid Recast data and a settled GroundNav field, provider-labeled `QueryBurst128` and `CrowdConvergence240` modes, behavior eligibility, structured raw output, and at least three alternating matched pairs on the same Development-game artifact; **(3)** complete PathNetwork snap plus undo/redo, the six GroundNav and PathNetwork walkthroughs, and LiveExtract cost observation; **(4)** perform authored World Partition/data-layer, real commandlet-write, real-map streaming/pathing, and build-machine Development/Test/Shipping acceptance; **(5)** integrate into BusterBlock last through an explicit Recast-first migration.

The BusterBlock step is a migration gate, not a pointer-only update. Its current `DefaultCkFoundation.ini` does not select Recast explicitly, while the new framework default is GroundNav; unauthored BusterBlock worlds would therefore select a provider with no published field. Its eight `Nav.Filter.*` settings still reference `UNavigationQueryFilter` classes, while the new neutral registry expects filter-definition assets, and its sidewalk vectorizer still directly queries `ARecastNavMesh`. Before the plugin update reaches gameplay, pin BusterBlock to Recast, migrate or bridge all eight filters, build and run the Recast baseline, introduce GroundNav on one isolated authored map, complete cook/package and matched A/B evidence, and retain a tested Recast rollback switch. Do not remove Recast in this campaign.

The operator runbook is `docs/campaigns/navigation-next/GROUNDNAV_ACCEPTANCE_GUIDE.html`. The zero-context executor prompt is `docs/campaigns/navigation-next/CONTINUATION_PROMPT_GroundNavShowcaseBenchmarkAndAcceptance.md`. No new implementation, benchmark result, human visual acceptance, real cook, packaged build, or BusterBlock integration is claimed by Gate118 planning.
